BDD harness — cluster findings

30 agent reports across 5 clusters. Every report is rendered whole: recommendations are only judgeable next to the code they are about.

Clusters
  • Identical-body collapses (52 groups) 6 agent reports
  • Shadowed sentences (11 pairs) 14 agent reports
  • The When primitive (2649 lines) 4 agent reports
  • Seeding duplication (creative + pricing) 4 agent reports
  • Seeding: intent + transport independence 2 agent reports

Identical-body collapses (52 groups)

Reproductionagent 1 of 6

Reproduction

Scan: ast.parse every tests/bdd/steps/**/*.py; for each function carrying a @given/@when/@then decorator, strip the docstring and join ast.dump(stmt, include_attributes=False) over the remaining body; group by that string; keep groups with ≥2 definitions and ≥2 distinct sentences. Total: 52 groups (matches the brief). Sorted by (ndefs desc, first sentence alphabetically), index % 6 == 5 gives my shard: 5, 11, 17, 23, 29, 35, 41, 47.

Feature-line counts below include Examples-table cells (several of these sentences are only ever reached as a <placeholder> substitution, so a step-line-only count reports 0 and is wrong).

FINDING, not a collapse. These two sentences mean genuinely different things and both happen to be true of the same object, which is why the bodies match. Line 199 grades BR-RULE-049 INV-4 edge — *"Formats without dimensions excluded by dimension filter"* — so the absence of renders is the whole point of that Given; lines 228/237 grade name_search and say nothing about renders. The bodies coincide only because FormatFactory (tests/factories/format.py) declares just format_id, name, is_standard, so no-renders is an undeclared factory default, not a declared state — the same defect the UC-011 F19 note calls out.

Collapsing to named "{name}" deletes the only declaration line 199's scenario depends on. Collapsing to with no render dimensions puts an irrelevant claim into two name-search scenarios. The bug is the body: given_registry_format_no_dimensions should declare the state it names — build with renders=None explicitly (and assert it) — after which the bodies differ and the group disappears on its own. Note the module already has four sibling steps (with renders:, with render width … height …, with responsive render dimensions, with non-responsive render dimensions, given_config.py:121-162) that all pass renders= explicitly; this one is the odd member that leans on the default.

Method: ast.parse every tests/bdd/steps/**/*.py, take each function carrying a given/when/then/step decorator, drop the docstring, key on "\n".join(ast.dump(n, include_attributes=False) for n in body); keep groups with ≥2 function definitions and ≥2 distinct Gherkin sentences. Sorted by (defs desc, first sentence alphabetically), 0-based index. My shard = indices 1, 7, 13, 19, 25, 31, 37, 43, 49 — 9 groups.

Reach counts below are expanded steps / source lines: Scenario Outlines are expanded against their Examples tables, because several of these sentences are reached only as Examples cells (Given <auth_state>). A literal grep undercounts them to zero and would have inverted two of my recommendations.

GROUP 19 — nothing persisted — FINDING, NOT A COLLAPSE

Shared body:

    _assert_no_new_media_buy(ctx)
sentencemodule::functionreach
no database records should be createdtests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py::then_no_db_records_created (L1145)2 / 2
no new media buy should have been createdtests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py::then_no_new_media_buy (L1156)2 / 2
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:915:    And no database records should be created
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:928:    And no database records should be created
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:2106:    And no new media buy should have been created

FLAGGED AS A FINDING. The two sentences make different claims and the body cannot tell them apart, so the body is the bug.

no new media buy should have been created is used by the idempotency scenarios (IDEMPOTENCY_IN_FLIGHT, IDEMPOTENCY_EXPIRED) where only the media buy matters. no database records should be created is used by BR-RULE-020 INV-4 and INV-5 — the pre-adapter-validation and dry-run scenarios whose stated obligation is "failure persists nothing" / "validates fully but never calls the adapter or persists". That claim covers packages, creative assignments, workflow rows — every write, not one row.

The shared helper (tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:1297) only ever looks for a media_buy_id, and worse, short-circuits green on the mere presence of an error:

    # Strategy 2: operation should have errored (no response = nothing created)
    error = ctx.get("error")
    if error is not None:
        # Error means the operation failed before creating anything
        return

"An error was raised, therefore nothing was written" is the exact inference a rollback test exists to refute. INV-4 is an error path, so it takes that branch and never touches the database at all. Collapsing the sentences would freeze the broad claim onto the narrow (and partly vacuous) check. Fix the body first: no database records should be created needs a real all-tables delta against the harness DB; only then decide whether one sentence still covers both.

RECOMMENDATION: the response contains error code {code}. "error variant" is a schema-union noun a buyer would never say, and the domain copy is a one-line duplicate of the generic step that already grades 23 lines across five feature files.

Two things to carry over when this collapses. Adopting the generic [A-Z_0-9]+ anchor closes that. Second, the two feature files using "error variant" also use the generic wording elsewhere in the same file (BR-UC-011:104), so the collapse removes an inconsistency inside a single document.

    Reproduction

    Method: ast.dump(Module(body=stmts)) (attributes excluded, leading docstring stripped) over every @given/@when/@then-decorated function under tests/bdd/steps/ (skipping _-prefixed modules, matching the existing guard tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_no_duplicate_steps.py). Groups = 2+ functions sharing a normalized body AND reached by 2+ distinct sentences. Result: 52 groups — the brief's number reproduces exactly (identical with _-modules included; they contribute nothing new). All 52 have exactly 2 definitions, so the sort collapses to alphabetical on the group's alphabetically-first sentence. Shard idx % 6 == 3 → indices 3, 9, 15, 21, 27, 33, 39, 45, 51.

    Feature-line counts below are *exclusive* reach: lines the pattern matches where no other registered step of the same keyword also matches (pytest-bdd parsers.re uses fullmatch; {x:d} restricted to digits). Contested lines are called out explicitly.

    Sentences

    sentencemodule::functionfeature lines
    the request revision is set to {revision:d}tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc003_update_media_buy.py::given_request_revision4 source lines / 10 rendered rows
    the request revision is set to "{revision}"tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc003_update_media_buy.py::given_request_revision_wrong_type1 rendered row (row L2273)

    Verbatim feature lines

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-003-update-media-buy.feature:2256:     And the request revision is set to <value>
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-003-update-media-buy.feature:2271:       | stale_revision | 5     | error "CONFLICT" with suggestion   |
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-003-update-media-buy.feature:2273:       | wrong_type     | "7"   | error "INVALID_REQUEST" with suggestion |

    FLAGGED — this group is not a collapse, and a mechanical pass over the 52 would silently delete a type-boundary test. The two sentences reach the *same feature line* (2256); only the Examples value differs (7 vs "7"). The discriminator is not in the body at all, it is in the decorator: {revision:d} coerces to int, "{revision}" yields str, and the whole point of Examples row 2273 is that 7 and "7" must NOT behave alike. Collapsing to one sentence forces one parser, which either breaks the matches_current row or turns the wrong_type row into a re-run of it — a production regression that accepts a string revision would then read green.

    The wording also cannot be improved to make this legible: all six rows share step line 2256, so the string row has no place to say "as a string" without changing the other five. Keep both definitions exactly as they are; given_request_revision_wrong_type's docstring already records this and is the reason to leave it alone.

    General implication for the other 51 groups: byte-identical bodies are proof of interchangeability only when the decorators are also equivalent. Anything in this set whose two sentences differ by a :d, a :f, or a quote character needs the decorator compared before the collapse is called proven.

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOESagent 1 of 14

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOES

    Both registrations are still live and still exactly two. AST scan over tests/**/*.py (walking @given/@when/@then/@step calls including parsers.parse/cfparse/re forms) returns:

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2210  @then  def then_real_validation_error
tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:814              @then  def then_real_validation_error
2 registrations

    Generic — tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:813. Two assertions, one conditional and one not:

    if wire_error_envelope_or_none(ctx) is not None:
    ctx["result"].assert_wire_error("VALIDATION_ERROR")

error = ctx.get("error")
assert error is not None, "Expected an error"
assert isinstance(error, ValidationError), (...)
assert error.errors(), "Expected ValidationError with field-level error details"

    Reads the wire envelope (conditionally) and hard-codes VALIDATION_ERROR; then reads ctx["error"] and requires it to be a pydantic.ValidationError object with non-empty .errors(). The docstring is explicit that the type half is unfalsifiable from the wire: *"both a raw pydantic.ValidationError and an AdCPValidationError collapse to the same VALIDATION_ERROR wire code, so nothing on the wire distinguishes 'real' from 'wrapped/simulated'."*

    Domain — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2209. One assertion:

    result = ctx.get("result")
if result is not None and result.wire_error_envelope is not None:
    return
raise AssertionError("Expected a real wire REJECTION carrying VALIDATION_ERROR, but no wire error envelope was captured. ...")

    Reads only result.wire_error_envelope. Asserts an error envelope exists — no code, no field, no structure. The docstring says so: *"It asserts NO code."*

    Correction to the brief: this pair is not a coin-flip. uc019_query_media_buys is absent from conftest's pytest_plugins list (tests/bdd/conftest.py:59-86); it is imported at module scope by tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:16 precisely so its redefinitions stay scoped. Test-module namespace beats plugin namespace deterministically. The arrangement is pinned by tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py, whose INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES frozenset names this exact sentence. So resolution is deterministic: UC-019 always gets the domain body, every other UC always gets the generic one. The defect is not nondeterminism — it is a silent semantic fork, one sentence carrying two different obligations depending on which feature file it appears in, and no reader of a feature file can tell which.

    2. WHY THEY DIVERGED

    Generic came first: 7f0d45a4e feat: introduce BDD behavioral test suite (#1146). Domain second: 61f6ad9a2 feat: behavioral test foundation ... 10 use cases, 4-transport coverage (#1335).

    The divergence is documented in the domain body itself and is a real complaint, not carelessness:

    > *"It used to assert VALIDATION_ERROR, which made it a second, hidden code claim that could contradict the one its own scenario states: the sandbox scenario sends an out-of-enum status_filter, which is INVALID_REQUEST under the pinned split ... and this step failed it while the scenario's own the error code should be "..." step passed."*

    So UC-019 forked because the generic body smuggles a code claim that the sentence does not contain. UC-019's fix was to strip the body down to almost nothing rather than fix the sentence.

    3. WHAT THE SENTENCE SHOULD ASSERT

    It cannot be made observable as written, and the repo has already proved that and already fixed it once.

    "Real, not simulated" is a claim about the provenance of an exception object, not about anything the buyer received. Both halves fail the observability test in different directions:

  • - The generic body's isinstance(error, ValidationError) grades the harness. Worse, on the standard dispatch path ctx["error"] is never a pydantic error at all — tests/bdd/steps/generic/_dispatch.py:98 writes ctx["error"] = cast("WireError", result.error), and the WireCtx TypedDict types that slot as WireError specifically so ctx["error"] = AdCPValidationError(...) is a mypy error. The only way to land a pydantic.ValidationError in that key is the blanket-except at _dispatch.py:201, which fires when env.call_via itself blew up — i.e. the request never reached the seller. The assertion passes hardest in exactly the case that most disproves "the seller validated the input."
  • - The domain body asserts "an envelope exists", which in its own scenario is a strict subset of the line above it. the error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST" (then_error.py:504) already refuses without a wire envelope: *"Expected wire error code ..., but no wire envelope was captured."* The domain body is a tautology.

    • The underlying property BR-RULE-209 INV-7 wants ("sandbox validation errors are real") is observable, and the observable form already exists in this repo. tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:1401 used to carry this sentence and no longer does. It was replaced by:

    And the response error issues include keyword enum for field billing

    bound at then_error.py:362, whose docstring is the argument in full:

    > *"An issues[] entry carrying the JSON-Schema keyword that failed and a pointer at the offending member IS production's validation output. It is also STRICTER than the type check it replaces: a sandbox-simulated or hand-wrapped error would not carry that structure, whereas any ValidationError — including one synthesised in the test process — satisfied an isinstance check."*

    That is the restatement. "Real validation ran" becomes "the rejection names the schema rule the value broke and points at the member that broke it" — checkable from the wire envelope alone, and unsatisfiable by a canned sandbox response.

    4. WHICH SCENARIOS USE IT — AND WHAT THEY MEASURE

    11 scenarios carry the step line. (UC-002:2731 and UC-003:2690 are outline-table *cell text*, different wording, not step bindings.) Measured against the last in-network run, test-results/innet_060926_1901/bdd_inprocess.json:

    FeatureBound to a test module?Body reachedOutcome
    UC-002 create_media_buyyesgenericxfailed (a2a)
    UC-004 deliveryyesgenericxfailed (a2a)
    UC-005 creative formatsyesgenericxfailed (a2a)

    The generic body has never graded a passing scenario. Every scenario that reaches it is xfailed; five more sit in feature files no test module binds (grep -rl "<feature>.feature" tests/bdd/*.py returns nothing for 001, 015, 016, 020, 023). The only live executions of this sentence run the domain body, whose assertion its own scenario already makes on the preceding line. Net grading contribution of this sentence across the suite today: zero.

    Does the proposed meaning hold for all 11? No — and that is the second finding. The issues[]/keyword restatement fits the schema-violation scenarios (002 budget 0, 005 min_width=-1, 016 invalid member identifiers, 018 out-of-enum status, 019 out-of-enum status_filter, 023 invalid catalog type). It does not fit UC-004 ("queries delivery metrics for a non-existent media buy") or UC-020 ("invalid target_format_id"): those are lookup failures, not schema violations, and no JSON-Schema keyword failed. Those two scenarios were never about validation-is-real; they were about not-found, and they inherited the sentence by copy-paste along with the identical # BR-RULE-209 INV-7 footer. The sentence was doing two jobs, and one of them was fictitious.

    5. RECOMMENDATION

    Delete the sentence. Delete both bodies. Do not write a third.

    There is no body to write, because the observable content already has a step and the redundant content already has a step. Concretely:

    1. Remove @then("the error should be a real validation error, not simulated") and then_real_validation_error from tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:813-846, and from tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2209-2237.

    2. Drop the sentence from INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES in tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py:44 — that guard will demand the decision, which is what it is for.

    3. In the six schema-violation scenarios, replace the line with the UC-011 form, naming the field the scenario actually made invalid, e.g. for UC-019:

       And the response error issues include keyword enum for field status_filter

    and for UC-005 keyword minimum for field min_width, UC-002 keyword exclusiveMinimum for field total_budget, etc.

    4. In UC-004 and UC-020, delete the line and the # BR-RULE-209 INV-7 footer with it. Those scenarios grade a not-found, and the footer is a mislabel — INV-7 is not what they exercise.

    5. UC-001, UC-015, UC-016, UC-020, UC-023 need the same edit, but note they are dead Gherkin until something calls scenarios() on them. That is a separate finding worth its own ticket: five feature files with @storyboard-shaped content that no test module collects.

    Steps 3–5 are scenario edits, which is the right level: this was never a step problem. Two people wrote two bodies because "real, not simulated" is a sentence about the test harness, and a step can only grade what the buyer received.

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOESagent 2 of 14

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOES

    Both registrations are live and at the lines given.

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_success.py:119-131 — reads the typed payload, serializes it, checks key absence:

    resp = require_payload(ctx)
dumped = resp.model_dump()
assert "sandbox" not in dumped, (...)

    require_payload returns TransportResult.payload (the Python model), *not* the wire. model_dump() is AdCPBaseModel's override, which defaults exclude_none=True (src/core/schemas/_base.py:1390). So this is a serializer round-trip on a harness-side object, one step removed from what the buyer received.

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2193-2206 — reads the Python attribute on the same typed payload:

    resp = require_payload(ctx)
sandbox = getattr(resp, "sandbox", None)
assert sandbox is None, (...)

    No serialization at all. sandbox is None — so sandbox=False fails here too.

    Measured divergence: none reachable today. Against the pinned models the verdicts are identical on every state:

    statewire (to_wire)generic verdictuc019 verdict
    unsetkey absentpasspass
    Falsekey presentfailfail
    Truekey presentfailfail

    They differ only in what they read, and that will bite: a response class that puts back exclude_none-dropped fields (_base.py:528), or a "sandbox": null on the wire, passes both — because neither one looks at the wire.

    Correction to the brief: this pair is *not* a registration-order coin-flip. tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:10-14 star-imports the domain module at test-module scope precisely so the override is deterministic and UC-019-scoped, and tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py:50 pins this sentence in INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES. The shadowing is deliberate, documented, and deterministic. The defect is that the same sentence means two different things in two different features — which is still the disease the brief describes, just not by the mechanism it names.

    2. WHY THEY DIVERGED

    Generic came first: 7f0d45a4e (2026-03-19, #1146). UC-019's copy landed ten weeks later in 61f6ad9a2 (2026-05-26, #1335), the "10 use cases, 4-transport coverage" bulk import. The override docstring's justification — "UC-019 answers these sentences itself because its responses carry sandbox/validation shapes the generic steps do not model" — is not true of this sentence: GetMediaBuysResponse carries the same optional sandbox: bool | None = None every other v3.1 response carries, and the two bodies reach the same verdict on all three of its states. This is a bulk import writing its own copy of a sentence it did not check for, and an allowlist row later ratifying it.

    Worse, the copy is a vacuous grader that the repo has already documented as vacuous. tests/bdd/conftest.py:644 says of the sibling scenario: *"sandbox-production passes vacuously (sandbox absent from response by default)."* Production sets sandbox nowhere; both bodies are unconditionally green.

    3. WHAT THE SENTENCE SHOULD ASSERT

    The sentence *is* observable — it needs no scenario rewrite, only the right read.

    Grounding (pinned AdCP 3.1.1, adcp==6.6.0): sandbox is {"type": "boolean", "description": "When true, this response contains simulated data from sandbox mode."} — no default, optional — and it is declared only on the success branch. media-buy/get-media-buys-response.json /properties/sandbox; creative/sync-creatives-response.json oneOf/0 has it while oneOf/1 (errors) and oneOf/2 (submitted/task) do not. An unset optional has no null spelling in these schemas.

    So the observable obligation is:

    > The success wire the buyer received carries no sandbox key at all — not the key with a JSON null, which is a schema-invalid emission of an unset optional.

    That is exactly wire_absent(ctx, "sandbox"), whose docstring (_outcome_helpers.py:240-249) already states the absent-vs-null rule this sentence needs, and which 30+ steps across uc003/uc010/uc011/then_media_buy already use for the same shape of oracle. Neither current body uses it.

    This is behavior-preserving and strictly stronger. Measured through the real seam:

    to_wire(GetMediaBuysResponse(media_buys=[]))        -> keys ['media_buys','replayed','status']  # sandbox absent
to_wire(GetMediaBuysResponse(media_buys=[], sandbox=False)) -> 'sandbox' in wire: True

    (src/core/tools/_wire.py:34 — response.model_dump(mode="json"), base exclude_none=True.) Everything green today stays green; a "sandbox": null — which both current bodies accept — goes red.

    4. WHICH SCENARIOS USE IT

    18 uses across 11 features. Not one meaning — two.

    Job A — BR-RULE-209 INV-5, "production account ⇒ no sandbox on the success wire" (16 uses). @T-UC-001/002/004/005/006/015/016/018/019/020/023-sandbox-production, plus the sandbox absent in response (production account) row of the UC-006/015/018/019/020 boundary outlines. The proposed meaning holds for all 16 unchanged.

    **Job B — BR-RULE-209 INV-11, "*sandbox* account, non-success branch ⇒ still no sandbox" (2 uses). @T-UC-006-sandbox-submitted-no-flag (line 1455) and @T-UC-006-sandbox-errors-no-flag (line 1469), plus their two outline rows at 1495-1496. The account is a sandbox** account in these — INV-5 does not apply. The obligation is the schema branch: oneOf/1 and oneOf/2 declare no sandbox property.

    The submitted case is fine — tests/harness/_base.py:907 puts the submitted envelope on wire_response, so wire_absent reaches it. The errors case is not. _wire_body raises "expected a success response, got error" on an error dispatch, and require_payload raises "the dispatch produced no payload — it errored instead" — so the *current generic body cannot serve it either*. It survives only because its Given (a sync request that fails operation-level validation) is registered nowhere (AST scan: zero hits), so the scenario auto-xfails as a missing step definition. Job B is dormant, not passing.

    So yes: the sentence is doing two jobs, and one of the two is structurally unservable by any single body.

    5. RECOMMENDATION

    Two sentences, plus delete the UC-019 copy. Do not apply — decision only.

    (a) Keep one canonical registration in then_success.py, reading the wire:

    @then("the response should not include a sandbox field")
def then_no_sandbox_field(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """The buyer's success wire carries no ``sandbox`` key at all.

    AdCP 3.1.1 declares ``sandbox`` (boolean, no default, "When true, this
    response contains simulated data from sandbox mode.") only on the SUCCESS
    branch — get-media-buys-response.json /properties/sandbox;
    sync-creatives-response.json oneOf/0. An unset optional boolean is ABSENT
    on a conformant wire, so a serialized ``"sandbox": null`` is a
    schema-invalid emission rather than an absence — the distinction
    ``wire_absent`` exists to keep, and the one a model_dump() or getattr read
    cannot see. BR-RULE-209 INV-5.
    """
    wire_absent(ctx, "sandbox")

    (b) Give Job B its own sentence, and rewrite the two UC-006 error-branch Then lines to use it:

    @then("the error envelope should not include a sandbox field")
def then_no_sandbox_field_on_error(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """A terminal-failure envelope carries no ``sandbox`` key, sandbox account or not.

    sync-creatives-response.json oneOf/1 (errors) declares no sandbox property,
    so a sandbox ACCOUNT still must not see the flag on the failure shape
    (BR-RULE-209 INV-11). The success-path readers cannot serve this at all:
    _wire_body raises on an error dispatch.
    """
    envelope = wire_error_dict(ctx)
    assert "sandbox" not in envelope, (
        f"error envelope carries sandbox={envelope['sandbox']!r}; "
        "the errors branch declares no sandbox property"
    )

    @T-UC-006-sandbox-submitted-no-flag and the submitted outline row keep sentence (a) — the submitted envelope rides the success wire.

    (c) Delete uc019_query_media_buys.py:2193-2206 and drop "the response should not include a sandbox field" from INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES (tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py:50). It grades nothing the generic step does not, and its stated justification does not survive checking.

    Verify before landing: run tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py and tests/bdd/test_uc006_sync_creatives.py serial on all in-process transports. to_wire measurement says the switch is green-for-green; if MCP structured_content turns out to carry "sandbox": null, the new red is a real schema-invalid-emission finding, not a test regression, and should be filed rather than worked around.

    Two adjacent items this surfaced, out of scope here:

  • - "the response should include sandbox equals true" is duplicated in exactly the same two modules (then_success.py:112, uc019:2181), both getattr-based, both vacuous, same allowlist row. Same treatment: assert wire_field(ctx, "sandbox") is True.
  • - Three sibling sentences are registered nowhere (AST scan): the response should include sandbox equals false (UC-019:1577), the response sandbox field should be false or absent (UC-015:1013), the response sandbox field, if present, should be false (UC-006:1493). Those outline rows are silently dormant. Three spellings of one tri-state oracle, which is what wire_lookup is documented for.

    0. Correction to the brief's premise: this pair is not a live coin-flip

    Measured, not inferred. tests/bdd/conftest.py:57-87 lists pytest_plugins; tests.bdd.steps.generic.then_media_buy is not in it, and the only import * in tests/bdd/ is tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:14 (uc019, not then_media_buy). So the generic module is never loaded as a plugin and its step fixtures are never collected. The repo already knows this — tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_step_module_reachability.py:47-56 allowlists it under reason (A) dead-pending-harness, and tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_success.py:17-19 says so in prose:

    > then_media_buy.py is a helper module that is not registered as a pytest-bdd plugin (its other steps duplicate domain-file steps).

    Both guards pass at HEAD (3 passed in 4.85s), so the allowlist entry is not stale. The live body is uc002's, always. The AST scan found exactly two registrations of the literal (plus two longer UC-026 sentences that are different steps).

    The two bodies are also near-identical, which is why the AST-normalization measurement misfired: the only difference is a dead assignment target.

    1. What each body actually does

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_media_buy.py:18-22 — then_response_succeeds (DEAD, never collected)

    assert "error" not in ctx, f"Expected success but got error: {ctx.get('error')}"
resp = require_payload(ctx)

    Reads two harness ctx keys. require_payload (_outcome_helpers.py:392) returns ctx["result"].payload — the reconstructed typed model — or falls back to ctx["self_dispatched_response"]. resp is assigned and never used (copy-paste from the sibling then_pricing_validation_passes at line 27, which does use it). Asserts nothing about the wire.

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1635-1638 — then_response_should_succeed (LIVE)

    assert "error" not in ctx, f"Expected success but got error: {ctx.get('error')}"
require_payload(ctx)  # raises with a diagnostic if the dispatch produced none

    Byte-for-byte the same two reads, minus the dead binding. Same sources: ctx["error"] membership and TransportResult.payload.

    Neither reads wire_response, wire_error_envelope, or a DB row.

    2. Why they diverged

    They did not diverge behaviorally — this is duplication, not disagreement. Order: uc002's came first, 0cf4de78a (2026-05-01, "migrate to adcp 3.12.0"). The generic copy arrived three weeks later in 61f6ad9a2 (2026-05-26, "behavioral test foundation for safe refactoring — BDD harness, 10 use cases"), which built the generic layer by lifting bodies out of the domain modules. The lift never completed: registering then_media_buy would have shadowed steps the domain modules already owned, so it was left out of pytest_plugins and the domain original kept grading. uc002_create_media_buy.py:2084-2086 records exactly that decision for a different step in the same module:

    > Moved from steps/generic/then_media_buy.py (a module NOT in pytest_plugins, so the step was unregistered/dead there): registering that whole module would shadow three step texts already registered by domain modules.

    3. What the sentence should assert

    The sentence is not vague and it is not redundant. It is the only line that pins the outcome.

    That is the measurement that decides this. Its immediate predecessor in every one of the 23 executing uses is the response is compliant with the <tool> [success] spec, and that step is deliberately outcome-aware (then_schema.py:54-79):

    if _dispatch_errored(ctx):
    then_error_compliant(ctx)
    return
wire = wire_dict(ctx)

    Its own docstring states the division of labour:

    > It does NOT weaken the grading, because it does not decide whether the outcome was correct — it only decides which contract applies to the outcome that happened. Whether the call SHOULD have succeeded is pinned by the scenario's own assertions sitting beneath this line.

    So a refused create_media_buy still passes the compliance line (graded as an error envelope). Delete the response should succeed and 23 scenarios accept a refusal. It carries real weight.

    Observable restatement: *the buyer received a success document rather than a refusal* — checkable as "a success-path wire body exists". That is exactly _wire_body (_outcome_helpers.py:32-89), which raises expected a success response, got error: ... when the dispatch errored and raises again when the env stashed no body.

    What the current body gets wrong is the source, not the claim:

  • - "error" not in ctx is a harness key, and it is *written by test code*: then_error.py:436 promotes resp.errors[0] into ctx["error"]. Step-ordering, not the wire, can decide it.
  • - require_payload accepts ctx["self_dispatched_response"] (_outcome_helpers.py:401-410) — a payload that never crossed a wire. A scenario whose When self-dispatches gets a green "the response should succeed" from an in-process object. Not exercised by these 23 today; it is the open channel.
  • - Membership (not in) rather than is not None means a step that sets ctx["error"] = None passes.

    • tests/CLAUDE.md already makes the wire the authority for error paths; this is the success-path mirror.

    28 uses, 5 feature files, 4 tools. Liveness measured against tests/bdd/dormant_scenarios.txt and the set of scenario-binding modules in tests/bdd/:

    The proposed meaning holds for all 23 live uses, and this is provable rather than asserted: each is immediately preceded by a compliance line that already calls wire_dict(ctx) on the success path, and the 4 dual-emit scenarios additionally run then_dual_emit_media_buy_status, which opens with wire = wire_dict(ctx). The wire body is demonstrably present in every live scenario. The change can only turn a scenario red where the dispatch was actually refused — which is the point of the sentence.

    No split is warranted. All 23 say the same thing. Three things to note rather than fold in:

  • - The 4 UC-021 uses are the only ones where the sentence is the leading Then with no compliance line. When UC-021 gets a binding module, add Then the response is compliant with the preview_creative spec above it — do not widen this step to compensate for a missing scenario line.
  • - T-UC-019-inv-293-2 is titled *"UNSUPPORTED_FEATURE ... before any DB read"* and then asserts the response should succeed. The scenario's own comment explains the invariant was wrong, so the assertion is right and the title is stale. Scenario-level fix, not a step fix.
  • - the operation should succeed (uc026_package_media_buy.py:1876) is a second spelling of this claim with a weaker body (adds a not-in-_FAILURE_STATUSES check on a typed field). Two sentences for one obligation; worth consolidating, out of scope here.

    One sentence, one body, one home. Delete the dead generic copy; keep a single registration and rewrite it against the wire.

    Placement: the sentence grades 4 tools (create_media_buy, update_media_buy, get_media_buys, and preview_creative once bound). It has no business living in uc002_create_media_buy.py. Move it to tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_success.py, which is already in pytest_plugins and already imports require_payload — no shadow is created, because deleting the uc002 registration in the same change leaves exactly one.

    Body I would write (not applied):

    @then("the response should succeed")
def then_response_succeeds(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """The buyer received a SUCCESS document, not a refusal.

    This is the outcome-PINNING partner to ``the response is compliant with the
    <tool> spec``, which is outcome-AWARE by design: on a refused dispatch
    ``_assert_compliant`` grades ``core/error.json`` and returns green, and its
    docstring delegates the outcome itself to "the scenario's own assertions
    sitting beneath this line". This is that line. Without it, all 23 live uses
    accept a refusal.

    Reads the WIRE. ``wire_dict`` raises naming the recorded error when the
    dispatch was refused, and raises separately when the env stashed no success
    body. The two ctx reads it replaces graded the harness: ``ctx["error"]`` is
    written by test code (``then_error.py`` promotes ``resp.errors[0]`` into it),
    and ``require_payload`` accepts ``ctx["self_dispatched_response"]`` — a
    payload that never crossed a wire.
    """
    wire_dict(ctx)

    The single call *is* the assertion: _wire_body raises on both failure modes. It satisfies test_architecture_bdd_no_pass_steps (a statement beyond the docstring) and test_architecture_bdd_no_trivial_assertions (its stated rule admits "delegate to a helper function"). Adding assert wire_dict(ctx), "..." would only re-check something wire_dict has already raised on.

    Two deletions go with it: uc002_create_media_buy.py:1635-1638, and then_media_buy.py:18-22 (the dead copy — note the same file's then_budget_validation_passes / then_pricing_validation_passes / then_date_validation_passes are duplicated into uc002 the same way and belong to whoever holds the "validation should pass" sentence).

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOES

    Both registrations confirmed by AST scan over tests/bdd/**/*.py (decorator-source match, so parsers.parse sentences are not missed). The brief's two locations are current; nothing moved.

    A — tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_media_buy.py:53-59

    @then(parsers.parse('the response should include a "{field}"'))
def then_response_includes_field(ctx: dict, field: str) -> None:
    """Assert response includes the specified field with a non-None value."""
    resp = require_payload(ctx)
    # Check on the response object — may be CreateMediaBuyResult wrapping a Success
    value = _get_response_field(resp, field)
    assert value is not None, f"Expected '{field}' in response, got None"

    Reads the reconstructed typed payload (TransportResult.payload, or ctx["self_dispatched_response"]). _get_response_field (_outcome_helpers.py:446) tries getattr(resp, field), then getattr(resp.response, field), then dict.get, else None. Asserts non-None uniformly. Never touches the wire.

    B — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1651-1662

    @then(parsers.parse('the response should include a "{field}"'))
def then_response_includes_field(ctx: dict, field: str) -> None:
    """Assert the response includes the specified field."""
    response = require_payload(ctx)
    if hasattr(response, field):
        assert getattr(response, field) is not None, f"Response field '{field}' is None"
    elif isinstance(response, dict):
        assert field in response, f"Response missing field '{field}': {response}"
    else:
        dumped = response.model_dump() if hasattr(response, "model_dump") else {}
        assert field in dumped, f"Response missing field '{field}'"

    Same source (typed payload). No wrapper unwrap. Non-None only on the hasattr branch; the dict and model_dump branches assert key presence only, so a present-but-null field passes. Never touches the wire.

    2. WHY THEY DIVERGED — and the shadow is currently latent, not live

  • - B came first: 0cf4de78a (2026-05-01, "migrate to adcp 3.12.0", #1217), in the domain file.

    • But tests.bdd.steps.generic.then_media_buy is not in tests/bdd/conftest.py's pytest_plugins. It is an allowlisted entry in tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_step_module_reachability.py:52 under reason "(A) dead-pending-harness", and tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_success.py:17 says so outright: *"then_media_buy.py is a helper module that is not registered as a pytest-bdd plugin (its other steps duplicate domain-file steps)."*

    So body A never executes. There is no coin-flip today; B grades every binding. The shadow is a landmine armed for the day #2132 wires the module in — and test_architecture_bdd_no_shadowed_steps.py cannot see it, because both its checks are scoped to pytest_plugins modules only.

    3. WHAT THE SENTENCE SHOULD ASSERT

    The sentence *is* restatable in observable terms — no scenario rewrite needed for the sentence itself. The repo owner's reading ("that one should be generic") is right, and the correct generic meaning is:

    > The key <field> is present on the WIRE response, is not JSON null, and — where it is a string, array or object — is not empty.

    Three things are wrong with the current meaning, all measurable:

    1. It reads the reconstructed payload, not the wire. The three *sibling* assertions in the very same scenario were already migrated: then_response_field_is_iso8601 (uc002:2193), then_response_field_integer_at_least (uc002:2212), then_response_field_is_array (uc002:2231) all open with from tests.bdd.steps._outcome_helpers import wire_dict and read wire_dict(ctx).get(field). Only the {field} step was left at its 2026-05-01 shape. tests/CLAUDE.md and then_schema.py's own docstring make the wire the authority.

    2. Presence-and-non-null alone is already graded, more strictly, by the line above it. Every scenario that binds this sentence also carries Then the response is compliant with the create_media_buy <branch> spec. That step (then_schema.py:252 → response_validator(tool, branch)) narrows to oneOf/0 and validates the wire against the pinned 3.1 schema, whose CreateMediaBuySuccess branch declares required: ["media_buy_id", "confirmed_at", "revision", "packages"] with media_buy_id: {"type": "string"} — so absent fails and null fails. A step that only re-asserts that is a second spelling of the compliance line.

    3. What the schema leaves ungraded is emptiness. {"media_buy_id": ""} validates against type: string. An empty identifier is the failure that defeats POST-S2 ("Buyer can track the media buy via media_buy_id"). That is the one obligation this sentence can own, it is field-agnostic, and it is exactly the precedent already set by its sibling …a "{field}" array, whose docstring says: *"'present' is not the obligation, 'populated' is."*

    The primitive for it already exists — wire_field (_outcome_helpers.py:122) does present-AND-non-null with the diagnostics; only the emptiness clause is new.

    One sentence, one body, kept generic — in the registered module, with the dead twin deleted. Do not split: the four honest bindings ask the same question about three different fields, and the fifth is a different sentence that was mis-spelled, not a second job.

    @then(parsers.parse('the response should include a "{field}"'))
def then_response_includes_field(ctx: dict, field: str) -> None:
    """Assert the WIRE carries *field*, non-null and non-empty.

    Presence and non-nullity are already graded, on the same wire document, by the
    `the response is compliant with the <tool> <branch> spec` line above every
    scenario that uses this one: the pinned CreateMediaBuySuccess branch declares
    media_buy_id required and `type: string`. What that schema does NOT reject is
    the EMPTY string — and an empty identifier is what defeats POST-S2 ("buyer can
    track the media buy via media_buy_id"). Emptiness is this step's obligation,
    the same distinction its sibling `…a "{field}" array` draws.

    Read on the wire, not on `require_payload`: a field can be present on the
    reconstructed typed payload and absent from what the buyer received (#1592),
    and the three assertions this scenario runs after this one already read there.

    from tests.bdd.steps._outcome_helpers import wire_field

    value = wire_field(ctx, field)  # asserts present AND not JSON null
    if isinstance(value, str | list | dict):
        assert value, f"{field!r} is present on the wire but empty ({value!r}) — the buyer cannot use it"

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOESagent 5 of 14

    1. WHAT EACH BODY ACTUALLY DOES

    Both registrations are at the paths the brief names — neither is stale.

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:579 then_error_has_fix_suggestion — reads the wire error object's top-level suggestion via _wire_suggestion(ctx) (→ TransportResult.wire_error_object → extract_wire_suggestion). If that is None it falls back to ctx["error"], and if that object is a pydantic.ValidationError it returns early after checking each errors()[i]["msg"] is a non-empty string — no suggestion involved at all. Otherwise it asserts the string is non-empty *and* that its whitespace-split words intersect a hard-coded 12-verb whitelist:

    action_verbs = {"use","try","check","provide","include","ensure","remove",
                "specify","set","omit","add","verify"}
found = words & action_verbs
assert found, f"Expected actionable fix suggestion with a verb (...), got: {suggestion}"

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2238 then_error_suggestion_for_fix — one call to _current_suggestion(ctx) (same module, line 1660), which reads the same wire position but requires a wire envelope (assert suggestion is not None, "...no wire error envelope was captured") with no exception fallback, then applies a length threshold:

    assert len(suggestion.strip()) >= 5, (
    f"Expected actionable suggestion string (>= 5 chars), got {suggestion!r}. ")

    Same read position, three differences: envelope-required vs. exception-fallback, ≥5 chars vs. verb-vocabulary, and a ValidationError escape hatch that only the generic one has.

    This pair is not a coin-flip. then_error is a global plugin (tests/bdd/conftest.py pytest_plugins, 29 entries); uc019_query_media_buys is not in that list — it is imported module-locally, deliberately:

    # tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:9-14
# Register UC-019 step definitions LOCALLY (module scope) ... The uc019 module
# intentionally redefines 8 generic step texts (sandbox / validation-error assertions);
from tests.bdd.steps.domain.uc019_query_media_buys import *

    pytest-bdd 8.1.0 inject_fixturedefs_for_step sorts candidate fixturedefs by iterparentnodeids(baseid) and lets pytest take the most specific, so the test module's copy deterministically wins for UC-019 and the plugin's for everyone else. The defect is still real — one sentence, two obligations, split by which use case you are in — but report it as a scoped override, not registration-order roulette.

    2. WHY THEY DIVERGED

    Generic came first: then_error_has_fix_suggestion lands in 7f0d45a4e (2026-03-19, #1146), already with the "actionable language" framing; the verb whitelist arrives in ccf91eb93 (2026-04-01, #1170). The uc019 copy lands in 61f6ad9a2 (2026-05-26, #1335) with the whole UC-019 step module, then is rewritten wire-first under salesagent-3dawm.18.

    Neither author was reading the scenario. The obligation is written in the feature file at all twelve use sites, and it says something neither body checks.

    3. WHAT THE SENTENCE SHOULD ASSERT

    Every one of the 12 occurrences carries the same trailing comment:

        And the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue
    # BR-RULE-209 INV-7: sandbox validation errors are real
    # POST-F3: suggestion field present

    POST-F3 is the storyboard postcondition ("Buyer knows how to fix the issue and retry"), and at these sites its realization is spelled out: *suggestion field present*. Restated observably:

    > The buyer received a wire error envelope, and its payload-layer error object carries a non-empty top-level suggestion (the STRICT error.json position — one buried in details does not count, #1417).

    That is checkable from what the buyer received. Both current extras fail that test, for measured reasons:

    The verb whitelist grades adcp's copywriting, not this system. suggestion is not authored at raise sites; CodeEntry refuses an empty one at construction (src/core/errors/codes.py:108) and the text comes from the pinned error-code.json enumMetadata. Measured against the live table:

    total codes: 100
codes whose suggestion has NO verb from the list: 55
  VALIDATION_ERROR -> review error details and fix field values
  CONFLICT         -> re-read the resource and retry with current state
  RATE_LIMITED     -> retry after the retry_after interval
  ...

    55 of 100 pinned suggestions fail an assertion that claims to grade whether a suggestion is actionable. Every one of them tells the buyer exactly how to fix the issue. The step passes today only because the live scenarios land on INVALID_REQUEST → "check request parameters and fix", which contains "check". Bumping a suggestion's wording upstream in adcp turns this red with no behavior change here.

    The ≥5-char threshold is the same oracle, weaker. CodeEntry already refuses ""; the only strings between empty and 5 characters that could reach it are ones the constructor would have to allow first. It adds nothing over non-emptiness.

    The repo has already ruled on text oracles, in tests/bdd/features/BR-CODES-002-table-resolved-suggestion.feature:

    > WHAT THESE SCENARIOS ASSERT, AND WHY NOT MORE: presence and non-emptiness only. The text is deliberately NOT asserted. Every available oracle for it is degenerate — comparing it to CODE_TABLE grades the table against itself, comparing it to the string it replaced checks for the absence of a defect, and writing the pin's sentence into a feature file transcribes the pinned file into a test.

    The verb list is a fourth degenerate oracle: it invents a vocabulary the pin does not commit to. Presence on the wire is not vacuous — BR-CODES-002 records that 220 of 294 construction sites emitted no suggestion at all before the field was table-resolved, so the envelope actually carrying it in the strict position is a real claim about serialization.

    One finding on the generic body's ValidationError branch: it makes the step passable by a request that never reached the seller. _get_error_code maps a client-side pydantic.ValidationError to "VALIDATION_ERROR" (then_error.py:138-143), so a scenario whose request dies at construction satisfies then_validation_error, then_real_validation_error, and this step — while the buyer received nothing. That branch grades the harness, exactly the class the brief rules out. uc019's _current_suggestion already refuses it and should be the shape that survives.

    4. WHICH SCENARIOS USE IT

    12 occurrences, one per feature, all the same generated BR-RULE-209 INV-7 sandbox-validation scenario:

    FeatureBound to a test module?RoutingBody that runs today
    BR-UC-011-manage-accounts:1426yeswired (uc011-sync)generic (verb check) — live

    The proposed meaning holds for all 12: each is an error path where an AdCP-coded rejection reaches the buyer, and POST-F3 is the same obligation in every one. No split is needed — the sentence is doing one job, and it is the same job as the error should include a "suggestion" field.

    Two live scenarios only, and the change is safe for both: UC-011 asserts INVALID_REQUEST on the wire (envelope present, suggestion "check request parameters and fix"), UC-019 already runs the envelope-required helper. The xfailed five are unmeasured — if any of them reaches green while dying at pydantic construction, dropping the ValidationError escape will keep it xfailing, which is the correct verdict for a scenario whose buyer never got an answer.

    5. RECOMMENDATION

    One sentence, one body: presence and non-emptiness on the wire. Delete then_error_suggestion_for_fix from uc019_query_media_buys.py entirely (its module comment says the local overrides exist to *strengthen* generic steps; once the generic body is the strict one, the override has nothing left to do), and replace the generic body with:

    @then("the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue")
def then_error_has_fix_suggestion(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """POST-F3 at every one of this sentence's 12 sites: the buyer received an
    error envelope carrying a non-empty top-level ``suggestion``.

    Text is NOT graded. ``suggestion`` is resolved from CODE_TABLE out of the
    pinned ``error-code.json`` enumMetadata, so any vocabulary check grades
    adcp's copy: measured, 55 of 100 pinned suggestions -- VALIDATION_ERROR's
    "review error details and fix field values" among them -- contain none of
    the twelve verbs the old whitelist demanded, while telling the buyer
    exactly how to fix the issue. BR-CODES-002's feature file already ruled
    every text oracle degenerate; this is presence, the honest claim.

    No exception fallback: a request that died at pydantic construction means
    the buyer received nothing, and "the error should include ..." has no
    subject. The old fallback returned green off a client-side ValidationError's
    field messages, which graded the harness.
    """
    envelope = wire_error_envelope_or_none(ctx)
    assert envelope is not None, (
        "Expected a wire error envelope to read a suggestion from, and none was captured -- "
        "the dispatch either succeeded or failed before reaching a transport."
    )
    from tests.harness.transport import extract_wire_suggestion

    suggestion = extract_wire_suggestion(envelope)
    assert isinstance(suggestion, str) and suggestion.strip(), (
        f"Expected a non-empty top-level suggestion on the error object (STRICT error.json "
        f"position -- one buried in details does not count, #1417), got {suggestion!r}: {envelope}"
    )

    Not applied, per the brief.

    Coordination note. This is now byte-identical in obligation to the error should include a "suggestion" field, which is *also* double-registered (then_error.py:559 vs uc019_query_media_buys.py:1743) and is presumably another agent's sentence. The right end state is both sentences stacked as two @then decorators on this one function. I have left that merge alone so the two decisions do not collide; whoever lands second should stack rather than write a second body.

    0. Two premise corrections, both measured

    (a) This pair is not a semantic coin-flip — the two bodies are the same body. Both were AST-compared with docstrings dropped and every string constant blanked:

    STRICT equal : False
RELAXED equal: True

    Both normalize to exactly:

    assert '<S>' not in ctx, f"<S>{ctx.get('<S>')}"
resp = require_payload(ctx)
media_buy_id = _get_response_field(resp, '<S>')
assert media_buy_id, '<S>'

    The only difference is — (em dash) vs -- in the docstring and two assertion messages. Both call the *same* require_payload / _get_response_field from tests/bdd/steps/_outcome_helpers.py (generic imports them at then_media_buy.py:862-863; uc002 at line 22). Whatever normalizer produced "zero of eleven pairs share a body" was constant-sensitive; for this pair its answer is an artifact.

    (b) There is no shadowing either, because one registration is dead. tests.bdd.steps.generic.then_media_buy is not in pytest_plugins (tests/bdd/conftest.py:57-87). It is an explicit entry in the "dead-pending-harness" allowlist of tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_step_module_reachability.py:52, and the repo already says so twice in comments — then_success.py:17 ("not registered as a pytest-bdd plugin") and uc002_create_media_buy.py:2084 ("a module NOT in pytest_plugins, so the step was unregistered/dead there"). The uc002 copy is the only live registration.

    (c) And that live registration never executes either. The one scenario using the sentence xfails at a blanket route gate, on all three transports:

    XFAIL ...::test_inv1_holds__total_budget_greater_than_zero[a2a] - UC-002 harness not yet wired for non-extension scenarios
XFAIL ...::test_inv1_holds__total_budget_greater_than_zero[mcp] - UC-002 harness not yet wired for non-extension scenarios
XFAIL ...::test_inv1_holds__total_budget_greater_than_zero[rest] - UC-002 harness not yet wired for non-extension scenarios

    Gate: EnvRoute(tag="uc002-not-wired", when=_uc("UC-002", lambda m: True), xfail_reason=...) at conftest.py:5280-5285. So nothing about this sentence grades anything today, in any copy.

    1. What each body actually does

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_media_buy.py:34-40 — reads ctx["error"] (asserts the key is absent), then require_payload(ctx), which returns ctx["result"].payload (the typed payload off the TransportResult the dispatch seam stashed) or falls back to ctx["self_dispatched_response"]. Pulls media_buy_id off it via _get_response_field (attr → .response attr → dict key → None) and asserts truthy. Reads nothing from the wire dict and nothing from the DB.

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1641-1647 — identical, character-for-character except em dash vs --. Same two helpers, same two reads, same two assertions.

    Neither asserts anything about a budget.

    2. Why they diverged

    They did not diverge; one was pasted. Pickaxe:

  • - generic: 61f6ad9a2 2026-05-26 feat: behavioral test foundation ... BDD harness, 10 use cases (#1335) — first.
  • - uc002: 9ac08dad3 2026-07-14 feat: UC-002/003 validation wiring + canonical error-code reconciliation (#1417) — second.

    • #1417 needed the step live for UC-002. The generic module it lived in could not be registered (registering it "would shadow three step texts already registered by domain modules" — the comment at uc002_create_media_buy.py:2084-2086), so the function was copied into a registered module and the original left in place. The em-dash-to--- change is the fingerprint of the paste.

    Two siblings show the same sentence family was never finished: the pricing validation should pass (feature lines 665, 675) and the date validation should pass (line 755) exist *only* in the dead generic module. They have no live binding at all; they are invisible today only because the same uc002-not-wired gate xfails their scenarios before the missing-binding detector runs.

    3. What the sentence should assert

    The sentence names BR-RULE-008, Budget Positivity. Production's budget validation is exactly three lines, at src/core/tools/media_buy_create.py:2203-2210:

    total_budget = req.get_total_budget()
budget_err = validate_budget_positive(total_budget, field=PACKAGES_FIELD)
if budget_err:
    raise AdCPBudgetTooLowError(field=PACKAGES_FIELD, context=req.context)

    So "the budget validation should pass" means, precisely: the sum the seller graded is the sum the buyer sent, it was accepted, and the accepted per-package budgets come back unaltered.

    That is observable. Measured on the wire path:

  • - Per-package budget is echoed: Package(..., budget=package.budget, ...) at media_buy_create.py:4097, and packages is required on the CreateMediaBuySuccess branch of the pinned 3.1/media-buy/create-media-buy-response.json.
  • - Response-level total_budget is not emitted — it is absent from the CreateMediaBuySuccess.sync_success(...) kwargs at media_buy_create.py:4110-4129, and the schema itself marks it conditional ("Present when the seller can compute a deterministic aggregate"). A body asserting total_budget == 5000 on the wire would fail. This is worth stating because it is the assertion a reader would reach for first.
  • - The DB row also carries it (MediaBuy.total_budget: Mapped[float | None]), and there is in-repo precedent for grading it that way: then_package_budget_persisted (then_media_buy.py:384, used by BR-UC-003 line 460).

    • What the current body asserts instead — "no error key, and the payload has a media_buy_id" — is budget-agnostic. It is a restatement of "the response should succeed" plus "it was the success branch, not submitted". And the second half is already sayable with a step the same feature file uses five times: the response is compliant with the create_media_buy success spec (BR-UC-002 lines 50, 818, 925, 1994). By the lane's own precedent — prefer assertions that *resolve* from what the scenario names over assertions that *restate* it — the existing body is strictly the weaker spelling of a step that already exists.

    4. Which scenarios use it

    Exactly one, tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:703-708:

    @T-UC-002-inv-008-1 @invariant @BR-RULE-008
Scenario: INV-1 holds -- total budget greater than zero

  And the account exists and is active

  And the budget validation should pass

    The proposed meaning holds for it, and improves it. The Given already establishes the number and self-checks it (given_media_buy.py:495-511 asserts the package budgets sum to the claimed amount). One scenario means no split is needed — the sentence is not doing two jobs, it is doing no job.

    Note the scenario's other weakness while you are in here: line 707 uses the whole-oneOf compliance form, which accepts the CreateMediaBuySubmitted branch. The scenario pins one outcome, so it should use the branch form. The then_response_compliant docstring says this itself: "Where a scenario pins ONE outcome, use the branch form — it is strictly stronger, and this one would accept the branch the scenario says did NOT happen."

    5. Recommendation

    One sentence, one body, in the live module — and delete the dead twin.

    1. Delete then_budget_validation_passes from tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_media_buy.py (and, in the same pass, decide the fate of its two orphan siblings then_pricing_validation_passes / then_date_validation_passes, which have no live binding anywhere — either move them to a registered module or record them as dormant).

    2. Strengthen the surviving body in tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1641 to grade the budget rather than the absence of an error.

    3. Change feature line 707 to the branch form, Then the response is compliant with the create_media_buy success spec, which subsumes the media_buy_id half of the old body.

    The body I would write (not applied):

    @then("the budget validation should pass")
def then_budget_validation_passes(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """BR-RULE-008: the buyer's budget was graded positive and accepted UNALTERED.

    Production grades ``req.get_total_budget()`` — the sum over every package —
    against ``validate_budget_positive`` (media_buy_create.py:2203). "Passed"
    is therefore observable as: the success response echoes package budgets that
    still sum to what the buyer sent. Response-level ``total_budget`` is NOT
    asserted: production does not emit it on this branch (sync_success at
    media_buy_create.py:4110 omits it) and the pinned schema makes it conditional.
    """
    resp = require_payload(ctx)
    sent = sum(float(p.get("budget", 0)) for p in ctx["request_kwargs"]["packages"])
    assert sent > 0, f"scenario setup sent a non-positive budget ({sent}) — it cannot assert acceptance"

    packages = _get_response_field(resp, "packages")
    assert packages, f"budget validation passed but the response carries no packages: {resp!r}"
    accepted = sum(
        float((p if isinstance(p, dict) else p.model_dump()).get("budget") or 0) for p in packages
    )
    assert accepted == sent, (
        f"the seller accepted a total budget of {accepted} but the buyer sent {sent} — "
        "BR-RULE-008 graded a different number than the request carried"
    )

    Two honest caveats on this recommendation:

  • - It is unverifiable until the harness gate lifts. T-UC-002-inv-008-1 xfails at uc002-not-wired before any dispatch happens, so this body cannot be shown to go green (or red) today. Land it together with the UC-002 harness wiring (#2132 territory), or land it now and treat it as ungraded — but do not record it as verified.
  • - If you want the persistence half too, say it in a second sentence, not by widening this one: And the package budget should be persisted as 5000 already exists (then_media_buy.py:384) and is used by BR-UC-003. That is the "two clearly-named sentences" answer, and it applies to the *response vs. row* split — not to the shadowing, which has no second behavior in it at all.

    Verdict: AGREE with the core recommendation (delete the sentence, delete both bodies, write no third), with two objections to the migration plan and one addition to the diagnosis.

    1. Accuracy of both descriptions — accurate

    I re-read both. The quotes are verbatim and the characterizations hold.

    Generic (tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:813-849): conditional wire grade hard-coded to VALIDATION_ERROR, then unconditional isinstance(error, ValidationError) + error.errors(). Domain (tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2209-2237): returns if result.wire_error_envelope is not None, else raises. No code, no field, no structure.

    The two supporting claims also check out:

  • - Deterministic resolution, not a coin flip. tests/bdd/conftest.py:57-86 does not list uc019_query_media_buys; tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:14 star-imports it at module scope, and tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py:44 pins the sentence in INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES.
  • - Tautology in UC-019. then_error_code (then_error.py:504) has no reconstructed fallback at all — its docstring records that the fallback was removed after an instrumented run showed 2642/2643 scenarios already graded the wire. So the error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST" on line 1545 already refuses without an envelope, and the domain body on 1546 asserts a strict subset.

    • 2. One thing the diagnosis under-states, and it matters

    The diagnosis says the isinstance check "grades the harness" and "passes hardest in the case that most disproves the claim." Both true, but it stops short of the sharper fact: the generic body cannot pass on any wire dispatch at all.

    WireError is class WireError(Exception) (tests/harness/_base.py:94) — not a pydantic.ValidationError. _dispatch.py:98 writes it into ctx["error"] unconditionally on the error branch. So whenever the buyer actually received an error, isinstance(error, ValidationError) is False and the step fails. It passes only via the blanket except Exception at _dispatch.py:201, i.e. when env.call_via blew up in-process and nothing reached a transport.

    The sentence is therefore inverted, not merely unobservable: it fails exactly when validation demonstrably ran, and passes exactly when it demonstrably did not. Consequence for planning: the five xfailed scenarios (UC-002/004/005/006/018) carry conftest reasons like "sandbox mode not implemented in create_media_buy", but this step guarantees a red for them even after that production gap closes. Deleting it is a prerequisite for graduating any of them, not a tidy-up afterwards.

    3. Is the proposed assertion observable? Yes — verified, not assumed

    then_response_error_issue (then_error.py:362-410) reads wire_error_dict(ctx) → locate_envelope_error(envelope) → error["issues"], matching keyword and the trailing segments of pointer. Every value comes from the captured envelope; it touches no ctx key the harness authored. It cannot be satisfied by a canned sandbox response, and it is strictly stronger than an isinstance check.

    Confirmed live: T-UC-011-sandbox-validation passes on a2a, mcp and rest in test-results/innet_060926_1901/bdd_inprocess.json. That is the existence proof.

    4. Would the proposal weaken anything? No — walked five, plus a correction

    The key fact the diagnosis leaves implicit: ten of the eleven scenarios already assert the wire code on the line immediately above the sentence. the response should indicate a validation error (then_error.py:790) grades assert_wire_error("VALIDATION_ERROR") when an envelope exists. So the generic body's wire half is redundant in every one of them, exactly as the domain body's presence check is redundant in UC-019.

    ScenarioBefore (this line adds)After
    UC-019:1546envelope exists — subset of error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST" on 1545line gone; code claim unchanged
    UC-018:661VALIDATION_ERROR (dup of 660) + guaranteed-red isinstanceline gone; 660 still grades the code
    UC-004:1120same dup + guaranteed-red isinstanceline gone; 1119 still grades the code
    UC-002:2021samesame
    UC-005:1104samesame

    Nothing specific becomes truthy. The only unique content anywhere in either body is the isinstance check, which is unsatisfiable-when-real. Net loss from deletion: zero. And since the recommendation keeps neither body, question 4's "does something get silently dropped" answers itself.

    Objection A — step 3 is unproven for five of six, and is a live regression risk for the one green scenario

    grep over tests/bdd/features/ returns exactly one use of the response error issues include keyword ... for field ...: BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:1425. So there is evidence that production emits structured issues[] for sync_accounts and for nothing else. The diagnosis proposes adding the line to six scenarios covering get_media_buys, list_creatives, create_media_buy, list_creative_formats, sync_audiences, sync_catalogs — with no check that any of those paths carries issues[].

    For five of them that is merely unverified (they are xfailed or dead, so the line lands harmless). For UC-019 it is a regression risk: it is the only currently-passing scenario touching this sentence, and swapping a tautology for keyword enum for field status_filter could turn it red on all three transports.

    What I would do instead: delete the sentence from all 11 features and both bodies as one change (that part is right and safe), and treat the issues[] upgrade as a separate, evidence-first change — instrument one dispatch of get_media_buys with an out-of-enum status_filter, read the actual envelope, and add the line only where the structure is really there. Where it is not, that is a production finding worth a ticket, not a scenario edit. UC-019 loses nothing in the interim: the error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST" plus the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue already grade it more tightly than the line being removed.

    Objection B — the fork is mis-attributed, and the real one survives the fix

    The diagnosis says UC-019 forked because the generic body smuggles a code claim the sentence does not contain. True as far as it goes. But the contradiction is upstream of the step: UC-018's scenario ("invalid status filter") asserts VALIDATION_ERROR via line 660, while UC-019's scenario ("invalid status_filter") asserts INVALID_REQUEST on line 1545. Same defect class, two codes, both written in the Gherkin. Deleting the sentence does not touch that and should not be described as resolving it. It deserves its own finding alongside the dead-Gherkin one.

    One hazard the plan does not name

    tests/bdd/conftest.py auto-xfails scenarios with missing step definitions. If the two bodies are deleted while any of the 11 feature lines survives, those scenarios do not error — they go quietly to xfail, and UC-019 silently drops from passed to xfailed. The edit must be atomic across all 11 features in the same commit.

    Confirmations of the diagnosis's incidental claims

  • - Feature occurrences: 11, exactly the UCs listed (001, 002, 004, 005, 006, 015, 016, 018, 019, 020, 023).
  • - Dead Gherkin: grep -rln "BR-UC-<n>-" tests/bdd/*.py returns nothing for 001, 015, 016, 020, 023. Confirmed.
  • - Run outcomes match innet_060926_1901 exactly: UC-019 passed ×3; UC-018 xfailed ×3; UC-002/004/005/006 xfailed [a2a].
  • - Dropping the entry from INTENTIONAL_OVERRIDES has precedent in that same file — the eighth override was removed after an instrumented run proved it a trap (see its docstring, lines 32-41). That guard is designed to demand exactly this decision.

    • Files: tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py, tests/bdd/steps/generic/_dispatch.py, tests/harness/_base.py, tests/unit/test_uc019_intentional_step_overrides.py.

    AGREE with the recommendation. All three edits are correct as written; I have one factual correction to its raagent 8 of 14

    AGREE with the recommendation. All three edits are correct as written; I have one factual correction to its rationale, one scope finding it missed, and one placement note.

    1. Both bodies re-read — descriptions are accurate

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_media_buy.py:53-59 and tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1649-1660 (the report says 1651-1662; off by two, immaterial). Both quoted bodies match byte-for-byte. _get_response_field (_outcome_helpers.py:446) is described exactly: hasattr → resp.response unwrap → dict.get → None. I reproduced its divergence table and got the same three results.

    Two things the report did not say, neither of which changes its conclusion: the generic module's require_payload / _get_response_field imports sit at lines 862-863, the bottom of the file, not the head — so the module does import cleanly; and its resp = require_payload(ctx) at line 22 is an unused binding.

    Its deadness claim is confirmed from two independent places: tests.bdd.steps.generic.then_media_buy is absent from tests/bdd/conftest.py's pytest_plugins and present in _ALLOWED_UNREGISTERED under reason (A), and no test module imports it (grep -rn then_media_buy tests/ returns only the file itself). The shadowed-steps guard's scoping claim is also confirmed — its module docstring states both checks run "over every module listed in tests/bdd/conftest.py's pytest_plugins."

    2. The proposed assertion is observable

    wire_field → wire_lookup → _wire_body (_outcome_helpers.py:55-88) reads ctx["result"].require_wire() — the wire held on TransportResult — and the serializer fallback was deleted: *"every BDD scenario now runs on a real wire, so a missing wire is a defect in the env, never a legitimate no-wire case."* Nothing here reads a ctx key the harness authored. This is the correct answer to the grading-the-fixture concern, and it is the axis on which the current body fails.

    3. No scenario is weakened

    Re-measured the binding with the real parser rather than by eye. parsers.parse matches at feature lines 53, 2144, 2177, 2196, 2311; it does not match 2180 (… array) or 2432 (… block). Liveness re-derived from ENV_ROUTES and _UC002_V31_SUCCESS_WIRED (conftest.py:4136-4138, 5256-5263): only T-UC-002-v31-success-revision-and-actions escapes uc002-not-wired, whose row calls pytest.xfail(...) imperatively, so the other four never execute a Then step at all.

    linebeforeafter

    53 (media_buy_id, xfail)never runsnever runs; stronger when #2132 wires it
    2144 (task_id, xfail, submitted branch)never runsnever runs; task_id is required in CreateMediaBuySubmitted and has no minLength, so emptiness is a real addition

    Nothing turns specific into truthy. The one live scenario's other three assertions (uc002_create_media_buy.py:2193/2212/2231) already open with wire_dict(ctx).get(field), and that scenario appears in no known-failures or dormancy ledger, which is direct evidence the wire read works on every transport it runs on.

    I verified the schema claim rather than taking it: in .venv/.../adcp/_schemas/3.1/bundled/media-buy/create-media-buy-response.json, CreateMediaBuySuccess has required: ["media_buy_id","confirmed_at","revision","packages"] and media_buy_id: {"type": "string"} with no minLength. So "" is schema-valid, and emptiness is genuinely the one obligation the compliance line above does not already own.

    Correction to its rationale. It claims presence-and-non-null is "already graded by the line above it" unconditionally. _assert_compliant (then_schema.py:77-79) is outcome-aware — on an errored dispatch it grades the *error* contract and returns. On an error path the compliance line passes and this step is what fails. Old and new bodies both fail there, so no behavior changes, but the proposed docstring should scope that sentence to the success path rather than assert it flatly.

    4. Nothing is dropped by keeping B

    B never had A's resp.response unwrap, and that is A's only unique capability — moot once the read moves to the flattened wire. A never executes, so no scenario loses anything. The generic module's *other* users of _get_response_field are untouched by deleting lines 53-59, and it keeps 31 other step definitions, so its _ALLOWED_UNREGISTERED entry stays valid under _defines_steps.

    Two things it missed

    The module has three exact-text collisions with the registered domain module, not one. Measured by AST over both files:

    then 'the response should succeed'
then 'the budget validation should pass'
then 'the response should include a "{field}"'

    The first two have byte-identical bodies in both files, so they are harmless duplicates; the {field} one is the only divergent pair. That makes the recommendation *more* right — it targets the one collision that would change grading — but the report should say #2132 still has two duplicates to clear in this module, not zero.

    Placement. Domain modules *are* in pytest_plugins, so a field-agnostic sentence living in uc002_create_media_buy.py is globally bindable: a future UC-003 or UC-018 line with this text binds to UC-002's body. That is tolerable precisely because the new body is field-agnostic and wire-based, but it does not satisfy the owner's "that one should be generic" reading. The honest home is a *registered* generic module — tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_payload.py is in pytest_plugins and already imports wire_field. I would not block on it (that file's docstring is creative-formats flavored, so it is not a clean home either), but the docstring should record that the step is generic-by-intent and lives in the domain module only because generic/then_media_buy is unregistered.

    AGREE with the recommendation — one body, envelope-required, presence and non-emptiness at the strict error.json position, and delete the uc019 override. Four corrections to the reasoning, one of which strengthens the case beyond what it argued.

    1. Accuracy of its reading of the two bodies

    Verified in .. Locations, structure, and the routing analysis are all correct:

  • - tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:579 and tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:2238 both exist as described; the verb set, the ValidationError early return, the >= 5 threshold are quoted correctly.
