  • - The 55/100 measurement reproduces exactly: total 100 no-verb 55, with INVALID_REQUEST -> check request parameters and fix (the only reason the step is green) and VALIDATION_ERROR -> review error details and fix field values among the 55.
  • - CodeEntry.__post_init__ (src/core/errors/codes.py:107-112) does refuse empty suggestion/message, and CODE_TABLE is *derived* from the pinned wheel's enumMetadata (_build_code_table, line 408, reading document["enumMetadata"] at line 271). So the verb whitelist grades a vendored string this repo does not author. That argument holds.

    • What it got wrong: it describes _wire_suggestion as an envelope check. It is not.

    def extract_wire_suggestion(envelope):        # tests/harness/transport.py:76
    if not envelope: return None
    errors = envelope.get("errors") or [{}]
    adcp_error = envelope.get("adcp_error") or {}
    return errors[0].get("suggestion") or adcp_error.get("suggestion")

    None comes back in two distinct cases: no envelope at all, *and* an envelope whose error object has no top-level suggestion (including one buried in details, which #1417 deliberately refuses). Both bodies conflate them, so both descriptions in the diagnosis are wrong on the same axis:

  • - The report says uc019's helper "requires a wire envelope". It doesn't — it fails with "...no wire error envelope was captured" even when an envelope *was* captured and the suggestion was simply missing or buried. It fails correctly and diagnoses wrongly, which is the #1417 case specifically.
  • - The report says the generic body falls back "if that is None... for IMPL/no-wire scenarios". It also falls back when a real wire error omitted suggestion — the exact defect POST-F3 exists to detect.

    • 2. Is the proposal observable?

    Yes, and it is the only one of the three that is.

    wire_error_envelope is real wire bytes: _real_wire_error_envelope (tests/bdd/steps/_outcome_helpers.py:252-268) is documented "Wire-only, with no synthesized-envelope disjunction behind it", and _envelope_from_adcp_error populates a *separate* synthesized_error_envelope field that only the deleted ImplDispatcher used. A2A reads the artifact DataPart, REST the HTTP body, MCP the JSON in ToolError.

    ctx["error"] is the opposite — harness-set from whatever the When step caught, including a client-side pydantic ValidationError raised during request construction. Grading it is grading the fixture.

    3. Does it weaken any scenario? No — and the generic body is weaker than it claims today

    The finding it should have led with. Chase the generic fallback one hop further than it did. For an AdCPSalesAgentError, _get_error_dict (then_error.py:164-181) does not read anything the buyer received — it calls build_two_layer_error_envelope(error) and rebuilds the envelope, and that builder sets suggestion=exc.suggestion (src/core/exceptions.py:1173) from a CODE_TABLE entry that cannot be empty. So a wire transport that drops suggestion, or emits it inside details, produces _wire_suggestion → None → _get_error_dict → freshly rebuilt envelope → suggestion present → PASS. The generic step is structurally incapable of failing on a serialization drop, which is the single non-vacuous claim it makes. That is worse than the ValidationError branch the report found, and it is fixed by the same code — for the reason the report did not state, so the proposed docstring should say it.

    Three scenarios, before → after:

    ScenarioTodayAfter
    UC-011 sandbox-validation (live, uc011-sync, generic body)non-empty suggestion from wire-or-rebuild + one of 12 verbs, green only because "check request parameters and fix" contains "check"envelope present + non-empty top-level suggestion.     UC-002 sandbox-validation (xfail, generic body)can go green with no wire at all: _get_error_code maps client-side ValidationError → "VALIDATION_ERROR" (then_error.py:138-145), and the branch returns after checking each errors()[i]["msg"] is a non-empty string — production was never askedmust produce a wire envelope.     Nothing is turned from specific into truthy. Both removed checks are text oracles over a derived string; both added checks (envelope presence, strict-position presence) are claims about what the buyer received.

    One premise it under-uses. It hedges about no-wire scenarios. There are none: tests/bdd/conftest.py:4458-4462 — *"IMPL sunsetted: it adds no coverage the wire transports don't, and it has no wire envelope (so it can't participate in error-envelope assertions). The four truthful transports are a2a/mcp/rest + e2e_rest."* Requiring the envelope costs nothing at any site, not just these 12.

    4. Does the surviving body drop anything?

    No. The generic body would gain uc019's envelope requirement and lose only the two degenerate text checks; uc019's _current_suggestion stays live for the error should include a "suggestion" field at line 1743, so no dead helper. Two housekeeping notes for whoever lands it:

  • - tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:11 says the module "intentionally redefines 8 generic step texts". Measured against all of tests/bdd/steps/generic/, it is 5 today (4 after this deletion). Fix or drop the number.

    • Where I'd go further than it did

    Stack the two decorators now rather than deferring. Once the fallback is gone, the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue and the error should include a "suggestion" field are the same assertion, and then_error_has_suggestion (then_error.py:559-576) carries the *same* rebuild-the-envelope fallback via _get_error_dict. Leaving two independently-maintained bodies with identical obligations is how this shadow got here. If the other agent's change lands first, this one should delete its body and add the decorator; if this one lands first, say so in the docstring so the second does the same.

For an *echo* assertion this is the whole point: what can drop budget between production and the buyer is the serializer stack in src/core/schemas/_base.py — the same machinery that silently dropped confirmed_at when two wrap serializers collided (documented at _base.py:802-808). A payload read cannot see that. Use packages = wire_field(ctx, "packages") and drop the isinstance(...)/model_dump() branch; the wire hands you dicts.

    (B) accepted == sent is stronger than the pin. In the pinned 3.1/media-buy/create-media-buy-response.json, the success branch's Package item has required: ["package_id"]. budget is optional, and the MUST-echo language in the schema covers product_id only. A conformant seller that omits per-package budget turns this scenario red for a non-violation. Production does echo it today (media_buy_create.py:4097), so it would pass — but the sentence would then pin a local implementation choice under a @BR-RULE-008 tag, which is exactly the over-specified-scenario hazard .claude/rules/workflows/xpass-graduation.md step 3 warns about. Either say so explicitly in the docstring ("this pins OUR echo; the pin permits omission"), or grade the persisted MediaPackage.budget instead — which is a real mandated-by-nothing-but-actually-written observable and is what the fourth orphan step already attempts.

    AGREE with the recommendation — one registration, in then_success.py, body wire_dict(ctx), both existing copies deleted.     1. Are the body descriptions accurate?

    Yes, on every point I could check, including the one that looked wrong. then_media_buy.py has only three imports at the top (__future__, pytest_bdd, _harness_db) and uses require_payload at line 22 — but the module puts two more imports at the bottom, lines 862-863:

from tests.bdd.steps._outcome_helpers import payload_or_none, require_payload, wire_absent

    So the dead copy would run if it were ever registered. Both bodies are quoted correctly, and the only difference really is the unused resp = binding.

    The deadness claim also holds independently of the reachability allowlist the diagnosis cites. tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_no_shadowed_steps.py has _ALLOWED_SHADOWS: set[str] = set() — empty, no allowlist by design — and _scan_shadowed_steps iterates only _registered_step_plugins(), i.e. the pytest_plugins list. I ran it: green at HEAD. The duplicate is invisible to the shadow guard precisely because then_media_buy is not registered. That is corroboration the brief's "shadowed pair" framing is wrong and the diagnosis's correction is right.

    2. Is the proposed assertion observable?

    Yes, and it is stronger than the diagnosis argues. wire_dict(ctx) → _wire_body(ctx) → TransportResult.require_wire():

    assert self.is_success, f"expected a success wire body, got error {self.error!r}"
assert self.wire_response is not None, ("wire_response is None on a successful call — ...")

    and is_success is self.error is None and self.payload is not None (tests/harness/transport.py:382-383). No ctx key written by test code is consulted.

    3. Does it weaken any scenario?

    No. Three walked, before → after:

  • - T-UC-002-v31-idempotency-replay (live, 3 transports). Before: "error" not in ctx (a key _dispatch.py:98 writes from result.error) + payload non-None. After: result.is_success + a stashed wire body.   • - T-UC-003-ext-scheduled-status (live). Same before/after. Its next line, the wire media_buy_status should be "pending_start", already reads the wire.
  • - T-UC-019-ext-e (live). Same before/after, and this is the one scenario where the sentence is the terminal Then.

    • Worth stating plainly: for every currently-executing use, the change is a behavioral no-op, because _assert_compliant already ran wire_dict(ctx) one line earlier on the success branch. The value is entirely prospective — it closes the self-dispatch and no-wire channels before UC-021 gets bound. The diagnosis presents it as a live strengthening; it isn't.

    4. Does the kept body drop anything?

    No, and here the diagnosis undersells its own proposal. It says the current body checks "no error + payload present" and the new one checks the wire, implying a trade. There is no trade: require_wire's assert self.is_success is error is None and payload is not None, so the new body subsumes require_payload for every dispatched scenario. The only dropped path is ctx["self_dispatched_response"], which is the intended fix — flag it for whoever binds UC-021, since a self-dispatching When will now hard-fail rather than pass on an in-process object.

    I also verified the guard claim empirically rather than reading it: running the guard's own AST helpers on the proposed body gives _has_meaningful_assertion_or_delegation → True, _has_a_skippable_branch → False. It passes. One caveat: wire_dict matches neither the assert_* nor the require_* prefix in _is_asserting_helper_name, so the body must stay straight-line — the moment anyone adds an if, the reachability rule fires.

    Objection 1 — the liveness inventory is wrong by ~3x

    Objection 2 — the sentence does not carry the weight claimed for it

    The diagnosis calls this "the measurement that decides this": *"Delete the response should succeed and 23 scenarios accept a refusal."* That is false for every live use but one, because the following line already requires the payload or the wire:

    That is still a sufficient reason to keep the sentence and to fix its body. It is not a reason to describe the current state as 23 scenarios one step away from accepting refusals.

    Worktree is unchanged (no edits made). Findings below.

    0. Locations — both are current, plus one the brief did not know about

    AST scan over tests/ (decorator-call string literals, not grep — parsers.parse() sentences do not grep): 20 step registrations mention suggestion. The exact sentence the error should include a "suggestion" field is bound in exactly two places, both as named:

  • - tests/bdd/steps/generic/then_error.py:559 → then_error_has_suggestion
  • - tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:1743 → then_error_has_suggestion

    • But this pair is not a coin-flip, and the real shadowing in this family is a third binding.

    Measured, via a pytest_bdd_before_step probe recording step_func.__module__:

    runusages hitresolved to
    test_uc019_query_media_buys.py (3 error scenarios × 3 transports)6/6domain.uc019_query_media_buys.then_error_has_suggestion
    test_uc005… + test_uc018… (full)24/24generic.then_error.then_error_has_suggestion

    Deterministic, and deliberately so. uc019_query_media_buys is absent from tests/bdd/conftest.py:57 pytest_plugins; tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:14 star-imports it at module scope with a comment saying exactly why ("Importing here keeps those overrides scoped to UC-019 scenarios only"). pytest-bdd 8.1 scenario.py:144 sorts matching step fixturedefs by iterparentnodeids(fixturedef.baseid), so a plugin-level def (baseid "") sorts before a module-level def and the module wins.

    The genuinely dead binding is the second decorator on the generic function — then_error.py:560, @then('the error should include "suggestion" field') (no a). It is shadowed by a parsers step defined *later in the same module*:

    then_error.py:862  @then(parsers.parse('the error should include "{field}" field'))
then_error.py:863  def then_error_includes_field(ctx: dict, field: str) -> None:

    Measured: 63/63 occurrences of the no-a spelling in test_uc003… + test_uc004… resolved to then_error_includes_field, zero to then_error_has_suggestion. That spelling has 138 wired usages repo-wide. The a-form does not match that parser (" must follow include , and a intervenes), which is why UC-005's a-form reached then_error_has_suggestion.

    1. What each body actually does

    generic/then_error.py:561 — reads _wire_suggestion(ctx), which is extract_wire_suggestion(wire_error_envelope_or_none(ctx)): errors[0]["suggestion"] or adcp_error["suggestion"], top-level only. If that is not None, asserts truthiness and returns. Otherwise falls back to ctx["error"] via _get_error_dict and asserts the key "suggestion" is present and truthy. Two regions, one body, silently.

    domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py:1744 — one call to _current_suggestion(ctx) (:1660), which reads the same _wire_suggestion, then assert suggestion is not None ("no wire envelope was captured"), then assert isinstance(suggestion, str) and suggestion.strip(). No fallback — its docstring still says "typed fallback", which is stale.

    Behavioural delta, exhaustively: (a) no wire envelope → generic falls back to a reconstructed dict, uc019 fails loudly; (b) whitespace-only suggestion → generic passes, uc019 fails; (c) non-str truthy → generic passes, uc019 fails.

    2. Why they diverged

    Generic came first: then_error.py created 7f0d45a4e 2026-03-19 (#1146). The uc019 copy arrived 61f6ad9a2 2026-05-26 (#1335), and its original body was a different claim entirely:

    assert isinstance(error, AdCPError), ...
suggestion = error.details.get("suggestion")     # buried in details

    #1417 (2026-07-14) declared a details-buried suggestion a conformance bug and rewrote both to be wire-first. They converged on mechanism and never converged on strictness — the classic outcome when the sentence does not say which region it means.

    3. What the sentence should assert

    Measurement first. AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion is a read-only derived property:

    @property
def suggestion(self) -> str:
    """The pinned correction hint for this code, derived like ``message``."""
    return CODE_TABLE[self._error_code].suggestion

    All 100 CODE_TABLE rows have a non-empty suggestion (99 distinct); there is no setter and no constructor parameter. build_two_layer_error_envelope passes it into adcp_error(...) and mirrors it onto both layers. Every suggestion observed across 30 probed dispatches was a table constant — 'check request parameters and fix' (INVALID_REQUEST), 'provide credentials via the auth header and retry' (AUTH_MISSING).

    The step is live, not dead. Break-it probe: blanking the property (AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion = property(lambda self: "")) turned all 12 probed scenarios red — 6 UC-019 through the uc019 body, 6 UC-005 through the generic body. So it grades real carriage.

    But "non-empty" cannot fail. Given an error emitted through the boundary at all, the assertion is a pure function of the code, and 43 of the 55 a-form usages pin that code on the line directly above. The step restates what the scenario already named.

    And it is not a spec obligation. Pinned adcp/_schemas/3.1/core/error.json: required: ["code", "message"]. suggestion is optional. This is a seller policy, ungraded by the storyboard.

    The observable restatement, following the the response is compliant with the <tool> spec precedent — resolve from what the scenario names rather than restate it:

    > The buyer's wire error object carries, at the protocol top level (not in details), a non-empty suggestion equal to the hint CODE_TABLE pins for the code on that same wire object.

    Left side is the buyer's bytes; right side is production's table. What sits between them — the boundary translator, adcp_error(), and this transport's framing — is what gets graded. It catches: dropped, blanked, whitespace, details-buried, truncated, and *a hint belonging to a neighbouring code* (which is worse than none: it tells the buyer to fix the wrong thing). It is strictly stronger than the current assertion and it is not a tautology.

    4. Which scenarios use it, and where the meaning breaks

    55 uses of the a-form, 52 in wired feature files:

    featureuseswired
    BR-UC-006-sync-creatives21yes
    BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats14yes
    BR-UC-019-query-media-buys8yes
    BR-UC-018-list-creatives6yes
    BR-UC-003-update-media-buy3yes
    BR-UC-016-sync-audiences2no
    BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory1no

    43 sit in scenarios that also pin the error code; the proposed meaning holds for all 43 unchanged (it reads the code off the wire, so it does not require the scenario to name it).

    The sentence is doing two jobs. Four usages are not on the error path at all — they are 200 responses carrying response.errors[]:

  • - BR-UC-019:120 ext-b principal_id_missing — Then the response is compliant with the get_media_buys spec / And the response should include an empty media_buys array / And the response errors array should include error code "principal_id_missing"
  • - BR-UC-019:132 ext-c principal_not_found — same shape
  • - BR-UC-019:1184 inv-293-5 — "empty media_buys with no DB query"
  • - BR-UC-019:1223 inv-294-3 — an errors[] entry with SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE (the feature's own comment marks this scenario dormant)

    • There is no wire *error* envelope in any of them. ext-b and ext-c are xfailed today ("UC-019 spec-production gap"), so the second job is invisible there — but it is not hypothetical: the UC-003/004/011/026 probe recorded 3 entries into the generic body's fallback branch, all error_type=Error (adcp.types.Error), from test_uc004_deliver_media_buy_metrics.py::test_adapter_error__ad_server_unavailable on all three transports. A live partial-success population is riding the fallback.

    That is the split, and the repo already has correctly-named sentences for that region: each error should include "suggestion" field (generic/then_media_buy.py:696, reads ctx["error_response"].errors) and the per-account error with code "{code}" carries a non-empty suggestion (domain/uc011_accounts.py:1507).

    5. Recommendation

    One sentence, one body, in the generic module — plus a scenario repoint and a dead-decorator deletion. Four parts:

    1. Delete uc019_query_media_buys.then_error_has_suggestion (:1743) and _current_suggestion (:1660) if nothing else calls it. UC-019 has no use case its own; its extra strictness (.strip(), isinstance) belongs in the generic body, where it protects all 52 usages instead of 8.

    2. Delete the dead decorator then_error.py:560. Leaving it there is a claim about 138 wired scenarios that is false — then_error_includes_field grades them. (Alternatively, retire the a-form onto the parser step and delete then_error_has_suggestion outright; that step already enforces the top-level position via _ERROR_JSON_TOP_LEVEL_FIELDS. That is the smaller diff and I would take it if the code-match below is judged out of scope.)

    3. Repoint the four partial-success scenarios (BR-UC-019 ext-b, ext-c, inv-293-5, inv-294-3) onto a region-naming sentence — the response errors entry for code "<code>" carries a non-empty suggestion — modelled on uc011_accounts.py:1507. A sentence that means "wire error envelope" cannot also mean "an entry in a 200 response".

    4. Rewrite the surviving body to the code-resolved assertion.

    The body I would write (not applied):

    @then('the error should include a "suggestion" field')
def then_error_has_suggestion(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """The buyer received THIS code's correction hint, at the protocol position.

    ``suggestion`` is OPTIONAL in AdCP 3.1.1 core/error.json (required: code,
    message), so this grades a seller policy, not the spec. "Non-empty" alone
    could not fail: ``AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion`` is a read-only property
    derived from the code and all 100 CODE_TABLE rows are non-empty. So the
    expected text is RESOLVED from the code on the wire rather than restated.
    What sits between the two sides -- the boundary translator, ``adcp_error()``
    and this transport's framing -- is what this actually grades: a dropped,
    blanked, truncated, details-buried, or wrong-code hint each fail here.

    Wire-only. There is no reconstructed fallback: IMPL is sunsetted (#1417) and
    a hint rebuilt from a caught exception is not what the buyer received. A
    ``response.errors[]`` entry on a 200 is a DIFFERENT region -- use
    'the response errors entry for code "<code>" carries a non-empty suggestion'.
    """
    from src.core.exceptions import CODE_TABLE
    from tests.helpers import locate_envelope_error

    # Loud accessor: a dispatch that captured no error envelope raises here rather
    # than silently asserting nothing (tests/CLAUDE.md, Error Verification Policy).
    error_object = locate_envelope_error(wire_error_dict(ctx)) or {}
    code = error_object.get("code")
    suggestion = error_object.get("suggestion")

    assert isinstance(suggestion, str) and suggestion.strip(), (
        f"Expected a non-empty top-level 'suggestion' on the wire error object for code "
        f"{code!r}. error.json puts it beside code/message; a hint in 'details' is a "
        f"conformance bug, not a pass (#1417). Keys present: {sorted(error_object)}"
    )

    row = CODE_TABLE.get(code)
    if row is None:
        # Open vocabulary: core/error.json types `code` as a wire string and the
        # published enum is documentary, so a code outside the table legitimately
        # reaches the buyer and has no pinned hint to match against.
        return
    assert suggestion == row.suggestion, (
        f"Wire suggestion for code {code!r} is not the hint pinned for that code. "
        f"expected={row.suggestion!r} got={suggestion!r}. A hint belonging to a "
        f"different code is worse than none -- it tells the buyer to fix the wrong thing."
    )

    Verification for whoever applies it: re-run the break-it probe (blank AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion) and confirm red, then swap one code's row onto another and confirm red on the new arm — a passing guard is not a grading guard.

    Verified against the worktree (no edits made; the only untracked paths, docs/reports/ and scripts/audit/build_bdd_findings_report.py, were there before I started). I ran three probe sessions against agent-pg-sa-impl-kill to settle the claims the report asserted but did not measure.

    Verdict: AGREE on the consolidation, OBJECT to the CODE_TABLE equality arm

    1. Body descriptions — accurate, with two nits

    Both readings are correct. generic/then_error.py:561 does exactly what the report says: _wire_suggestion → extract_wire_suggestion(envelope) = errors[0]["suggestion"] or adcp_error["suggestion"] (tests/harness/transport.py:93), then truthiness; otherwise _get_error_dict(ctx["error"]) and key-presence. uc019_query_media_buys.py:1744 → _current_suggestion (:1660) hard-asserts is not None, isinstance(str), .strip(), no fallback. The three behavioural deltas (no envelope / whitespace / non-str) are right.

    Nits:

  • - The uc019 binding is @then(parsers.parse('the error should include a "suggestion" field')), not a bare @then(...). Immaterial to matching, but the report describes both as bare.
  • - "its docstring still says 'typed fallback', which is stale" — true, and it is stale in two places: the step docstring at :1746 and _current_suggestion's own at :1661.

    • Resolution mechanics check out. find_fixturedefs_for_step (.venv/.../pytest_bdd/scenario.py:53) sorts by iterparentnodeids(baseid) and getfixturevalue takes the last, so a module-level def beats a plugin-level one — which is why the star-import at tests/bdd/test_uc019_query_media_buys.py:14 wins.

    2. The dead-decorator claim — CONFIRMED by measurement

    pytest_bdd_before_step probe on test_uc003_update_media_buy.py (159 passed, 48 deselected, 1203 xfailed):

    'the error should include "suggestion" field'  =>  generic.then_error.then_error_includes_field : 51

    51/51. Zero to then_error_has_suggestion. then_error.py:560 is dead, exactly as claimed.

    3. The fallback evidence is MIS-ATTRIBUTED — but the conclusion survives

    BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature contains zero a-form usages — 10 no-a usages and one the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue. Those 3 fallback entries therefore cannot have come from then_error_has_suggestion; they came from then_error_includes_field (or then_error_has_fix_suggestion). The report's only evidence that the a-form's fallback is live is evidence about a different step.

    I measured the real number. Probe wrapping _wire_suggestion, run over test_uc005_discover_creative_formats.py + test_uc018_list_creatives.py (5 failed, 480 passed, 118 deselected, 642 xfailed — the 5 failures are sandbox/asset-types scenarios, unrelated, and pre-existing on this branch):

    5. Weakening walk — no weakening found

  • - T-UC-019-ext-d (invalid parameter types; a2a + mcp; graduated, green). Before: envelope required, isinstance(str), .strip(). After: wire_error_dict (loud), non-empty str, plus code match. Stronger. Caveat below.
  • - UC-005 error scenarios (the measured 24). Before: errors[0] or adcp_error suggestion, truthy. After: errors[0] only, non-empty str, code match. Stronger on value; a whitespace or non-str value that passes today would fail.
  • - T-UC-019-inv-151-4 (xfailed in _UC019_XFAIL_TAGS). No change either way.
  • - UC-006's 21 / UC-003's 3. No change — they never run.

    6. Objections

    (a) The CODE_TABLE equality arm buys almost nothing and imports production into the oracle.

    Verified: CODE_TABLE has 100 rows, all suggestions non-empty, 99 distinct; AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion is a read-only property at src/core/exceptions.py:405-407; build_two_layer_error_envelope passes suggestion=exc.suggestion at :1173 while the code on the same object comes from the same exc. So the two sides of the proposed equality originate from one expression. It can only fail if a transport rewrites one field and not the other — and truncation/blanking is already caught by the non-empty arm it sits beside. Meanwhile a table edit moves both sides together, so it can never detect a wrong hint.

    This is also in tension with a precedent the repo already set, in tests/bdd/conftest.py around the T-UC-019-ext-e graduation note: a scenario demanding specific MESSAGE text was removed as "an authored sentence that cannot exist, since AdCPSalesAgentError.message is a read-only property returning CODE_TABLE[code].message … Removing that tautology is what un-xfailed it." suggestion is the same kind of property. Adding an assertion against CODE_TABLE re-imports the thing that was just removed, with the comparison inverted.

    What I would do instead: take the report's own smaller alternative. Delete then_error_has_suggestion entirely and let both spellings land on then_error_includes_field (:862), adding the a-form as a second decorator or a parser tweak. That step already enforces the top-level position via _ERROR_JSON_TOP_LEVEL_FIELDS (which contains suggestion), already reads through _wire_error_object → locate_envelope_error, and already serves 517 no-a usages. One binding, one body, no production import, and the measured 24 lose nothing. If UC-019's .strip() / isinstance(str) strictness is wanted, add it there — it then protects all ~570 usages instead of 24.

    (b) The region change is real and unmeasured. The proposed body reads through wire_error_dict + locate_envelope_error, which is errors[0] only (tests/helpers/envelope_assertions.py:38-61). extract_wire_suggestion is errors[0] or adcp_error. Any envelope populating only the envelope-level mirror flips from green to red. My 24 measurements can't distinguish (the or prefers errors[0]). This is a defensible strengthening, but it should be measured — probe locate_envelope_error(envelope) is None across the 24 — before it is applied, not assumed. The report presents it as incidental.

    (c) The sibling shadow is missed, and it keeps _current_suggestion alive. _current_suggestion has two callers, not one: :1750 (then_error_has_suggestion) and :2247 (then_error_suggestion_for_fix, binding the error should include a suggestion for how to fix the issue) — which shadows generic then_error_has_fix_suggestion at then_error.py:580 with the same wire-strict-vs-fallback divergence. Recommendation #1 hedges with "if nothing else calls it," but does not notice the sibling. Fixing one sentence and leaving the twin one line over means the "one sentence, one body" claim is half-delivered, and _current_suggestion survives to keep the divergence alive.

    (d) Proposal #3 (repoint the four partial-success scenarios) is correct but should not ride along. Verified: ext-b (:126), ext-c (:139), inv-293-5 (:1190), inv-294-3 (:1233) are all 200-response errors[] scenarios with no wire error envelope, and the repo already has region-correct sentences (generic/then_media_buy.py:697, domain/uc011_accounts.py:1507). But under today's uc019 body all four already fail hard; ext-b/ext-c are xfailed under _UC019_XFAIL_TAGS for an unrelated reason ("error code mismatches / not implemented") and inv-294-3 is dormant on a missing Given. So repointing changes no currently-green result, and editing a generated feature carries the upstream-mirroring obligation from the xpass-graduation workflow. Separate change.

    7. What I would apply

    1. Delete uc019_query_media_buys.py:1744 then_error_has_suggestion and :2239 then_error_suggestion_for_fix and _current_suggestion (:1660) — the whole cluster, not one third of it.

    2. Delete the dead decorator then_error.py:560, and retire the a-form onto then_error_includes_field, deleting then_error_has_suggestion. Move UC-019's isinstance(str) + .strip() strictness into that one body.

    3. Skip the CODE_TABLE equality arm.

    4. Before applying, measure locate_envelope_error(envelope) is None across the 24 live dispatches so the errors[0]-only tightening is a known change rather than a surprise.

    5. File the four UC-019 partial-success repoints separately.

    The report's verification advice is sound and I would keep it: blank AdCPSalesAgentError.suggestion and confirm red before believing any of this grades.

    The When primitive (2649 lines)

    1. Corpus totals (re-measured)

    2. Payload proportions — design vs. measured

    Classes disjoint, precedence table > outline placeholder > inline scalar > none.

    classdesignmeasuredlines

    inline scalar30%33.7%898

    docstring body—0%0

    Verdict: the proportions hold, and every miss is in the same direction — the design rounds down its own problem. Payload-in-the-When is 49.8%, not the 45% the design's three numbers sum to. The data-table class is 40% larger than stated (112 lines, not ~84).

    classexecutesdead% dead

    Inside the executing half, 66% of When lines are bare. Inside the dead half, only 38%. The design found this correlation for the Given corpus; it holds at least as sharply for When payloads.     4. The 4.2% data-table cases (112 lines)

    30 distinct sentences, 66 distinct table bodies. 55 of 112 lines (49%) repeat a table byte-for-byte.

    sync_accounts — 47 lines, BR-UC-011 (loaded). MOVE.

    43 of 47 tables have exactly one body row. 41 of 47 share the header brand.domain | operator | billing. One table body appears 31 times:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:246
    When the Buyer Agent sends a sync_accounts request with:
    | brand.domain    | operator        | billing  |
    | acme-corp.com   | acme-corp.com   | operator |

    identical at :264, :295, :319, :328, :345, and 25 more. The delta case retypes the whole thing to change one cell:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:308
    When the Buyer Agent sends a sync_accounts request with:
    | brand.domain    | operator      | billing |
    | acme-corp.com   | acme-corp.com | agent   |

    And the corpus already contains the right answer, spelled the long way:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:388
    When the Buyer Agent sends a sync_accounts request with:
    | brand.domain    | operator      | billing   |
    | acme-corp.com   | acme-corp.com | <billing> |

    That is baseline-plus-one-overridden-field — but with the baseline retyped instead of named.

    The cost is visible in the step file. Five @when step functions all funnel into one call:

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc011_accounts.py:1263  @when("...sync_accounts request with no idempotency_key and:")
tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc011_accounts.py:1274  @when("...sync_accounts request with:")
tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc011_accounts.py:1280  @when(parsers.re(r'...with idempotency_key "(?P<key>[^"]+)" and:'))
tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc011_accounts.py:1293  @when(parsers.re(r'...carrying idempotency_key "(?P<key>[^"]+)" and:'))

    each body _dispatch_sync_table(ctx, datatable, ...) differing by one kwarg. Five sentences, one dispatch. dry_run, delete_missing and idempotency_key are AdCP request fields by the design's own M-rule, so putting them in the verb phrase is what multiplied the sentence.

    Exception inside this family: 4 tables have 2 body rows (:280, :427) and are grading array semantics — the row count is the assertion. Those keep a table, but it belongs to a Given (Given a sync_accounts request with accounts: <table>), leaving the When bare.

    get_products — 35 lines, BR-UC-001 (never loaded). MOVE.

    All 35 use the identical header | field | value |. buying_mode in 35/35, brief in 26, brand in 18. Two bodies cover 12 of the 35:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory.feature:36
    When the Buyer Agent sends a get_products request with:
    | field        | value                               |
    | buying_mode  | brief                               |
    | brief        | Display ads for tech audience Q4     |
    | brand        | {"domain": "acme.com"}              |

    (x5 verbatim: :36, :1410, :1430, :1475, :1584; a wholesale variant x7)

    This is the purest case. The | field | value | header is baseline-plus-delta — it just has no baseline name, so the baseline gets retyped 35 times and {"domain": "acme.com"} is a JSON literal in a table cell, the exact thing the target shape exists to avoid. grep -rn "sends a get_products request with" tests/bdd/steps/ returns nothing: no step definition exists, so all 35 are inert.

    sync_catalogs — 19 lines, BR-UC-023 (never loaded). MOVE 13, keep 6.

    13 of 19 have one body row. The header drifts across seven shapes for one entity: catalog_id|type, +name, +url+items, +url+feed_format, feed_field|value|catalog_field, feed_field|catalog_field|asset_group_id, gtins. One-row cases move. The genuinely multi-row ones (When the Buyer Agent syncs catalogs of mixed types:, :108, :82) keep the table, in a Given.

    Admin form — 5 lines, BR-ADMIN-ACCOUNTS. KEEP.

    tests/bdd/features/BR-ADMIN-ACCOUNTS.feature  When the admin submits the create account form with:   (x4)
                                              When the admin submits the edit form with:             (x1)

    This is one of the design's three genuine non-dispatch events — a Flask test client, no AdCP request model to baseline against, and the form's field set is itself what's under test. The table stays in the When.

    5. The 33.7% inline-scalar cases (898 lines)

    898 lines → 740 distinct verbatim → 477 distinct after normalizing quoted values, numbers, arrays and objects. Normalizing collapses only 35%, and 369 of the 477 normalized shapes (77%) are used exactly once.

    That is the finding. The variety is not values — it is the field set. Each new combination of payload fields mints a new English sentence and a new step regex.

    list_tasks: 44 lines, 24 distinct shapes.

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:770   When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks with filter protocol "media-buy"
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:811   When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks with filter protocols "media-buy,signals,governance"
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:654   When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks with filter protocols []
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:990   When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks with filter statuses []
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:665   When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks with filter task_ids containing 101 items
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:40    When the Buyer Agent invokes list_tasks via MCP with no filters

    protocol vs protocols, scalar vs comma-string vs array — three sentences for one field, plus a transport named in the sentence.

    get_creative_delivery: 38 lines, 14 shapes, three of which are one field away from each other:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-022-creative-delivery-features.feature:421
    When the Buyer Agent invokes get_creative_delivery with only optional params start_date "2026-01-01"
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-022-creative-delivery-features.feature:525
    When the Buyer Agent invokes get_creative_delivery with media_buy_ids ["mb-032"] without max_variants

    "with only optional params" and "without max_variants" are English descriptions of an override set. That is a table column written as a clause.

    Some inline scalars are a JSON body inside the sentence outright:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:290
    When the Buyer Agent requests formats with output_format_ids filter [{"agent_url": "https://creatives.adcontextprotocol.org", "id": "display_static"}]
tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature:572
    When the Buyer Agent requests delivery metrics for "mb-001" with reporting_dimensions {"device_type": {}}

    Verdict: MOVE, with one qualification. The high-frequency identifier sentences look like a counterexample — When the Buyer Agent sends a get_media_buys request for media_buy_ids ["mb-001"] appears 49 times verbatim (BR-UC-019:178), and for a read tool the identifier is arguably the whole payload. But 49 verbatim repeats is the definition of a baseline, not a delta. It should be named once and fired bare, with the id in the Examples column on the handful of scenarios that vary it.

    The identity override does stay a modifier of the dispatch (design M1), but it needs one spelling; today it is fused into the payload sentence: When the Buyer Agent authenticated as "buyer-001" sends a list_creatives request (BR-UC-018:520).

    Transport must not stay: <transport> appears in 18 When lines and via MCP/A2A/REST in 32 — BR-UC-013:127, BR-UC-015:84, BR-UC-020:27, BR-UC-010:545. Per this repo's rule that belongs to the parametrization.

    6. The 11.9% placeholder cases — the shape is half-right already

    318 lines, 233 distinct, and 77 different placeholder names for the same idea: <boundary_point> 76, <partition> 44, <request_params> 18, <value> 15, <setup> 13, <input> 11, <filter_config> 6, <event_config> 6, <action> 4, <sync_config> 2, <test_config> 2 …

    And the column carries English, not a field/value pair:

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-018-list-creatives.feature:166
    When the Buyer Agent sends a list_creatives request with <request_params>
    ...
      | request_params                      |
      | no parameters                       |
      | statuses filter ["approved"]        |
      | statuses filter as empty array      |
      | empty filters object                |

    statuses filter as empty array and statuses filter [] (BR-UC-027:990) are the same value spelled two ways. So even the class that has the right *structure* still needs a natural-language parser inside the step.

    7. Where the design's Given estimate is wrong

    Same failure mode as the P01 miss the brief flags. The design names P14 as the request field X is Y at 246 lines. That sentence does not exist in the corpus — 0 lines match. The field-delta is spelled as a | field | value | table hung off a valid X request with: instead. Current counts: a valid create_media_buy request* 161, a valid update_media_buy request* 153, the request includes* 105, a valid … request (all tools) 329. Given steps carrying a data table: 254, already 2.3× the When side.

    8. Answer to the question asked

    The payload belongs in a Given/factory for ~110 of the 112 table lines and for essentially all 898 inline-scalar lines. It stays in the When only for the admin-form steps (5 lines, not a dispatch) and, as a call modifier rather than payload, for the identity override.

    The target shape is already built on both halves and just isn't wired:

  • - The baseline exists. tests/bdd/steps/generic/_create_request.py:26 — build_create_request_kwargs(...), whose own docstring calls it "The single base-request literal."
  • - The table reader exists. tests/bdd/steps/generic/_table.py:32 — rows(datatable) → list[dict], generic.
  • - The Gherkin form exists, in one file:
    • tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-002-media-buy-status-dual-emit.feature:70-73
    And a valid update_media_buy request with:
    | field        | value       |
    | media_buy_id | mb_existing |
    And the request includes 1 package update with:
    | field      | value   |
    | package_id | pkg_001 |
    | budget     | 5000    |
    When the Buyer Agent sends the update_media_buy request

    That is baseline + delta in the Given and a bare When. It is what 199 sends the create_media_buy request and 174 sends the update_media_buy request lines already do (160 and 139 distinct Given blocks behind them respectively) — payload variety in the Given, one sentence in the When. 79% of bare When lines sit in a scenario whose Given names a request; 86% have some payload-shaped Given.

    The one thing missing from that form is the name. a valid update_media_buy request with: has an implicit baseline nobody can refer to, which is why get_products retypes buying_mode+brand 35 times and sync_accounts retypes one table 31 times. Naming the baseline is the whole remaining delta between the corpus and the design's target.

    I have the full measurement. Writing the report.

    When-lines that are Then-obligations: full ledger

    Worktree ., branch exec/itsii-1-drop-impl. Nothing modified. Every count below is from an AST/parser pass over tests/bdd/features/*.feature and tests/bdd/steps/**/*.py, binding each feature line to a step definition with pytest-bdd's own parsers objects (is_matching), not by eye.

    1. The design's numbers, re-measured

    Design claimMeasuredVerdict
    2649 When lines2649 literal When; 2666 counting the 17 And/But that inherit Whenconfirmed
    1365 distinct1380off by 15
    1133 normalized1139off by 6
    90.8% is one operation2411 / 2666 = 90.4% after removing 174 obligations and 81 genuine non-dispatch eventsholds

    The verb histogram is right where it is quoted and incomplete where it stops. Head-verb counts over the 2666-line When corpus:

    sends 1204   invokes 391   syncs 194   requests 183   calls 124
validates 74   submits 70   logs 57   queries 19   attempts 9   posts 2

    validates (74) and logs (57) are larger than five of the twelve the design names, and the design hides them behind "(+2 more)". That matters for this report specifically: validates is not a thirteenth dispatch verb, it is the single largest obligation verb. Sixty-six of those 74 lines are the system validates … / the seller validates …, which is the category below.

    2. The category, measured

    174 When lines (6.5% of 2666) are Then-obligations phrased as actions. The design said 234; 234 is very close to a different number I also measured — the count of obligation-shaped When lines sitting in feature files that have no test module at all (146 by my families, 234 by a looser regex) — so the design likely conflated "obligation" with "obligation in a dormant file."

    Three families:

    FamilyCountHave a @when
    O1 — the SUT is the grammatical subject (the system validates …, the seller assembles …)715
    O2 — a response/artifact is the subject (the build_creative response is received, the boundary case <X> is evaluated)891
    O3 — bare passive, no actor (format compatibility is checked)147
    total17413

    Excluded on purpose, as genuine events rather than obligations: 81 lines that are real seller-initiated outbound work — the Seller Agent calls check_governance outbound … (UC-030, 21 lines), the Seller invokes calibrate_content … (UC-024), the system delivers a webhook report … (UC-004), the governance agent posts the collection_list_changed webhook. Those are the design's third bucket and they are correctly Whens.

    3. The bigger fact underneath: 161 of the 174 execute nothing

    Only 13 of the 174 bind to a step definition. The other 161 do not, and 146 of those sit in one of the 18 feature files that no test module ever loads:

    BR-UC-001, 008, 012, 013, 014, 015, 016, 017, 020, 021,
022, 023, 024, 025, 027, 028, 030, 032   (18 of 46 .feature files)

    Corpus-wide, this is not confined to my category: 1636 of 2666 When lines bind to no step definition. Only 1030 do. So for most of these sentences the question "what does its step definition do" has the answer *there isn't one* — the sentence is prose in a file nothing reads.

    Fifteen unbound obligations *are* in collected features. Ten are UC-004 and are ledgered dormant in tests/bdd/dormant_scenarios.txt (T-UC-004-boundary-media-buy-status, -sandbox, -package-commercial, -metric-aggregates, -missing-metrics, -measurement-window, -aggregated-reach, -delayed-data, T-UC-004-envelope-two-layer, T-UC-004-v31-forecastable-metric-vocab) — they xfail invisibly.

    Side-finding worth a ticket. Five UC-002 obligations are in a collected feature, unbound, and not in the ledger. grep -c "T-UC-002" tests/bdd/dormant_scenarios.txt returns 0. _record_dormancy (conftest.py:~1740) says: *"Returns True when the scenario is an already-recorded dormant entry (caller converts it to xfail), False when it is new (caller leaves it FAILING)."* By source, @T-UC-002-inv-017-4, @T-UC-002-inv-018-8 and @T-UC-002-v31-submitted-envelope-message should be RED today. (@T-UC-002-alt-manual-reject is rescued by a hand-written xfail at conftest.py:1923; its -override twin is not.) I could not run the suite to confirm.

    4. The 13 that execute — line by line

    4a. Vacuous pass, confirmed

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature:541

      @T-UC-004-webhook-no-config @alternative @webhook @edge-case
  Scenario: Webhook fires for media buy without webhook configuration
    Given a media buy "mb-001" without a reporting_webhook configured
    When the webhook scheduler evaluates "mb-001"
    Then the system should skip "mb-001" (no webhook to deliver to)
    And no delivery attempt should be made

    Step definition, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc004_delivery.py:1099:

    @when(parsers.parse('the webhook scheduler evaluates "{mb_id}"'))
def when_webhook_evaluates(ctx: dict, mb_id: str) -> None:
    """Webhook scheduler evaluates a media buy for delivery."""
    wh = ctx.get("webhook_config", {}).get(mb_id, {})
    if not wh.get("active"):
        ctx["webhook_skipped"] = True
    else:
        ctx["webhook_evaluated"] = mb_id

    This touches no production code. It reads a dict a Given put on the context and reimplements the scheduler's decision in the test. Neither webhook_skipped nor webhook_evaluated is read by any Then (grep finds them only at their two assignment sites). The two Thens read the real origin instead:

  • - then_skip_no_webhook (:2408) — posted_mb_ids = [req.json().get("media_buy_id") for req in _webhook_deliveries(ctx)], and _webhook_deliveries returns ctx["env"].delivered_requests.
  • - then_no_delivery_attempt (:2424) — assert env.delivery_attempts == 0.

    • Because the When dispatched nothing, both lists are empty by construction. Both assertions pass no matter what production does. The scenario was trying to assert "a media buy with no reporting_webhook produces no POST"; it currently asserts "a step that makes no calls made no calls."

    Recommend: REWRITE as a dispatch — the When must run the real scheduler over the seeded buy so delivery_attempts can be non-zero when production regresses.

    4b. A unit test wearing Gherkin, self-documented

    BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature:970 and :986

        When the webhook is configured with credentials "<partition>"
    When the webhook credentials are at boundary "<boundary_point>"

    uc004_delivery.py:1226-1237 both route to _validate_reporting_webhook_credentials, whose docstring at :3382 says it outright:

    > FIXME(#2109): this grades a Pydantic constructor in the test process, not production — nothing is dispatched, so the a2a/mcp/rest axis carries no information for the 14 auth-scheme and credential rows that route here.

    and:

    > Note the graduation note in conftest.py for these two tags says they pass "on all transports" — they do, but only because this constructor check is transport-independent. That is the gap #2109 names, not evidence of coverage.

    The scenario was trying to assert that a webhook credential shorter than 32 chars is rejected at the create_media_buy wire boundary. It asserts that pydantic enforces MinLen=32 in the test process. 42 tests (14 rows × 3 transports).

    Recommend: REWRITE as a dispatch. The docstring already documents that the naive migration goes red and why (wrong ENV_ROUTES verb) — this is a scenario-routing change, not a sentence change alone.

    4c. An assertion the author labelled as one, in a comment

    BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:1212

      Scenario: Baseline list_creative_formats response carries format_id objects with agent_url and id
    Given the Buyer Agent calls list_creative_formats without filters
    When the response returns a non-empty formats array
    Then the response is compliant with the list_creative_formats spec
    And every entry's format_id should be an object carrying both agent_url and id

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc005_format_id_shape.py:39:

    @when("the response returns a non-empty formats array")
def when_response_returns_non_empty_formats(ctx: dict) -> None:
    # Precondition guard (phrased as a When by the storyboard): the Given's
    # dispatch must have returned a non-empty formats array before shape checks.
    assert len(require_payload(ctx).formats) >= 1

    The dispatch is in the Given; the When is a bare assert. The author wrote "phrased as a When by the storyboard" rather than fix it.

    Recommend: MOVE to Then. One line: Then the formats array should not be empty, and promote Given the Buyer Agent calls list_creative_formats without filters to the When.

    4d. Same operation, two sentences, in the same Rule

    BR-UC-006-sync-creatives.feature:632 and :663 — When format compatibility is checked, against :640, :655, :671 in the same BR-RULE-039 block — When the Buyer Agent syncs the creative / … with assignments. uc006_sync_creatives.py:3919 admits it:

    > """Dispatch sync_creatives — the format compatibility check happens inside.

    > This is the same as 'the Buyer Agent syncs the creative' but named for scenarios that focus on the format check behavior."""

    This is the clean proof of the twelve-verbs thesis: not two verbs across two files, but two sentences for the identical dispatch eight lines apart under one rule, differing only in what the author was thinking about.

    Recommend: REWRITE — collapse into the Buyer Agent syncs the creative. "Which behaviour we are focused on" belongs in the Scenario name and the Then, never in the dispatch sentence.

    4e. Correctly a When, wrongly phrased (keep, rename)

  • - BR-UC-019-query-media-buys.feature:960, :980 — And one successful update_media_buy lands between t1 and t2. uc019_query_media_buys.py:2758 lands a real state-changing repository write between the two reads, with a deliberate rationale ("the obligation graded here is the READ path… the write still goes through the production seam that owns both columns"). Genuinely a When; the passive voice hides the actor. KEEP, rename to name the writer.
  • - BR-UC-004:363, :382 — When the system evaluates the circuit breaker state. uc004_delivery.py:1013 calls env.drive_breaker_transition(endpoint_key) then env.call_send() — real clock/state advancement plus a real send, one of the three legitimate non-dispatch events. KEEP, rename (e.g. *the breaker's open window elapses and the seller retries*), because "evaluates the state" reads as an assertion.

    • 4f. A Given, not a When

    BR-UC-011-manage-accounts.feature:793 — And the request includes a push_notification_config with url "…", following a When … sends a sync_accounts request. tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_config.py:277 registers it under both keywords and the docstring is the whole argument for keyword discipline:

    > REGISTERED UNDER BOTH KEYWORDS, on purpose. Both feature lines that use this sentence write it as And, which inherits whichever keyword came before … A sentence whose keyword depends on its neighbour cannot be owned by one keyword.

    > The two copies were not equivalent, and the difference was a live defect: the @given one set push_notification_config (which the dispatch reads) AND the url; the @when one set only push_notification_url. Scenarios routed to the @when copy therefore dispatched with NO webhook config, while the Then step that checks "the system registered the webhook" fell back to the url key and passed anyway.

    An already-shipped, already-diagnosed silent pass caused by exactly this defect. Recommend: MOVE to Given (it is request setup) and reorder the scenario so the dispatch is last.

    5. The 161 that execute nothing — the shapes

    The worst single shape: 22 identical lines carrying zero information.

    BR-UC-028-manage-collection-lists.feature:503, 517, 534, 548, 561, 576, 590, 603, 615, 626, 641, 654, 666, 679, 694, 707, 720, 736, 749, 763, 776, 790

      @T-UC-028-bva-idempotency-format @boundary @bva @create @idempotency
  Scenario Outline: idempotency_key format boundary -- <boundary_point>
    Given a create_collection_list request is prepared for the idempotency_key boundary
    When the boundary case <boundary_point> is evaluated
    Then the outcome is <expected>
    Examples:
      | boundary_point           | expected                  |
      | 16-char alphanumeric key | accepted                  |
      | 15-char key              | rejected: INVALID_REQUEST |

    Nothing in the Gherkin says what operation runs. Both the When and the Then are placeholders; the entire test is free-text English in two table columns. The failure mode this enables is visible one scenario down, at :517 (@T-UC-028-bva-idempotency-policy), whose boundary_point rows read selection_type=publisher_collections with publisher_domain + collection_ids — selection-type cases pasted under an idempotency-policy scenario. Nobody caught it because the sentence has no content to contradict.

    Recommend: DELETE all 22 and rewrite the outlines with a real dispatch When and per-row parameters, not English labels.

    Second worst: 11× the build_creative response is received — BR-UC-020-build-creative.feature:590, 623, 633, 785, 813, 825, 844, 854, 864, 873, 882. Every one follows a Given that already produced the response (Given the creative agent successfully generates a creative). The line means "now look at it."

    Recommend: DELETE all 11. Same for its siblings in UC-020: the output creative_manifest is examined (:732), the provenance is examined (:750, :768), the asset's effective provenance is determined (:777).

    UC-024's partition outlines, 14 lines — the artifact is submitted for calibration (:724, :922), the records are submitted for validation (:747, :940), the verdict is evaluated (:804, :987), the artifacts are retrieved (:828, :1010), the validation is executed (:848, :865, :1025), the response is evaluated (:881), the summary is evaluated (:1053), the calibration request is processed (:903, :1070). Each sits between Given a <thing> conforming to <partition> partition and Then <outcome> — both parametrized. The When is a connector.

    Recommend: DELETE.

    UC-001's 41 lines are the clearest "unit test in Gherkin" without any step code to prove it — the shape is unmistakable in the feature alone:

    BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory.feature:583
    Given a product has a pricing_option with fixed_price set and floor_price null
    When the system validates the pricing option
    Then the pricing option is valid (fixed pricing model)

    There is no buyer, no tool, no request. The Given hands the system under test a bare object, the When names an internal function (validates, converts, applies AI ranking, resolves), and the Then reports its return value. If these were ever wired they could only be wired as a pytest.raises(ValidationError) around a constructor — precisely the FIXME(#2109) shape UC-004 already has and already regrets. The same holds for the system returns refinement_applied (:696–:731, 5×), the system validates the refine entry (:738–:1763, 5×), the system converts the product to AdCP schema (:637–:920, 4×), and the five the seller validates … lines at :1598–:1728.

    Recommend: REWRITE as When the Buyer Agent calls get_products … with the pricing option in the seeded product, so the Then can grade the wire. These invariants are gradeable — they are just phrased against an internal call.

    6. Recommendation totals

    DispositionLinesof which live today
    DELETE as vacuous — contentless connector; Given + Then already carry the whole test830
    REWRITE as a dispatch — a real operation hiding behind a verb that names an internal function436
    MOVE to Then — an assertion about the response, dispatch already elsewhere431
    KEEP as an action, rename to name the actor — real event, passive voice44
    MOVE to Given — request setup11
    17413

    7. What this says about the twelve verbs

    The twelve dispatch verbs are worth collapsing, but they are not the expensive problem. Only 13 of the 174 obligation lines execute at all, and 146 of them are in files nothing loads — so most of what looks like coverage in the corpus is text. Collapsing sends/invokes/calls/syncs/requests/submits/queries into one sentence is a mechanical win; the load-bearing decision in the same pass is that a When whose subject is the system under test is a Then, because that rule is what would have prevented the four defects that are already documented in this repo's own docstrings:

  • - the a2a/mcp/rest axis carrying no information for 42 credential tests (#2109),
  • - a webhook-skip scenario that passes because its When calls nothing (BR-UC-004:541),
  • - a scenario dispatching with no webhook config while its Then passed on a fallback key (given_config.py:277),
  • - a boundary outline holding the wrong Examples rows entirely (BR-UC-028:517).

    • The precedent for the fix is already in the tree: tests/bdd/conftest.py:1933 retires T-UC-002-nfr-006 with the reason *"original dispatch-in-Then scenario replaced by BR-UC-002-nfr-enforcement.feature with proper Given/When/Then structure."* Same disease, opposite direction, already treated once by rewriting the scenario rather than the step.

    Files referenced: tests/bdd/features/*.feature, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc004_delivery.py, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc005_format_id_shape.py, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py, tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc019_query_media_buys.py, tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_config.py, tests/bdd/conftest.py, tests/bdd/dormant_scenarios.txt.

    The twelve dispatch verbs: measurement and decision

    1. Corpus re-measurement (design's claims verified)

    ClaimDesignMeasuredVerdict

    Verb counts reproduce only as grep -cw line-hits, which double-count: sends a get_products request is counted under both sends and requests. Positional head-verb extraction gives the honest figure.

    verbdesign (grep -cw)head-verbnote

    Two corrections to the design's list. request is not a dispatch verb — zero When lines use it as one. And twelve undercounts the tail: there are 98 distinct head verbs, with validates (74) and logs (55) both larger than submits.

    2. The finding that reframes everything: 55.6% of the corpus never executes

    46 feature files exist; 28 are loaded by a scenarios() call, 18 are not bound to any test module at all — not dormant-xfailed, just unread text.

    When lines in bound features:   1176
When lines in UNBOUND features: 1473  (55.6%)

    This is where the verb diversity lives:

    verbboundunbound% unbound
    sends68851443%
    invokes338799.2%
    syncs1237137%
    requests17853%
    calls913226%
    submits56593%
    logs055100%
    validates27297%
    queries1900%

    invokes is the design's #2 verb and it grades three lines. Harmonizing it is a text edit with no test-behaviour risk.

    3. Body groups (AST-normalized, not verb-grouped)

    235 distinct @when functions across 251 decorators. Resolving each body transitively (including helpers imported inside function bodies) to whether it reaches the one dispatch seam — dispatch_request / dispatch_via_client / _call_via / _call, all of which funnel through env.call_via into _populate_ctx_from_result:

    WHEN functions:  SEAM 211  |  NO-SEAM 24
bound When LINES: SEAM 984  |  NO-SEAM 46      → 95.5% one seam

    Four groups, and they do not align with verbs:

    G1 — dispatch through the seam (984 lines, 95.5%). Contains sends, requests, syncs, calls, queries, invokes, creates, updates, validates, previews, retries, format. Every body reduces to dispatch_request(ctx, **payload).

    G2 — admin UI (15 lines). admin_accounts.py::when_navigate_*, when_submit_*, when_status_change. Flask test client, no AdCP wire. Verbs navigates, submits, sends.

    G3 — seller-initiated outbound webhook (19 lines). uc004_delivery.py::when_deliver_*, when_attempt_webhook, when_webhook_fires, when_evaluate_circuit_breaker. Verbs delivers, attempts, webhook, evaluates.

    G4 — off-wire in-process call (10 lines). uc002_task_query.py::when_query_task_list → _dispatch_list_tasks → asyncio.run(list_tasks(...)) directly against production, bypassing every transport. This is a dispatch wearing a different verb because it was never wired to the seam — a defect, not a category.

    Two design claims do not survive:

  • - M2 (verbatim body via _call_raw) is real but invisible in the verb. Inside when_request.py, requests covers RAW and TYPED indiscriminately: when_request_format_ids → _call_raw, when_request_min_width → _call, when_request_type_filter → _call, when_request_output_format_ids → _call_raw. Same verb, opposite dispatch mode.
  • - Clock advancement does not exist. Zero When lines mention a clock, elapsed time, or advancing time. The design names three genuine non-dispatch events; there are two.

    • 4. Do any two verbs differ in behaviour?

    No. Not one pair. Four independent proofs:

    (a) Three verbs, one tool, one funnel. BR-UC-010-discover-seller-capabilities.feature:

    92:    When the Buyer Agent calls get_adcp_capabilities
312:   When the Buyer Agent invokes get_adcp_capabilities via <channel>
522:   When the Buyer Agent sends a get_adcp_capabilities skill request via A2A with the token

    All three land in _call_capabilities (tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc010_capabilities.py:557), whose own docstring says *"Single funnel for every capabilities dispatch (DRY)."*

    (b) Two verbs, literally the same helper call. tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc004_delivery.py:

    1287: def when_query_single_mb(ctx: dict, mb_id: str) -> None:
1289:     ctx.setdefault("query_variant", True)
1290:     _request_single_mb(ctx, mb_id)

1131: def when_request_single_mb(ctx: dict, mb_id: str) -> None:
1133:     _request_single_mb(ctx, mb_id)

    Serving BR-UC-004:1093 When the Buyer Agent queries delivery metrics for media buy "mb-001" and BR-UC-004:686 When the Buyer Agent requests delivery metrics for "mb-001". The only difference is ctx["query_variant"] — and grep -rn query_variant tests/ returns exactly one hit, the write. It is never read. The same is true of ctx["id_format"] at line 1301. The verb distinction is backed by dead writes.

    (c) The authors already wrote it down. tests/bdd/steps/generic/when_request.py:176:

    @given("the Buyer Agent calls list_creative_formats without filters")
@when(parsers.re(r"the Buyer Agent (?:requests the format catalog"
                 r"|requests all formats with no filters"
                 r"|sends a list_creative_formats request)"))
def when_request_unfiltered(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """...Serves both the generic 'requests/sends a list_creative_formats request'
    When phrasings and the UC-005 baseline Given 'the Buyer Agent calls
    list_creative_formats without filters' (single canonical dispatch step)."""
    _call(ctx)

    Three verbs, one line of body.

    (d) Only two step functions carry mixed-verb decorators, and both prove sameness. The other is uc006_sync_creatives.py:280:

    @when("the Buyer Agent syncs the creatives")
@when("the Buyer Agent syncs the creative")
@when("the Buyer Agent syncs the creative via the REST/A2A endpoint")
@when("the Buyer Agent syncs the creative via the MCP tool")
@when("the Buyer Agent sends a sync_creatives request")
@when("the Buyer Agent sends sync_creatives")
@when("the Buyer Agent sends sync_creatives with the corrected manifest")
def when_sync_creative(ctx: dict) -> None:

    134 feature lines, two verbs, one body. Two of those seven aliases name a transport — and both match zero feature lines. They are dead text left over from before transport moved to the pytest parametrization (tests/bdd/conftest.py:4461, transports = [Transport.A2A, Transport.MCP, Transport.REST]). Had they been live, syncs the creative via the MCP tool would have run on a2a and rest as well, because the body reads ctx["transport"] and ignores the sentence.

    On the transport question you raised: a2a "sends a task" vs mcp "calls a tool" is not carried by any of the twelve verbs. Transport is named in 64 When lines across 16 features, and it is named as a *phrase*, orthogonally to the verb — calls the get_signals MCP tool (BR-UC-008:37), sends a build_creative request via A2A (BR-UC-020:27), calls build_creative MCP tool (BR-UC-020:42), invokes get_adcp_capabilities via <channel> (BR-UC-010:312), creates a content standard via <transport> (BR-UC-012:55). Per this repo's rule those phrases belong in the parametrization, and the step bodies already ignore them. So no verb needs to be preserved for a transport distinction.

    5. Recommendation

    Canonical verb: sends. Canonical form:

    When the Buyer Agent sends a <tool_name> request
When the Buyer Agent sends a <tool_name> request with:      # payload in a table

    Three reasons:

    1. It is already the plurality by a factor of three (1202 head-verb, 45.4%), so it is the smallest edit.

    2. It is the only verb already spanning every tool family in the bound corpus, so no family has to be re-taught.

    3. It forces the tool name into the sentence. The other verbs elide it — syncs the creative, requests delivery metrics, logs 5 valid purchase events to "src_web", validates delivery, submits a usage record all name an activity instead of a tool, and a reader cannot recover which tool runs without opening the step file. sends a sync_creatives request cannot hide it.

    Adopting it retires invokes, syncs, requests, calls, queries, logs, plus the creates/updates/deletes/previews/filters/retries tail — roughly 1160 lines renamed, ~2417 lines (91.2%) covered by one sentence shape. The risk is near zero for invokes (3 bound lines), logs (0), validates (2) and submits (5).

    6. Verbs whose lines cannot move to sends

    232 lines (8.8%). Four groups, each for a different reason.

    verb(s)lineswhy it cannot move
    navigates, submits(admin), sends(admin)15Not an AdCP dispatch. BR-ADMIN-ACCOUNTS.feature:27 When the admin navigates to the accounts list page, :39 When the admin submits the create account form with:, :128 When the admin sends a status change request for "Acme Corp" to "suspended". Body group G2 — Flask client, no tool, no wire. Different subject (the admin), different mechanism. Note :128 already misuses sends for a form POST; that one should move *away* from sends.
    delivers, attempts, evaluates, fires44 (19 bound)Seller-initiated outbound. BR-UC-004:278 When the system delivers a webhook report for "mb-001", :335 When the system attempts to deliver a webhook report, :363 When the system evaluates the circuit breaker state. Body group G3. The buyer sends nothing; the direction of travel is reversed.
    posts2Neither buyer nor seller. BR-UC-014:1223 When the host posts a user-action with surfaceId "surf-1"…, BR-UC-028:470 When the governance agent posts the collection_list_changed webhook. Third-party inbound; no step definition exists, so the intended mechanism is unrecorded.
    validates, returns, converts, processes, applies, assembles, rejects, publishes173 (100 internal-state + 73 seller-subject)Not dispatch at all, and should not be When steps. BR-UC-001:584 When the system validates the pricing option, :637 When the system converts the product to AdCP schema, :696 When the system returns refinement_applied, :1629 When the seller validates the product. These grade seller internals, which the harness cannot observe from the wire. Renaming them hides the problem; they need rewriting as buyer-observable dispatches or deleting. 165 of the 173 are in unbound features, so they grade nothing today either way.

    One special case, worth separating from the above because it *can* move but not yet:

    queries the task list10BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:1403. Binds to uc002_task_query.py:246 when_query_task_list, which calls asyncio.run(list_tasks(identity=env.identity, params)) in-process, off the wire** (_dispatch_list_tasks, line ~230). list_tasks is a registered tool, so the sentence should be sends a list_tasks request — but renaming it while the body bypasses every transport would put a dispatch sentence on a non-dispatch body. Wire it to dispatch_request first, then rename.

    The model holds for 90.7%. The 7.4% in (e) is the part the design does not account for.

    (a) plain dispatch — 2334 lines

    BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:49        When the Buyer Agent sends the create_media_buy request

    The verb sprawl is worse than twelve. Among the 2403 dispatch lines there are 29 distinct head verbs, not 12. The design's named set covers 91.7% of them; the tail is 18 more verbs over ~200 lines:

    logs 55 · validates 39 · creates 31 · updates 28 · deletes 13 · resends 10 · filters 8 · rejects 5 · attempts 4 · retries 3 · discovers 3 · references 3 · polls 2 · issues 2 · previews 2 · completes 1 · fetches 1 · replays 1

    Same tool, different verb, no semantic difference. This is the direct evidence that the verb carries nothing:

    toolverbs used

    Transport leaks into 53 When sentences (42 distinct) — the repo rule says that belongs to the parametrization:

    BR-UC-010-discover-seller-capabilities.feature:522   When the Buyer Agent sends a get_adcp_capabilities skill request via A2A with the token
