    Note 522 and 545 are the same operation with the same identity; the only difference between the two sentences is the transport, which the parametrization already supplies.

    (b) dispatch + identity override — 24 lines (0.9%)

    BR-UC-019-query-media-buys.feature:110     When the Buyer Agent sends a get_media_buys request without authentication
BR-UC-006-sync-creatives.feature:413       When the Buyer Agent syncs creative "creative-1" as principal "buyer-B"
BR-UC-013-manage-property-lists.feature:465  When an unauthenticated Buyer Agent sends a <operation> property list request

    M1 barely lives in the When at all. Identity language (authenticated/unauthenticated/token/principal/identity) appears on 719 Given/And lines and only 54 When lines, of which 24 are a real override. The M1 axis is a *Given* concern in this corpus, not a When one. Of the 24, only 7 are in wired scenarios.

    (c) verbatim/raw body — 45 lines (1.7%)

    Measured by AST reach to when_request._call_raw — not by the sentence, because the sentence never says so. All 45 are in BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature and read exactly like ordinary payload dispatch:

    BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:447   When the Buyer Agent requests formats with disclosure_positions filter []
BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:530   When the Buyer Agent requests formats with output_format_ids filter [{"id": "display_static"}]
BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:290   When the Buyer Agent requests formats with output_format_ids filter [{"agent_url": "https://creatives.adcontextprotocol.org", "id": "display_static"}]

    Line 290 (typed path, _call) and line 530 (raw path, _call_raw) are grammatically identical sentences that take different seams. Exactly one of the 45 admits it in words:

    BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:1101  When the Buyer Agent sends a list_creative_formats request with invalid dimension filters

    M2 is a step-implementation decision, not information the When carries. A parametrization that reads only the sentence cannot reconstruct it. Whatever replaces these steps has to take "raw vs typed" from somewhere other than the text — the AdCP model's own acceptance of the payload is the obvious derivation.

    (d) genuine non-dispatch event — 49 lines (1.8%)

    The design's three families are real and are the only three. Admin UI 15, seller-initiated webhook 27, clock/timeout 7.

    BR-ADMIN-ACCOUNTS.feature:128                        When the admin sends a status change request for "Acme Corp" to "suspended"
BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature:267      When the webhook scheduler fires for "mb-001"
BR-UC-024-content-compliance.feature:559             When neither party responds within the session timeout

    Two lines the word "webhook" nearly mis-binned — they are plain dispatch with a webhook URL in the payload, and I moved them to (a):

    BR-UC-013-manage-property-lists.feature:176  When the Buyer Agent creates a property list with name "Webhook Test" and webhook_url "https://example.com/hook"
BR-UC-028-manage-collection-lists.feature:224  When the Buyer Agent creates a collection list with name "Webhook Test" and webhook_url "https://example.com/hook"

    (e) SOMETHING ELSE — 197 lines, 126 distinct

    193 of 197 are in feature files that bind no step definitions at all. 19 feature files (1545 When lines, 58% of the corpus) have zero bound @when steps — no tests/bdd/test_*.py module exists for them. So (e) is overwhelmingly *unexecuted* text. That does not make it harmless: it is the text a rewrite would have to bind, and it does not fit the model.

    Five families.

    F1 — partition/boundary declaration, not an event (49 lines)

    A Gherkin When used as an Examples index. No actor, no action.

    BR-UC-028-manage-collection-lists.feature:503   When the boundary case <boundary_point> is evaluated
BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory.feature:878   When a product has relevance in the <partition> range
BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory.feature:1270  When the refinement_applied array is at boundary <boundary_point>

    When the boundary case <boundary_point> is evaluated alone repeats 22 times. It names no tool, no payload and no event — the entire content is in the Examples table.

    F2 — seller-side or third-party stipulation (86 lines)

    The subject is the seller, the system, a brand agent or an upstream backend. These are Givens about a *counterparty's* behavior, written as Whens.

    BR-UC-001-discover-available-inventory.feature:621   When the v3.1 schema validates the pricing option
BR-UC-014-sponsored-intelligence-session.feature:964 When the brand agent returns a UI element with type "app_handoff"
BR-UC-027-manage-async-tasks.feature:891             When the seller backend emits a tasks-get-response that omits the protocol field

    Also here: BR-UC-016-sync-audiences.feature:642 When the system validates authentication; BR-UC-030-manage-governance-binding.feature:404 When the Seller Agent surfaces a finding to the buyer.

    Two of these are wired and confirm the smell — BR-UC-004-deliver-media-buy-metrics.feature:970 When the webhook is configured with credentials "<partition>" binds uc004_delivery:when_partition_credentials:1228, a step that configures state and dispatches nothing.

    F3 — the response asserted inside a When (30 lines)

    A When that acts on the result of a dispatch that already happened. There is no action here at all; this is a Then in the wrong slot.

    BR-UC-020-build-creative.feature:590     When the build_creative response is received
BR-UC-021-preview-creative.feature:846   When the Buyer Agent receives the preview_creative response
BR-UC-005-discover-creative-formats.feature:1212  When the response returns a non-empty formats array

    The third is wired — it binds tests.bdd.steps.domain.uc005_format_id_shape:when_response_returns_non_empty_formats:39, whose body reaches no dispatch seam. A When step that dispatches nothing and reads a previous result.

    F4 — bare placeholder (3 lines)

    BR-UC-023-sync-product-catalogs.feature:783   When <action>

    Three occurrences. The whole step is one Examples column. Nothing in the sentence says what operation runs, so nothing can be derived from it without reading the table.

    F5 — other (29 lines)

    Mostly value stipulations and process narration:

    BR-UC-014-sponsored-intelligence-session.feature:1461  When the bound value is { "literalString": "Hello" }
BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:804                 When the approval task is paused awaiting the human decision
BR-UC-002-create-media-buy.feature:864                 When the task fails fatally
BR-UC-013-manage-property-lists.feature:795            When the resolved property set changes
BR-UC-024-content-compliance.feature:848               When the validation is executed

    BR-UC-014-sponsored-intelligence-session.feature:1461-1500 is a six-line block where the entire "When" is a JSON literal with no verb, no actor and no operation.

    What this means for the design

    1. 90.8% survives measurement. Classes (a)+(b)+(c) = 90.7%. The claim is sound.

    2. "Twelve verbs" is an undercount. 29 distinct head verbs across the dispatch lines. The tail (18 verbs, ~200 lines) is where a naive verb-alias table would silently drop scenarios — logs (55) and validates delivery (39) are each larger than attempts, posts, issues, polls and previews combined.

    3. M2 is not in the sentence. 44 of the 45 verbatim-body lines are indistinguishable in text from typed dispatch. Any single-When-step design has to derive raw-vs-typed from model acceptance, not from the wording, or those 44 stop grading the server.

    4. M1 is not really in the sentence either. 24 lines out of 2649. Identity variation is a Given in this corpus (719 lines), which means the When parametrization can probably drop the M1 axis entirely and read identity from ctx.

    5. The three non-dispatch events are correct and small — 49 lines, all three families confirmed, no fourth family found.

    6. The gap is (e): 197 lines / 126 distinct. Four of the five families are Whens that do not act — a partition index (F1), a counterparty stipulation (F2), a response assertion (F3), a bare literal (F4/F5). These are mis-shaped scenarios, not a hole in the dispatch model, and 193 of 197 sit in the 19 feature files that bind nothing today. They should be reshaped (into Givens, Examples, or Thens) rather than given a dispatch verb.

    Method caveat: the (e) bucket was read line by line (all 126 distinct strings). The (a) bucket was classified by regex plus step-binding and spot-checked, not exhaustively read; a small number of (a) lines could belong in (e) on close reading. Every quoted line above was re-read from the file with sed -n '<n>p' and matches verbatim.

    3. Where the pre-3.1.1 fields still live in production

    3a. _build_creative_data reads five fields the pinned model cannot carry

    src/core/tools/creatives/_assets.py:146-190, verbatim:

        data: dict[str, Any] = {
        "url": url,
        "click_url": getattr(creative, "click_url", None),
        "width": getattr(creative, "width", None),
        "height": getattr(creative, "height", None),
        "duration": getattr(creative, "duration", None),
    }
    if creative.assets:
        data["assets"] = creative.assets
    snippet = getattr(creative, "snippet", None)
    if snippet:
        data["snippet"] = snippet
        data["snippet_type"] = getattr(creative, "snippet_type", None)
    template_variables = getattr(creative, "template_variables", None)
    if template_variables:
        data["template_variables"] = template_variables

    Its own docstring at :152 still advertises them: optional fields (assets, snippet, snippet_type, template_variables),.

    None of click_url, width, height, duration, snippet, snippet_type, template_variables is a declared field on CreativeAsset1/CreativeAsset2. They can only arrive through extra. And the request DTO is CreativeAssetRequest, whose config is:

        model_config = ConfigDict(extra=get_pydantic_extra_mode(), from_attributes=True)

    — src/core/schemas/creative.py:176

    def get_pydantic_extra_mode() -> Literal["ignore", "forbid"]:
    """Get Pydantic extra field handling mode based on environment.

    Production: "ignore" - Accept extra fields for forward compatibility
    Non-production: "forbid" - Reject extra fields to catch bugs early
    """
    return "ignore" if is_production() else "forbid"

    — src/core/config.py:172-178

    So in every test environment the seven getattr calls are structurally unreachable: sending the field is a VALIDATION_ERROR, not sending it makes getattr return None. In production extra="ignore" drops it before getattr runs. These branches cannot execute at the pin. They are the tail of the pre-3.1.1 shape, and they are what makes tests still write snippet / media_url payloads that nothing consumes.

    5. The push_notification_config disease, reproduced exactly — 38 seeders, 6 protocols, one file

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py (≈7 600 lines) contains 38 step functions that seed ctx["creatives"]. Each writes its own subset of three satellite ctx keys. Measured distribution:

    satellite keys writtencount

    [] (none)8

    ['creative_format_id', 'creative_id']2
    ['creative_format_id', 'creative_agent_url', 'creative_id']2
    ['creative_id']1

    Six protocols for one concept. 10 of the 38 build the payload with no shared builder at all.

    6. Seeded shapes that the shape fix will break, named

    Once the request DTO enforces the pin (assets required, asset_type discriminated):

    a. Payloads with no assets at all. 84 of 110 hand-built sites. Inside the BDD file: :2062, :4954, :4974, :5347. The first is deliberate and documented:

        creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": creative_id,
        "name": "Creative Without media_url",
        "format_id": {"id": format_id, "agent_url": env.DEFAULT_AGENT_URL},
        # Intentionally no "assets" and no "url" / "media_url" — triggers the
        # has_media_url=False path in _processing.py preview branch.
    }

    — :2061-2068, whose docstring at :2056 reads optional — assets are optional on the request schema. That premise is false at the pin. This scenario needs a different lever.

    b. Asset slots with no asset_type. The union at asset_union.py:82 is discriminator='asset_type':

            "assets": {
            role: {"content": content},
        },

    — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:5245-5247 (given_generative_creative_with_asset_role). This is exactly make_legacy_asset_dict's shape hand-inlined, bypassing text_spec, in a step whose sibling generative Givens at :5072 and :5113 *do* use text_spec.

    c. "assets": {}. :5276 and :5297. The pattern-properties object permits empty, but _processing.py:474 (if creative.assets:) treats {} as absent, so these scenarios grade the same branch as (a) by accident.

    d. format_id as a bare string. tests/fixtures/factories.py:242, tests/fixtures/data/sample_creatives.json:3,26. The pin: *"Always a structured object {agent_url, id} — never a plain string."*

    e. variants=[] into Creative(...). ~20 sites incl. two shared helpers (§3d).

    f. Asset role keys must match ^[a-z0-9_]+$. Every image_spec/text_spec role in the tree passes, but the 84 hand-built payloads have never been checked against it.

    7. Recommended order (the owner's own sequencing, made concrete)

    1. Delete tests/fixtures/factories.py:237-298 CreativeFactory and tests/fixtures/data/sample_creatives.json. Its single consumer (tests/integration/conftest.py:812-827) is already broken on create_batch; repoint that fixture at tests/factories/creative.py:CreativeFactory. Drop "CreativeFactory" from tests/fixtures/__init__.py:17 so the name collision stops existing. This removes 2 of the 3 pre-3.1.1 owners for free.

    2. Collapse owners 9–14 onto creative_payload (tests/helpers/creative_test_helpers.py:80), which already has the right four defaults. Fix make_test_creative/make_test_creative_list (:271, :308) to drop variants, use format_id not format, and make the second call the first. Have SyncCreativesRequestFactory (tests/factories/request.py:200-205) call it instead of restating the literal.

    3. Unify production first, not last. Extract the one creative-manifest builder from _processing.py:466/:821, and reconcile creative_helpers.py:236 against media_buy_create.py:753 into a single adapter-asset builder. Until those two agree, no test-side fix can be checked against a single truth.

    4. Give the BDD file one seeder that writes all four ctx keys unconditionally (creatives, creative_format_id, creative_agent_url, creative_id), route all 38 through it, then delete every ctx.get(...) or ... fallback at :891, :2197, :2223, :2245, :4530, :5729, :6216 and the seven creative_format_id defaults. The fallbacks are the mechanism by which a divergent Given passes; leaving them in means step 5 cannot be graded.

    5. Only then flip the shape: assets required, asset_type on every slot, and delete the dead snippet/snippet_type/template_variables/duration/click_url/width/height reads at _assets.py:167-181 and listing.py:264-269.

    Steps 1–4 are pure deduplication and are independently verifiable. Step 5 applied first would land on 110 hand-built sites in 41 shapes across 33 files, and would look done.

    (c) What the pin actually requires — check this first, it reframes the rest

    adcp==6.6.0 (pyproject.toml:10), AdCP 3.1.1. .venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/adcp/_schemas/3.1/core/pricing-option.json is only a oneOf over nine members.       "required": [
    "pricing_option_id",

    "currency"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": true

    Optional properties: fixed_price, floor_price, max_bid, price_guidance, min_spend_per_package, price_breakdown, eligible_adjustments.

    "description": "... If fixed_price is present, it's fixed pricing. If absent, it's auction-based (floor_price and price_guidance optional). ..."

    "pricing_option_id": "po_default",
}

    and tests/integration/test_mcp_tool_roundtrip_validation.py:45-50.

    S6 — pre-V3 dict (19 literals / 10 files). Two distinct populations. The dead one, tests/unit/test_adcp_contract.py:347-356:

            return {
            "tenant_id": tenant_id,
            "product_id": product_id,

    The legitimate one — package_pricing_info, an internal adapter contract whose production owner is src/core/tools/media_buy_create.py:1644-1650:

            return {
            "pricing_model": first_option.pricing_model,

    mirrored by hand in tests/unit/test_gam_currency_detection.py:238-246, tests/unit/test_gam_products_map_delivery_type.py:95-102, tests/e2e/test_gam_lifecycle.py:438-445, tests/integration/test_delivery_poll_behavioral.py:2099-2103, tests/unit/test_multi_package_id_bug.py:24-27,109-125.

    S7 — Signal VendorPricingOption (6 literals / 4 files). tests/unit/test_adcp_contract.py:1016:

                    {"pricing_option_id": "cpm_usd", "cpm": 2.50, "currency": "USD", "model": "cpm"},

    This one is correct and must be left alone. It is a different entity — core/vendor-pricing-option.json → core/signal-pricing.json, discriminated by model with required: ["model","cpm","currency"]. It collides with product pricing only on the attribute name pricing_options. A regex-driven "fix" would break it.

    (b) What the shapes disagree about

    1. pricing_option_id — the ORM has no such column. src/core/database/models.py:498-509 declares pricing_model, rate, currency, is_fixed, price_guidance, parameters, min_spend_per_package and nothing else. So every consumer synthesizes the id. Nine independent copies of the same f-string, four in src/, five in tests/:

  • - src/core/product_conversion.py:81 — f"{pricing_model}_{currency.lower()}_{'fixed' if is_fixed else 'auction'}"
  • - src/core/database/product_pricing.py:52-53
  • - src/core/tools/media_buy_create.py:1658-1659, again at :1677, again at :2368-2371
  • - src/core/tools/media_buy_delivery.py:1047-1048
  • - tests/bdd/steps/generic/_create_request.py:23-24
  • - tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc026_package_media_buy.py:106-107 and, separately, :356-357
  • - tests/integration/conftest.py:962-963
  • - tests/integration/test_mcp_tool_roundtrip_validation.py:53
  • - tests/integration/test_bdd_create_request_builder.py:202

    • 2. rate vs fixed_price/floor_price. S1/S2/S6 write rate + is_fixed; S3/S4/S5 write fixed_price/floor_price. The pinned members reject rate outright (extra_forbidden, per src/core/schemas/pricing.py:83 model_config = ConfigDict(extra=get_pydantic_extra_mode())).

    3. price_guidance.floor vs floor_price. S1/S2 encode the floor inside the JSON blob (price_guidance={"floor": 10.0, "p50": 20.0, ...}, tests/integration/conftest.py:599); the pin puts floor_price at top level and leaves price_guidance percentile-only.

    4. **rate literal type across the *same* factory:** 74× Decimal("..."), 8× float, 6× str (tests/helpers/sample_account.py:84, tests/integration/test_wire_omission_matrix.py:109, .../test_get_products_placement_schema.py:104,149, .../test_get_products_format_id_filter.py:43,61), 1× None. The column is DECIMAL(10, 2).

    5. max_bid, price_breakdown, eligible_adjustments — in the pin, absent from S1, S2, S3, S5, S6. Exactly one test writes max_bid (tests/unit/test_pricing_option_schemas.py:153).

    6. The protocol around the shape diverges, exactly as with push_notification_config. BDD Given steps write six different ctx keys after seeding — ctx["default_pricing_option"] (given_media_buy.py:1075), ctx["pricing_option_map"] (uc026:377), ctx["product_pricing_options"] (uc026:379), ctx["pricing_option_max_bid"] (uc026:680), ctx["expected_missing_pricing_options"] (uc026:668), ctx["pricing_validated"] (given_media_buy.py:683). _resolve_pricing_id (uc026:38-46) reads pricing_option_map:

           mapping = ctx.get("pricing_option_map", {})
       return mapping.get(label, label)

    pricing_option_map is written only inside given_product_with_pricing (uc026:377). Every other Given that seeds a pricing option — all of _setup_fixed_pricing / _setup_auction_pricing / _setup_both_pricing (given_media_buy.py:1073-1144) — writes kwargs["packages"][0]["pricing_option_id"] directly and never populates the map. A scenario that seeds through the second path and resolves through the first falls through to mapping.get(label, label) and silently gets the raw label back. That is the same fallback-passes failure mode as the push-config precedent, on a shared factory.

    7. Test/production drift on the same expression. tests/unit/test_pricing_option_rootmodel.py:103-110 says it tests production but re-types the logic locally, and the two now differ:

           # This is the FIXED logic from media_buy_create.py. V3 schema models
       # carry no is_fixed flag — fixed pricing is fixed_price being present.
       ...
       is_fixed = "fixed" if first_option.fixed_price is not None else "auction"

    Production at src/core/tools/media_buy_create.py:2370 still reads the ORM flag:

                               is_fixed = "fixed" if first_option.is_fixed else "auction"

    (d) Which shapes are WRONG against the pin today

    **W1 — S3 (legacy flat model), 26 sites in 5 files, is wrong on three axes and is the only shape that is wrong *and* actively graded.**

  • - Its validator invents a rule the pin does not state (src/core/schemas/_base.py:1361-1364):
    •           if has_fixed and has_floor:
              raise ValueError("Cannot have both fixed_price and floor_price - use one or the other")
          if not has_fixed and not has_floor:
              raise ValueError("Must have either fixed_price (for fixed-rate) or floor_price (for auction)")

    The pinned member accepts "neither" — verified above. Three tests grade the invented half: tests/integration/test_get_products_behavioral.py:800-811, tests/unit/test_product_schema_obligations.py:1243-1253, tests/unit/test_media_buy.py:553-565.

  • - It carries is_fixed (not in the pin) and lacks max_bid / price_breakdown / eligible_adjustments.
  • - Two of those tests pass vacuously. tests/unit/test_media_buy.py:546 and :562 omit the required pricing_option_id, so the raise never reaches the XOR validator:
    •   kwargs: {'pricing_model': 'cpm', 'currency': 'USD', 'fixed_price': 5.0, 'floor_price': 2.0}
     errors: [('missing', ('pricing_option_id',), 'Field required')]

    Both would still pass with the XOR validator deleted, and both cite a spec permalink for a rule the pinned schema does not contain (test_media_buy.py:540-541: "Spec: CONFIRMED -- cpm-option.json description implies XOR").

    W2 — four PricingOptionFactory calls seed rows the production schema rejects. alembic/versions/a098c8bb42ed_add_pricing_option_constraints.py:43-52:

        op.create_check_constraint(
        "check_auction_has_price_guidance",
        "pricing_options",
        "(is_fixed = true) OR (is_fixed = false AND price_guidance IS NOT NULL AND price_guidance ? 'floor')",
    )

    Violating sites (is_fixed=False, no price_guidance): tests/integration/test_product_deletion.py:49,57,69, tests/integration/test_get_products_database_integration.py:185-190. A fifth violates check_fixed_has_rate on purpose — tests/integration/test_product_pricing_options_required.py:318-323 (rate=None, is_fixed=True).

    All five pass. src/core/database/models.py:514-521 declares only the FK and the index — the two check constraints and the prevent_empty_pricing_options trigger live in migrations only, and tests/conftest_db.py:419 builds the test schema with Base.metadata.create_all(bind=eng, checkfirst=True). The tests cannot see this class of seeding error at all. Note test_product_pricing_options_required.py cannot exist as written against a migrated database.

    W3 — S6's dead half. tests/unit/test_adcp_contract.py:343-356 returns a dict shaped like the ORM row (tenant_id, product_id, rate, is_fixed) and is documented as """Helper to create pricing option dict for tests."""; tests/unit/test_product_conversion_pricing.py:37-49 builds another. These feed convert_pricing_option_to_adcp, which accepts dict-or-ORM (src/core/product_conversion.py:71-75), so they are load-bearing for that one function and wrong for anything downstream of it.

    W4 — create_test_pricing_option, tests/helpers/adcp_factories.py:575-596, self-declares as broken and still has 2 callers:

        DEPRECATED: Use create_test_cpm_pricing_option() or other specific pricing
    functions instead. This function creates incomplete pricing options that
    don't match adcp 2.1.0 discriminated union requirements.
    ...
    return {"pricing_model": pricing_model, "currency": currency, **kwargs}

    Missing the required pricing_option_id.

    Not wrong: S1/S2 (they are the DB row, and the DB genuinely has rate/is_fixed), S4, S5, S7, and the package_pricing_info half of S6.

    Ranking the fix by sites covered

    1. Derive pricing_option_id once (9 copies, 4 in src/). Nothing else can be deduplicated safely until this exists, because the id is the only handle 194 factory calls give a test, and it is currently re-derived by whoever needs it. It is also the mechanism behind the pricing_option_map fallback in §b6. Smallest diff with the largest reach.

    2. S1, 194 calls / 37 files. Give the factory a pricing_option_id LazyAttribute using that one derivation, and make is_fixed=False imply a floor-bearing price_guidance so W2's four sites stop being silently invalid. Also add the two check constraints to models.py.__table_args__ so create_all reproduces the production schema — otherwise the seeding fix is unverifiable.

    3. S2, 64 calls / 25 files → route to S1. 45 of the 64 are byte-identical modulo literals; tests/integration/test_gam_pricing_models_integration.py and test_gam_pricing_restriction.py (10 sites) differ only by spelling None for three nullables.

    4. W1: retire S3, 26 calls / 5 files. src/core/schemas/_base.py:1304 already says the model has no production consumers. Move the 26 sites to src.core.schemas.pricing members, and reconcile the three XOR tests against the pin before moving them (the rule is not in 3.1.1) rather than porting the assertion. Fix the two vacuous ones in test_media_buy.py first — they currently prove nothing either way.

    5. S5, 25 literals / 10 files. One helper already exists (create_test_cpm_pricing_option, 50 refs); fold _DEFAULT_PRICING_OPTION (tests/harness/product_unit.py:36) and _pricing_kwargs_from_orm's fallback (test_mcp_tool_roundtrip_validation.py:45) into it, and delete create_test_pricing_option (W4, 2 callers).

    6. S6, 19 literals / 10 files. Split first: the package_pricing_info population (5 files) gets one builder mirroring media_buy_create.py:1644-1650; the two ORM-shaped dict helpers (W3) go away with S2.

    7. S7 — do not touch. Different pinned schema, correct as written.

    Seeding-protocol audit: creatives and pricing options

    0. The measured counts, verified and refined

    brief saidverified
    pricing options40 files use PricingOptionFactory, 35 build inline40 files confirmed. The sharper number: 137 distinct functions call PricingOptionFactory(...) directly. Only 18 of them are in a seeding-helper layer (bdd steps / harness / helpers); the other 119 are inside test bodies and fixtures.
    creatives36 files use CreativeFactory, 5 build inline36 files confirmed, but there are two different classes named CreativeFactory (§4). For the BDD creative payload: 40 step functions write ctx["creatives"], and all 40 build the payload dict inline. Zero delegate.

    The creative-payload number is the one that matters most: python3 -c over the AST of uc006_sync_creatives.py + uc006_storyboard_creative_sync.py finds 40 writer functions, 40 inline dict literals, and 18 distinct ctx key-sets left behind.

    1. CREATIVES — 40 seeders, 18 different protocols

    Every one of the 40 builds {"creative_id", "name", "format_id", "assets"} by hand. What differs is what else they leave on ctx:

    Only 5 of 40 write ctx["creative_agent_url"]. Only 3 write ctx["creative_id"]. 7 write nothing at all.

    1a. The exact push_notification_config shape: one key, three reader disciplines

    The 35 seeders that never write creative_agent_url cannot be paired with the first two sentences without a KeyError, and pair *silently* with the third — which then builds the product against env.DEFAULT_AGENT_URL rather than against the creative's actual agent. When the creative was seeded for e2e (_E2E_AGENT_URL = "https://creative-agent.adcp.test:8443/api/creative-agent", line 48), the fallback builds a product pointing at a different agent entirely and the format-compatibility check grades a mismatch it manufactured.

    creative_id has the same three disciplines in the same file:

  • - tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:3699 — creative_id = ctx["creatives"][-1]["creative_id"]
  • - tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:2223 — creative_id = ctx.get("creative_id") or ctx["creatives"][-1]["creative_id"]
  • - tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:7088 — creative_id = getattr(result, "creative_id", None) or ctx.get("creative_id")

    • 1b. A Then whose assertion is *conditional* on a key 7 seeders never write

    2. CREATIVES — the shape defect, and the helper built to prevent it that nobody calls

    tests/factories/creative_asset.py:18-33 exists precisely to own the wrong shape:

    def make_legacy_asset_dict(asset_id: str, **fields: object) -> dict:
    """Build a LEGACY (AdCP v1) single-dict asset entry: ``{asset_id: {**fields}}``.

    The v1 shape has NO ``asset_type`` discriminator and is NOT a list — it keys
    each role directly to a flat dict of fields (e.g. ``url``/``width``/``height``,
    ...
    Use this ONLY for legacy-input / negative tests that deliberately exercise the
    old shape. New or valid creative assets must use the AssetSpec mechanism
    (``image_spec``/``text_spec``/``video_spec``/``url_spec``/``asset_spec`` +
    ``build_assets``). Centralising the legacy shape here keeps it out of inline
    test dicts — see #1391.
    """

    It has 2 consumers, both unit tests (test_validate_creative_assets.py, test_creative_conversion_assets.py). Meanwhile the seeding layer hand-rolls that exact legacy shape at 16 sites, for creatives on the happy path (approved=True), which is the case the docstring says must use build_assets:

    3. PRICING OPTIONS — the ORM row has no id, so five places derive one and four more invent literals

    src/core/database/models.py:491-509 — there is no pricing_option_id column:

    class PricingOption(Base):
    __tablename__ = "pricing_options"

    id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer, primary_key=True)
    tenant_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(50), nullable=False)
    product_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(100), nullable=False)
    pricing_model: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), nullable=False)
    rate: Mapped[Decimal | None] = mapped_column(DECIMAL(10, 2), nullable=True)
    currency: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(3), nullable=False)
    is_fixed: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, nullable=False)

    The wire id is derived. Five independent copies of the derivation:

    production ×3

  • - src/core/product_conversion.py:81 — pricing_option_id = f"{pricing_model}_{currency.lower()}_{'fixed' if is_fixed else 'auction'}"
  • - src/core/database/product_pricing.py:53 — pricing_option_id = f"{po.pricing_model}_{po.currency.lower()}_{fixed_str}"
  • - src/core/tools/media_buy_create.py:1656 — # Construct pricing_option_id in same format as get_products returns

    • tests ×2

  • - tests/bdd/steps/generic/_create_request.py:17-24:
    •   def pricing_option_id(pricing_option: Any) -> str:
      """Synthetic pricing_option_id string from a PricingOption ORM row.

      Matches the production/`given_media_buy` convention
      ``{pricing_model}_{currency_lower}_{fixed|auction}``.
      """
      fixed_str = "fixed" if pricing_option.is_fixed else "auction"
      return f"{pricing_option.pricing_model}_{pricing_option.currency.lower()}_{fixed_str}"
  • - tests/harness/webhook_registration.py:227-239 — a *deliberate*, documented copy:
    •           ``pricing_option_id`` is derived here rather than imported: the ORM row
          has no id column of its own, so the request-side identifier is built from
          (model, currency, fixed-vs-auction). The same derivation exists in
          ``tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py``, and it is deliberately
          NOT imported from there — a BDD step module is not an API, and importing
          from one would make this harness a downstream of the step registry.
          """
          return create_test_media_buy_request_dict(
              product_ids=[product.product_id],
              pricing_option_id=(
                  f"{pricing_option.pricing_model}_{pricing_option.currency.lower()}_"
                  + ("fixed" if pricing_option.is_fixed else "auction")
              ),

    The reason is sound (a step module is not an API) but the conclusion isn't the only option — the derivation belongs in tests/factories/product.py next to the factory, which both may import.

    a sixth, inline, in a BDD step — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc026_package_media_buy.py:356-357:

    4. PRICING OPTIONS — the protocol defect: one writer, fifteen mutators, one clobberer

    ctx["default_pricing_option"] is written in exactly one place, tests/bdd/conftest.py:4930-4936:

    def _seed_media_buy_chain(ctx: dict, env: object) -> None:
    """Seed the full create dependency chain (tenant/principal/product/pricing)."""
    tenant, principal, product, pricing_option = env.setup_media_buy_data()
    ctx["tenant"] = tenant
    ctx["principal"] = principal
    ctx["default_product"] = product
    ctx["default_pricing_option"] = pricing_option

    Fifteen Given steps in given_media_buy.py create a NEW PricingOption and re-pin the package to it — none of them updates that key. Measured across the whole tree: of the 137 PricingOptionFactory call sites, 11 do commit + derive + kwargs["packages"][…]["pricing_option_id"] = … and none writes ctx["default_pricing_option"].

    Three consequences, all live:

    (a) A reader falls back to a hardcoded literal that duplicates the other step's literal. tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:1001-1017:

    @given("a package pricing option has floor_price set and fixed_price null")
def given_floor_price_only(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """Create an auction pricing option with floor_price (no fixed_price)."""
    env = ctx["env"]
    auction_po = PricingOptionFactory(
        product=ctx["default_product"],
        pricing_model="cpm",
        currency="USD",
        is_fixed=False,
        price_guidance={"floor": 2.0},
    )
    env._commit_factory_data()
    kwargs = _ensure_request_defaults(ctx)
    ...
    kwargs["packages"][0]["pricing_option_id"] = pricing_option_id(auction_po)

    and the step that reads it back, :1027-1038:

        # Derive bid from the actual floor price if available, otherwise use safe default
    po = ctx.get("default_pricing_option")
    floor = 2.0  # default from given_floor_price_only
    if po is not None and not po.is_fixed:
        guidance = getattr(po, "price_guidance", None)
        if isinstance(guidance, dict) and "floor" in guidance:
            floor = float(guidance["floor"])
    kwargs["packages"][0]["bid_price"] = floor + 3.0

    ctx["default_pricing_option"] is still the chain-seeded is_fixed=True row, so not po.is_fixed is False, the guidance branch never runs, and the step uses the literal 2.0 — whose comment names the other function. It passes only because two independently-maintained 2.0s currently agree. This is the push_notification_config defect exactly: a step reading the key its sentence never wrote, green on a fallback.

    (b) A Then-shaped Given asserts the package still points at the stale default. :975-998:

        po = ctx.get("default_pricing_option")
    assert po is not None, "No default_pricing_option in ctx — Given step ordering error"
    assert po.is_fixed, "Default pricing option should be fixed"
    ...
    expected_po_id = pricing_option_id(po)
    actual_po_id = kwargs["packages"][0].get("pricing_option_id")
    assert actual_po_id == expected_po_id, (

    Order-dependent by construction: any preceding pricing Given makes this fail, and it passes only when nothing re-pinned.

    (c) A builder silently reverts every re-pin. tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:109-126:

    def harness_create_request_kwargs(ctx: dict) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Request kwargs for a create dispatched from a harness-seeded scenario.
    ...
    """
    kwargs = _ensure_request_defaults(ctx)
    product = ctx.get("default_product")
    pricing_option = ctx.get("default_pricing_option")
    if product is not None:
        kwargs["packages"][0]["product_id"] = product.product_id
    if pricing_option is not None:
        kwargs["packages"][0]["pricing_option_id"] = pricing_option_id(pricing_option)
    return kwargs

    Unconditional overwrite from the never-updated key. Callers: tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc004_delivery.py:1088 and tests/bdd/steps/domain/egress_ssrf.py:231, 261. Any scenario that runs a pricing Given and then dispatches through one of those loses the pricing option it seeded.

    4a. Three request-builders with three different write disciplines on the same key

    functiondiscipline on ctx["request_kwargs"]
    _create_request.py:27-81 build_create_request_kwargsunconditional assign — :80 ctx["request_kwargs"] = kwargs
    given_media_buy.py:63-106 _ensure_request_defaultsif-absent — :78 if "request_kwargs" not in ctx:
    given_media_buy.py:109-126 harness_create_request_kwargsre-pin two fields after the if-absent

    _ensure_request_defaults documents the hazard at :70-72:

        * the if-absent guard. ``ctx["request_kwargs"]`` is an accumulator that every
      Given mutates, and the builder assigns unconditionally, so calling it on an
      already-populated ctx would discard earlier steps' work.

    build_create_request_kwargs is nonetheless called directly by uc019_query_media_buys.py:3007 and uc002_create_media_buy.py:1476 — both of which discard whatever earlier Givens accumulated.

    4b. A fourth pricing protocol, with its own fallback-to-label

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc026_package_media_buy.py:315-379 writes ctx["pricing_option_map"] (label → canonical id) and ctx["product_pricing_options"], and never touches default_pricing_option. Its reader, :38-46:

    def _resolve_pricing_id(ctx: dict, label: str) -> str:
    """Map a feature-file pricing label to the synthetic pricing_option_id.

    Feature file uses "cpm-standard" / "cpm-auction"; production code constructs
    "{pricing_model}_{currency}_{fixed|auction}" (e.g. "cpm_usd_fixed").
    Falls through to the raw label if no mapping exists.
    """
    mapping = ctx.get("pricing_option_map", {})
    return mapping.get(label, label)

    A scenario whose table names cpm-standard but whose Given chain never ran given_product_with_pricing sends the literal string "cpm-standard" as pricing_option_id. No product has it — so the request fails on a seeding artifact, and a scenario grading a *rejection* passes for entirely the wrong reason.

    5. Two different classes named CreativeFactory, and the one that feeds columns the model does not have

    tests/factories/creative.py:16 — the real one, SQLAlchemyModelFactory over the ORM Creative.

    tests/fixtures/factories.py:237-277 — a second class, same name, plain dicts:

    tests/integration/conftest.py:812 imports that one and feeds its output straight into the ORM model at :843-850:

        from tests.fixtures import CreativeFactory, MediaBuyFactory
    ...
        for creative_data in creatives_data:
            creative = Creative(
                tenant_id=creative_data["tenant_id"],

    src/core/database/models.py:689-708 has no format_id and no content — the columns are format, agent_url, data, plus required name. So Creative(format_id=…, content=…) raises TypeError. Three further problems in the same eight lines:

  • - status="pending" is the exact non-spec value tests/factories/creative.py:34-38 was fixed to stop emitting: *"a non-spec value (this was "pending") made every factory-built creative reach list_creatives through the reader's unparseable-status branch rather than through real status semantics."*
  • - content: json.dumps(content) — a JSON string handed to what would be a JSONType column, against the CLAUDE.md rule.
  • - agent_url and name are nullable=False and never supplied.

    • The fixture test_media_buy_workflow has zero consumers. It survives only as two entries in the repository-pattern allowlist (tests/unit/test_architecture_repository_pattern.py:104 and :712) — the allowlist is what keeps this compiling-but-broken seeder alive.

    6. What this means for the ordering the owner asked for

    The brief's instinct is right, and stronger than "N wrong copies". Two specific traps for a shape-first fix:

    1. The shape fix has no single owner to apply it to. For creatives there are 40 inline payload builders and 16 legacy-assets sites; for pricing options, 6 derivations of the id and 4 literal conventions. make_legacy_asset_dict and pricing_option_id() already exist as the intended owners and are bypassed by almost everything.

    2. Deduplicating the shape leaves the protocol wrong. Even after every creative goes through one builder, ctx["creative_agent_url"] is read three different ways and written by 5 of 40 sites; ctx["default_pricing_option"] is read by 5 sites and updated by 1 of 16 mutators. Consolidating the builder does not make harness_create_request_kwargs:124-125 stop clobbering, and does not make then_creative_validated_by_agent:6096 stop skipping its assertion.

    Concretely, before touching shape, the seeding contract needs to be one function per object that writes the complete key-set:

  • - creatives → (payload, format_id, agent_url, creative_id) written together, always; readers drop every .get(..., default) and or chain.
  • - pricing options → any step that mints a PricingOption must update ctx["default_pricing_option"] (or the concept must be deleted and the id carried on the package only); harness_create_request_kwargs's unconditional re-pin has to go either way.
  • - the id derivation moves to tests/factories/product.py beside PricingOptionFactory, so _create_request.py, webhook_registration.py and uc026_package_media_buy.py can import it without a step module becoming an API.

    • The model for the creative side already exists in-tree: tests/bdd/test_uc018_list_creatives.py:108-153.

    SURFACE REPORT — what is actually broken now, and whether it is one shape or manyagent 4 of 4

    SURFACE REPORT — what is actually broken now, and whether it is one shape or many

    Verified: the run's crash line numbers align exactly with the current worktree source in six independent files (test_device_type_breakdown.py:366, test_response_shapes.py:824, test_adcp_contract.py:1903, test_delivery_poll_behavioral.py:1612, test_mcp_tools_audit.py:151, media_buy_delivery.py:1031). The run graded this tree. Note the commit that introduced the new shape (f150df8e6, 13:22) postdates the run's completion (10:10) — cassini graded the dirty tree; the code is the same.

    Answer to the question asked: TWO wrong shapes, each reached many ways, plus a tail of ~40 genuinely unrelated defects. 696 of the 745 failures across unit + integration + bdd_inprocess + bdd_e2e are two shapes.

    Bearing on the order of work

    For pricing, dedupe genuinely must come first, and the target is not PricingOptionFactory (which is fine — tests/factories/product.py:38-49, five sane defaults). The target is the buy→package→pricing linkage, which today has 101 hand-written literals, 6 seeding sites, 5 re-implementations of the synthetic id, and 3 mutually incompatible protocols (synthetic string / DB PK / arbitrary label). A shape fix applied to MediaBuyFactory alone leaves 5 of the 6 sites wrong and the 345 UC-004 rows still red, because site 5 (the UC-004 Given) never touches a package at all and site 4 patches _get_pricing_options out of existence (delivery_poll_unit.py:50, defaulted to {} at :79).

    For creatives the order does not apply: one file, one shape, upstream-blocked.

    Seeding: intent + transport independence

    Report follows.agent 1 of 2

    Report follows.

    ONE CONSTRUCTION, EVERY TRANSPORT — seeding across bdd_inprocess and bdd_e2e

    Worktree ., branch exec/itsii-1-drop-impl. Read-only; nothing modified.

    0. The headline

    Row seeding is already transport-independent, and has been since #1418. Mock seeding is not, and that is where the whole divergence lives. Measured on innet_070926_0757: of 2199 xfailed e2e_rest scenarios, 180 are E2EUnsupportedSetup, and 143 of those 180 are one method — CreativeFormatsEnv.set_registry_formats. One transport-dependent creative seeder accounts for 79% of the escape-hatch xfails.

    Transport parametrization, same run:

    bdd_inprocess.json  8195 tests  {'[a2a': 2845, '[rest': 2668, '[mcp': 2655}

    1. HOW seeding differs between in-process and e2e today

    It does not differ in code. The same seed function runs on both; only the engine underneath it changes. Two lines decide everything.

    The engine switch — tests/harness/_base.py:1336-1353

                    if self._database_url:
                    from sqlalchemy import create_engine

                    from src.core.database.database_session import _pydantic_json_serializer

                    self._e2e_engine = create_engine(
                        self._database_url, echo=False, json_serializer=_pydantic_json_serializer
                    )
                    self._guard("db_engine", self._dispose_engine)
                    engine = self._e2e_engine
                else:
                    engine = get_engine()

                self._session = SASession(bind=engine)
                self._guard("db_session", self._close_session)

                for f in ALL_FACTORIES:
                    f._meta.sqlalchemy_session = self._session

    self._database_url is set in __init__ (tests/harness/_base.py:365):

            self._database_url = database_url or (e2e_config.postgres_url if e2e_config else None)

    Every factory in ALL_FACTORIES is bound to that one session, and every factory carries sqlalchemy_session_persistence = "commit". So CreativeFactory(...) in a step body writes to the per-test Postgres in-process and to the live server's Postgres over e2e_rest, with the *same call*.

    The production-engine switch — tests/bdd/conftest.py:5039-5052

    def _db_scope_for(request: pytest.FixtureRequest, e2e_config: object | None) -> AbstractContextManager[None]:
    """Select the production-DB scope for an e2e-capable harness branch.

    In-process transports need the per-test database (``integration_db``).
    Over e2e_rest, ``integration_db`` would repoint production's cached engine
    at an empty per-test DB while the env's factories write to the live server
    DB — so any in-process production call inside an e2e scenario (raw
    ``get_db_session()`` read-backs in Then steps, TRANSPORT-BYPASS Givens
    calling an ``_impl``) would read the wrong database. Point production at
    the server DB instead for the scenario duration.
    """
    if e2e_config is None:
        request.getfixturevalue("integration_db")
        return nullcontext()
    return _production_db_pointed_at(e2e_config.postgres_url)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]

    2. What makes a seeding helper transport-independent

    The rule: a seed is transport-independent iff its only effect is a row in a table the live server also reads. A seed whose effect is a Python object in this process is not, and cannot be made so without a production read.

    Works everywhere — MediaBuyCreateEnv.setup_media_buy_data, tests/harness/media_buy_create.py:93-130

        def setup_media_buy_data(self) -> tuple:
        """Create the full dependency chain needed for create_media_buy.
        ...
        """
        from tests.factories import AuthorizedPropertyFactory

        # Seed the tenant as auto-approve (human_review_required=False). The
        # in-process transports never hit the tenant approval gate because this
        # env's mocked adapter sets manual_approval_operations=[] — but the live
        # e2e_rest server has no patches and reads the REAL tenant row, where
        # the column defaults to True, routing every create to the submitted
        # (manual-approval) envelope instead of completing it. Seeding False
        # makes the live server's gate state match what every other transport
        # already grades (#1418 class; #1417/#1537 scenarios re-triaged after
        # the adcp-6.6 merge). ...
        tenant, principal = self.setup_default_data(human_review_required=False)
        ...
        self.setup_default_account()
        # Satisfy the create_media_buy setup-checklist "Authorized Properties"
        # gate. In-process transports skip it via the testing context, but the
        # live e2e_rest server enforces it (validate_setup_complete), so a
        # fully-set-up tenant needs at least one authorized property.
        AuthorizedPropertyFactory(tenant=tenant)
        product, pricing_option = self.setup_product_chain(tenant)
        return tenant, principal, product, pricing_option

    Every line is a factory write. The two comments are the whole discipline: the seeder pays the cost of *the strictest* transport (the live server, which has no patches), so the weaker ones are satisfied automatically. setup_product_chain (:132-168) is the pricing-option owner and is the same shape:

            pricing_option = None
        if with_pricing:
            pricing_option = PricingOptionFactory(
                product=product, pricing_model="cpm", currency=currency, is_fixed=True
            )
        return product, pricing_option

    Does not work anywhere but in-process — CreativeFormatsEnv.set_registry_formats, tests/harness/creative_formats.py:151-165

        @realize_e2e(_validate_registry_formats)
    def set_registry_formats(self, formats: list[Any]) -> None:
        """Configure mock registry to return these formats from list_all_formats.

        In-process: injects ``formats`` on the registry mock. E2E: validates the
        request is realizable against the live catalog (the live stack serves the
        full reference set by construction, so there is no per-scenario registry
        to write) — see :func:`_validate_registry_formats`.
        """
        from src.core.creative_agent_registry import FormatFetchResult

        self.mock["registry"].return_value.list_all_formats = AsyncMock(return_value=formats)
        self.mock["registry"].return_value.list_all_formats_with_errors = AsyncMock(
            return_value=FormatFetchResult(formats=list(formats), errors=[])
        )

    and its e2e branch, tests/harness/creative_formats.py:56-91:

    def _validate_registry_formats(env: Any, formats: list[Any]) -> None:
    """E2E realization of set_registry_formats: validate against the live catalog.
    ...
    """
    from src.core.format_cache import load_reference_formats

    if not formats:
        raise E2EUnsupportedSetup(
            "live stack always serves the agent catalog; an empty catalog cannot be realized over e2e"
        )

    reference_ids = {_format_identity_key(f) for f in load_reference_formats()}
    requested_ids = {_format_identity_key(f) for f in formats}
    missing = requested_ids - reference_ids
    if missing:
        named = sorted(f"{format_id} @ {agent_url}" for agent_url, format_id in missing)
        raise E2EUnsupportedSetup(
            f"requested formats not in the reference catalog: {named}. ..."
        )

    The precise difference. setup_media_buy_data writes a products row and a pricing_options row; both transports read those rows through the same production query. set_registry_formats writes an AsyncMock attribute; the live server's format read never touches Python objects in the test process. The production read the live server actually performs is src/core/creative_agent_registry.py:722-726:

            # In testing mode (ADCP_TESTING=true), serve the checked-in reference formats
        # to avoid external HTTP calls (and to match the e2e server by construction).
        if os.environ.get("ADCP_TESTING", "").lower() == "true":
            logger.info("list_all_formats: Using reference formats (ADCP_TESTING=true)")
            return FormatFetchResult(formats=_get_reference_formats(), errors=[])

    _get_reference_formats() reads a checked-in file (tests/fixtures/creative_formats/reference_formats.json, via src/core/format_cache.py:63 load_reference_formats(), @lru_cache(maxsize=1)). It short-circuits self._get_tenant_agents(tenant_id) — the DB read — entirely. There is no per-tenant, per-scenario surface, which is exactly why _validate_registry_formats can only validate rather than realize.

    The steps that drive it build formats as process-local Pydantic objects — tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_entities.py:85-93:

    @given("the creative agent registry has formats across multiple categories")
def given_registry_multi_categories(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """Registry has formats spanning multiple categories (display, video, etc.)."""
    ctx["registry_formats"] = [
        FormatFactory.build(name="banner", type=CATEGORY_MAP["display"]),
        FormatFactory.build(name="pre-roll", type=CATEGORY_MAP["video"]),
        FormatFactory.build(name="audio-spot", type=CATEGORY_MAP["audio"]),
    ]
    _sync_registry(ctx)

    FormatFactory.build() — not .create(). No row exists. _sync_registry is tests/bdd/steps/generic/_registry.py:17, one line: ctx["env"].set_registry_formats(ctx.get("registry_formats", [])).

    The counter-example that proves the rule is fixable — CapabilitiesEnv, tests/harness/capabilities.py:136-188

        def _realize_adapter_unavailable(self) -> None:
        """E2E realization: persist the 'unavailable' fault-injection flag."""
        from tests.factories.core import set_adapter_test_behavior

        set_adapter_test_behavior(self, self._tenant_id, unavailable=True)

    @realize_e2e(_realize_adapter_unavailable)
    def make_adapter_unavailable(self) -> None:
        """Adapter factory raises — production degrades to default channels.

        In-process: the adapter-class mock raises directly. E2E: persists
        test_behavior['unavailable']=True, read by get_adapter_class_for_tenant
        (src/core/helpers/adapter_helpers.py), which raises for mock-adapter
        tenants only.
        """
        self.mock["adapter"].side_effect = Exception("adapter unavailable (harness)")

    This started as an e2e_unsupported and was graduated by adding a production read (get_adapter_class_for_tenant honouring AdapterConfig.config_json["test_behavior"]). The ledger records it at tests/bdd/e2e_rest_known_failures.txt:155-160. That is the template for fixing formats.

    And CapabilitiesEnv.declare_capabilities (tests/harness/capabilities.py:117-136) is the cleanest statement of the rule in the codebase:

            No ``@realize_e2e``: this is a real tenant-config DB write, not a
        monkeypatch. ``configure_tenant_field`` (tests/harness/_base.py) updates
        both the in-memory tenant overrides (mock identity path) and the DB
        ``tenants`` row, which the real MCP/A2A/e2e auth chain reads back via
        ``config_loader.get_tenant_by_id`` ... Because it needs no test-only injection
        seam it declares no ``E2EUnsupportedSetup``, so the e2e escape-hatch pin
        (``EXPECTED_UNSUPPORTED_DECLARATIONS``) does not grow.

    3. The e2e escape hatches

    tests/unit/test_architecture_e2e_rest_escape_hatches.py names four routes (:1-31):

    > 1. the nodeid ledger (locked);

    > 2. an is_e2e_rest-gated xfail route in the BDD conftest's pytest_collection_modifyitems;

    > 3. an env-level E2EUnsupportedSetup declaration in tests/harness/;

    > 4. never parametrizing e2e_rest at all, in pytest_generate_tests — the quietest of the four, because a transport dropped at collection is exactly as ungraded as an xfail while showing no marker anywhere (#1802).

    Pinned inventory, and what each means for seeding:

    Route 1 — nodeid ledger. tests/bdd/e2e_rest_known_failures.txt, 14 live entries (:56-60, :97-98, :142-148). Only the 7 in the "parallel e2e_rest mock-injection artifacts" block (:123-148) are seeding divergences: set_adapter_response (UC-004) and set_registry_formats (UC-005) again.

    Route 2 — 19 pinned xfail conditions (EXPECTED_XFAIL_ROUTES, :80-116). Two are unconditional blankets — "is_e2e_rest" and "not is_e2e_rest" — and one is a whole-UC drop: "is_e2e_rest and any((t.startswith('T-UC-019') for t in marker_names))".

    Route 3 — 15 pinned E2EUnsupportedSetup declarations (EXPECTED_UNSUPPORTED_DECLARATIONS, :696-...). By file:

  • - tests/harness/creative_formats.py — _validate_registry_formats ×2 (static + <dynamic>) → 143 + 1 = 144 xfails
  • - tests/harness/creative_sync.py — configure_agent_served_creative, observe_effects_at_notification, committed_sync_effects → 10 + 1 + 3 = 14 xfails
  • - tests/harness/capabilities.py — make_adapter_channel_enumeration_fail, break_tenant_config_db, set_supported_versions, set_build_version, set_idempotency_posture → 1 + 3 + 5 + 10 = 19 xfails
  • - tests/harness/_mixins.py — set_adapter_error, assert_no_retry_schedule_entered, assert_circuit_breaker_failure_recorded → 2 xfails
  • - tests/harness/_base.py — inject_untyped_exception → 1 xfail

    • Three of these fifteen are not seeding at all — observe_effects_at_notification, committed_sync_effects, assert_no_retry_schedule_entered, assert_circuit_breaker_failure_recorded are *observation* hatches. Worth naming separately: an observation hatch cannot be fixed by a seeding surface.

    Route 4 — parametrize-time drops. One gate (EXPECTED_E2E_REST_PARAMETRIZE_GATES), one call site, seven exclusion points (EXPECTED_E2E_REST_EXCLUSION_POINTS, :565-579), one single-transport tag ({"a2a_untyped_ingest": "A2A"}).

    The unpinned fifth hatch

    51 step bodies reach into env.mock[...] directly, bypassing realize_e2e entirely. These are neither declared unsupported nor realized on e2e; they silently do nothing to the live server and the scenario grades whatever the live server happens to do. Sample:

    $ grep -rn "env.mock\[" tests/bdd/steps/ | wc -l
51

    Some pay the cost by hand — tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:2853-2870 runs *both* branches unconditionally:

        env = ctx["env"]
    mock_adapter = env.mock["adapter"].return_value
    ...
