    mock_adapter.update_media_buy.side_effect = error
    # Also write to DB so Docker adapter raises the same error.
    # Must also disable manual approval so the adapter is actually called
    # (manual approval short-circuits before calling adapter.create_media_buy).
    _sync_adapter_approval_to_db(ctx, manual_approval_required=False)
    _sync_adapter_error_to_db(

    That is realize_e2e hand-rolled at the step, in the one shape the DRY invariant treats as a defect. Others (tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc003_update_media_buy.py:2109-2159) poke the mock and write nothing.

    4. Recommendation

    What is and is not the problem

    Creative and pricing-option row seeding is already transport-independent — 22 PricingOptionFactory( and 27 CreativeFactory( call sites across tests/bdd/, every one of them writing through the env-bound session, every one of them correct on all four transports today. The sibling cluster's target (one owner instead of N copies) is a duplication fix, not a transport fix; do not let anyone claim it buys e2e coverage, because those sites already have it.

    The transport problem is a different set: format seeding, which has no row at all.

    The function that should exist, #1 — fix the 143

    Add a production read for a per-tenant format override, mirroring the AdapterConfig.test_behavior precedent exactly, then make set_registry_formats realize into it.

    Production side, src/core/creative_agent_registry.py:722-726 — the short-circuit gains one lookup before it answers:

    if os.environ.get("ADCP_TESTING", "").lower() == "true":
    override = get_reference_formats_override(tenant_id)   # NEW: reads CreativeAgent.config_json["test_formats"]
    return FormatFetchResult(formats=override or _get_reference_formats(), errors=[])

    placed beside get_adapter_class_for_tenant's existing test_behavior read in src/core/helpers/adapter_helpers.py, gated on ADCP_TESTING so it cannot fire in production. Harness side, tests/harness/creative_formats.py:

    def _persist_registry_formats(env: Any, formats: list[Any]) -> None:
    """E2E realization: persist the scenario's format set for this tenant."""
    from tests.factories.core import set_creative_agent_test_formats

    set_creative_agent_test_formats(env, env._tenant_id, formats=[f.model_dump(mode="json") for f in formats])

@realize_e2e(_persist_registry_formats)   # replaces @realize_e2e(_validate_registry_formats)
def set_registry_formats(self, formats: list[Any]) -> None:
    ...

    with set_creative_agent_test_formats(env, tenant_id, *, formats) added to tests/factories/core.py as the sibling of set_adapter_test_behavior (:268) — same upsert shape, same env._commit_factory_data() tail. The empty-catalog case (formats == []) becomes realizable too: an explicit empty list is a different value from an absent override.

    This removes 144 of the 180 escape-hatch xfails and shrinks EXPECTED_UNSUPPORTED_DECLARATIONS by two rows — which is the pin's own stated direction of travel.

    The function that should exist, #2 — give creatives a harness owner

    CreativeSyncEnv and CreativeListEnv have no creative seeder. setup_media_buy_data/setup_product_chain are on MediaBuyCreateEnv only, so a creative env cannot reuse the one seeder that already knows the e2e-vs-in-process gate differences. Promote to IntegrationEnv (tests/harness/_base.py:1522), beside setup_default_data and setup_default_account:

    def setup_creative(self, *, creative_id=None, principal=None, fmt="display_300x250",
                   status="approved", assets=None, **overrides) -> Any:
    """Get-or-create one creative owned by this env's principal."""
    tenant, default_principal = self.setup_default_data()
    return get_or_create(self, Creative, {...}, lambda: CreativeFactory(...))

    built on tests/factories/core.py:29 get_or_create — the idempotent path the shared e2e DB needs — rather than the bare factory. That is the single call tests/bdd/test_uc018_list_creatives.py:110 _seed_creative, tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:2038 _create_approved_creative, and the 25 other sites collapse onto. tests/bdd/test_uc018_list_creatives.py:159 _get_or_create_tenant_and_principal is a module-local re-implementation of setup_default_data and disappears with it.

    Move setup_product_chain up the same way, so PricingOptionFactory(product=..., pricing_model="cpm", currency=..., is_fixed=...) — repeated 22 times with only currency, is_fixed and price_guidance substituted — becomes env.setup_pricing_option(currency="EUR", is_fixed=False, price_guidance={"floor": 1.0}).

    Smallest change that proves the whole thing on all four transports

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:611-636 (given_packages_same_currency) is the cheapest end-to-end proof, because it seeds a pricing option AND is already graded on e2e_rest:

    1. Add setup_pricing_option to IntegrationEnv.

    2. Rewrite that one step to call it.

    3. Run the module serial on all four transports; the e2e_rest leg must stay green.

    That is a two-file diff that demonstrates the seeding surface without touching production. Then #1 (formats) is the change that actually moves the 143, and it is the one that needs a production read — cite the AdapterConfig.test_behavior precedent and the ledger's own record of it (tests/bdd/e2e_rest_known_failures.txt:155-160) when arguing for it, because "add a production branch for a test's benefit" is exactly the objection make_adapter_channel_enumeration_fail's declaration was written to refuse, and the difference is that a *per-tenant catalog* is a real seller capability while *making channel enumeration raise* is not.

    One thing the owner asked that is not a seeding problem

    "the same construction works for … e2e REST, MCP and A2A." McpE2EDispatcher and A2AE2EDispatcher exist and are registered in DISPATCHERS (tests/harness/dispatchers.py:224,267,313-320), but BDD never parametrizes them — tests/bdd/conftest.py:4461-4462 builds [Transport.A2A, Transport.MCP, Transport.REST] and _parametrize_ctx (:4172-4192) appends exactly one e2e member. The run confirms: 2843 e2e_rest, zero e2e_mcp, zero e2e_a2a. Both dispatchers refuse without an explicit tool_name= because no env exposes an MCP tool attribute the way REST_ENDPOINT is exposed — except BaseTestEnv now *does* declare MCP_TOOL and A2A_SKILL (tests/harness/_base.py:340-341), so McpE2EDispatcher's stated blocker ("no env exposes an MCP equivalent") is stale. Wiring e2e_mcp/e2e_a2a is a dispatch change, independent of seeding, and would triple the e2e grading surface that the seeding fix unlocks. Worth its own ticket.

    Deliberate malformation in the BDD seeding surfaceagent 2 of 2

    Deliberate malformation in the BDD seeding surface

    Worktree: . (branch exec/itsii-1-drop-impl). Read-only; nothing modified.

    0. Headline

    You cannot tell a deliberate malformation from an accidental one at the seeding site. Every mechanism that carries a deliberately-wrong payload is the same mechanism that carries a correct one; the only thing distinguishing them is English — a docstring, a step-text phrase, or a partition label — plus, in two places, a Python comment. No sentinel, no distinct call, no decorator, no tag reaches the Python that builds the creative or the package.

    Two further facts change the shape of the decision:

  • - The one machine-readable declaration of "this seed must be refused" — the Examples table's outcome column, read by then_outcome — lives in the .feature file. The Given that builds the payload never sees it.
  • - The one place a Given records deliberateness in Python, ctx["expected_missing_pricing_options"], is write-only. It has two writers and zero readers.

    • And the grading fact (from test-results/innet_070926_0757/bdd_inprocess.json): every deliberately-wrong creative and pricing-option seeding site named below is currently unexecuted. See §5.

    1. Mechanisms by which a test sends a deliberately-invalid payload

    Five exist. Counts are over tests/bdd/steps/ unless stated.

    M-a. _call_raw — the named negative-path dispatch (23 call sites, all in one file)

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/when_request.py:83

    def _call_raw(ctx: dict, **payload: Any) -> None:
    """Dispatch the LITERAL payload — no ``ListCreativeFormatsRequest`` in the way.

    THE NEGATIVE-PATH DISPATCH. Use this whenever the scenario expects the
    request to be REJECTED, so the SELLER performs the validation and the buyer
    receives a real wire envelope.

    Real use (when_request.py:193):

    @when("the Buyer Agent sends a list_creative_formats request with invalid dimension filters")
def when_send_request_invalid_dimensions(ctx: dict) -> None:
    # min_width=-1 violates the schema's Ge(0) — the SELLER must say so.
    _call_raw(ctx, min_width=-1)

    But the name does not mean "deliberately invalid." Of the 23 sites, several are plain positive-path dispatch that happens to be flat, e.g. when_request.py:214:

    def when_request_asset_types_and_name_search(ctx: dict, asset_types: str, name_search: str) -> None:
    parsed_assets = json.loads(asset_types)
    _call_raw(ctx, asset_types=parsed_assets, name_search=name_search)

    The file itself acknowledges the split at when_request.py:479 and :491:

        # ── Positive branches: typed request via _call (see _call_raw's docstring
    # for why this module deliberately uses both dispatch paths).
...
    # ── Negative branches: the payload goes out RAW so the seller rejects it and
    # the buyer receives a real envelope. Each dict below is deliberately NOT a
    # FormatId — constructing one would raise here and production would never run.
    elif partition == "empty_array":
        _call_raw(ctx, **{field: []})
    elif partition == "invalid_format_id_missing_agent_url":
        _call_raw(ctx, **{field: [{"id": "some-id"}]})
    elif partition == "invalid_format_id_missing_id":
        _call_raw(ctx, **{field: [{"agent_url": DEFAULT_AGENT_URL}]})

    This block is the *only* place in the tree where the deliberateness is stated as an in-code comment adjacent to the perturbed literals.

    M-b. dispatch_request(ctx, **kwargs) — the raw seam that is also the default (111 call sites, 12 files)

    tests/bdd/steps/generic/_dispatch.py:176

    def dispatch_request(ctx: dict, *, identity: Any = NO_IDENTITY_OVERRIDE, **kwargs: Any) -> None:
    """Dispatch a request through ctx['transport'] via ``env.call_via``.

    Distribution: uc004_delivery.py 37, uc011_accounts.py 32, egress_ssrf.py 8, uc002_create_media_buy.py 8, uc006_sync_creatives.py 8, remainder ≤4 each.

    This is the seam creatives actually go through, valid or not. tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:117:

    def when_sync_creative(ctx: dict) -> None:
    ...
    account_ref = ctx.get("account_ref")
    creatives = ctx.get("creatives", [])
    kwargs: dict = {"account": account_ref, "creatives": creatives}

    One When step consumes ctx["creatives"] for all 42 creative dicts in that file. It cannot know which of them is meant to be refused.

    M-c. Factory.payload() vs Factory.build() — the designed fork (5 sites in BDD, 30 tree-wide)

    tests/factories/request.py:135

        def payload(cls, **overrides: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """The conformant baseline as a wire dict, with *overrides* applied verbatim.

        Overrides land AFTER ``model_dump``, so they may carry values the DTO
        would reject — that is the point of a negative-path perturbation. Pass
        ``OMIT`` to delete a key.
        """

    This is the only mechanism in the tree where the *fork itself* is the design: build() = DTO validation runs, payload() = it does not. Stated at tests/factories/webhook.py:80:

    # ``payload(**overrides)`` applies its overrides AFTER ``model_dump``, so it can
# carry a value the model itself would refuse; ``OMIT`` deletes a key outright.
# Verified against the pinned schemas, every one of these produces a document the
# schema rejects::
#
#     R = ReportingWebhookRequestFactory
#     R.payload(reporting_frequency="fortnightly")  # not one of hourly/daily/monthly
#     R.payload(reporting_frequency=OMIT)           # required property missing

    It is essentially unused where the duplication is. The five BDD uses are all webhook/push-config (given_config.py:321, uc004_delivery.py:1089 and :3423, egress_ssrf.py:232 and :262). Zero creative seeds and zero pricing-option seeds go through it.

    M-d. OMIT sentinels — three of them, in two modules, under two names

    tests/factories/request.py:83

    class _Omit:
    """Sentinel: ``payload(field=OMIT)`` deletes the key instead of setting it.

    A negative-path test that grades a MISSING required field cannot express
    itself with ``None`` — ``None`` is a value, and the baseline dump already
    drops null fields, so ``payload(account=None)`` would read as "leave the
    default in place" rather than "send a request without an account".
    """

    tests/harness/media_buy_create.py:28

    # Sentinel for missing-key tests: pass idempotency_key=OMIT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY to send a
# request with NO key (the schema rejects it as "Field required" — AdCP 3.0.1).
OMIT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY: Any = object()

# The sibling sentinel for ``account``, which create-media-buy-request.json also lists in
# /required. A scenario that means to send NO account cannot signal that by omitting the
# kwarg -- omission is what every scenario that simply does not care about accounts also
# looks like, and those get the seeded default. Same shape, same reason, as the key above.
OMIT_ACCOUNT: Any = object()

    This is the best existing answer to the owner's question, and the only mechanism in the tree where "deliberately absent" is distinguishable *by construction* from "I don't care": the harness reads the sentinel identity at media_buy_create.py:363, and three-way-splits absent / None / OMIT_*. Exactly one BDD step uses it — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc002_create_media_buy.py:1487:

    @given("a create_media_buy request with the idempotency_key field omitted")
def given_request_idempotency_key_omitted(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """Build a create_media_buy request that carries NO idempotency_key on the wire.

    Uses the harness OMIT sentinel: the request assembler keeps it, and
    MediaBuyCreateEnv._ensure_idempotency_key pops it so the constructed
    CreateMediaBuyRequest is missing the REQUIRED field — production rejects it

    Two names for one concept (OMIT, OMIT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY/OMIT_ACCOUNT) in two packages, neither importable from the other's usual context.

    M-e. make_legacy_asset_dict — a named wrong-shape builder for creatives (17 uses, all unit tests, zero BDD)

    tests/factories/creative_asset.py:18

    def make_legacy_asset_dict(asset_id: str, **fields: object) -> dict:
    """Build a LEGACY (AdCP v1) single-dict asset entry: ``{asset_id: {**fields}}``.
    ...
    Use this ONLY for legacy-input / negative tests that deliberately exercise the
    old shape. New or valid creative assets must use the AssetSpec mechanism
    (``image_spec``/``text_spec``/``video_spec``/``url_spec``/``asset_spec`` +
    ``build_assets``). Centralising the legacy shape here keeps it out of inline
    test dicts — see #1391.

    This is the correct model for creatives specifically, and it is used only in tests/unit/test_validate_creative_assets.py and tests/unit/test_creative_conversion_assets.py. No BDD step imports it. The BDD steps hand-roll the same legacy shape inline instead (see §3, item C1).

    M-f. The Examples-table outcome column — machine-readable, but in the wrong file

    tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-026-package-media-buy.feature:821

      @T-UC-026-partition-pricing-option @partition @pricing_option_id
  Scenario Outline: Pricing option partition validation -- <partition>
    Given a create_media_buy request with pricing_option_id per <partition>
    When the Buyer Agent sends the create_media_buy request
    Then the response is compliant with the create_media_buy spec
    And the outcome should be <outcome>

    Examples: Valid partitions
      | partition              | outcome                                    |
      | valid_pricing_option   | success with pricing resolved               |
      | valid_with_max_bid     | success with ceiling semantics              |

    Examples: Invalid partitions
      | partition                    | outcome                                    |
      | pricing_option_not_found     | error "INVALID_REQUEST" with suggestion    |
      | pricing_option_wrong_product | error "INVALID_REQUEST" with suggestion    |

    The column is live — consumed by then_outcome at tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc026_package_media_buy.py:1912, which routes to result.assert_wire_error(expected_code, require_suggestion=require_suggestion). But it reaches the Then, never the Given. given_partition_pricing_option receives only the string "pricing_option_not_found".

    2. Can a reader — or an automated pass — tell, at the seeding site?

    No. Concretely, in one file, these two sit 23 lines apart and are structurally identical:

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:2644 — shape is correct, deliberate perturbation is the empty name:

    @given("a creative with format_id but an empty name")
def given_creative_format_id_empty_name(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """Set up a creative with a valid format_id but empty name — boundary case."""
    env = ctx["env"]
    ensure_tenant_principal(ctx, env)
    creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": "creative-fmt-empty-name-001",
        "name": "",
        "format_id": {"id": "display_300x250", "agent_url": env.DEFAULT_AGENT_URL},
        "assets": build_assets(image_spec("image")),
    }
    ctx.setdefault("creatives", []).append(creative_payload)

    tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:2659 — assets is deliberately the wrong type:

    @given("a creative with invalid schema structure")
def given_creative_invalid_schema(ctx: dict) -> None:
    """Set up a creative payload with invalid schema — triggers CREATIVE_VALIDATION_FAILED.

    Has a format_id (to avoid CREATIVE_FORMAT_REQUIRED) but provides assets
    in the wrong structure (string instead of dict) to trigger schema validation.
    """
    env = ctx["env"]
    ensure_tenant_principal(ctx, env)
    creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": "creative-invalid-schema-001",
        "name": "Invalid Schema Creative",
        "format_id": {"id": "display_300x250", "agent_url": env.DEFAULT_AGENT_URL},
        "assets": "not-a-valid-assets-structure",
    }
    ctx.setdefault("creatives", []).append(creative_payload)

    Same dict literal, same four keys, same ctx.setdefault("creatives", []).append(...), same downstream dispatch_request. The only discriminator is the docstring and the @given text.

    Now the counter-example that makes a naive pass dangerous — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py:5236:

    def given_generative_creative_with_asset_role(ctx: dict, role: str, content: str) -> None:
    """Set up a generative creative with a specific asset role containing prompt text.

    INV-2: prompt found in assets (message/brief/prompt role) -> that text used as build prompt.
    """
    ...
    creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": "creative-gen-inv2-001",
        "name": "Generative Prompt From Assets",
        "format_id": fmt,
        "assets": {
            role: {"content": content},
        },
    }

    {role: {"content": content}} is the legacy v1 asset shape with no asset_type discriminator — the exact shape the same file forbids 1857 lines earlier, at uc006_sync_creatives.py:3377:

                # Built through the canonical AssetSpec mechanism, never hand-rolled:
            # 3.1 assets are a discriminated union keyed by role, and a hand-built
            # dict misses the asset_type discriminator (GH #1391).
            "assets": build_assets(text_spec("prompt", content="a headline about running shoes")),

    And its scenario expects success ("that text used as build prompt"), not a refusal. So in the same file, on the same field, one hand-rolled assets literal is a deliberate refusal-seed and one is an accidental shape bug awaiting a fix, and nothing at either site says which.

    The partial signals, and why none of them is usable

    Candidate signalWhereWhy it fails
    _call_raw namewhen_request.pyUsed for positive dispatch too (:207, :216, :246, :284)
    dispatch_request111 sitesThe default seam for *every* BDD dispatch
    Step-function name (given_creative_invalid_schema)uc006English only; given_creative_output_format_ids_present is also deliberate and carries no signal
    Valid pricing ids go through _resolve_pricing_id, invalid ones are literalsuc026_resolve_pricing_id docstring at :38: *"Falls through to the raw label if no mapping exists"* — an accidentally unmapped label produces exactly the same literal as a deliberate one
    ctx["expected_missing_pricing_options"]uc026:592, :668Write-only. grep over all of tests/ returns those two lines and nothing else
    Examples outcome column.featureRead by the Then, invisible to the Given

    3. The do-not-touch list: creative and pricing-option seeds that are deliberately wrong

    56 hand-built creative dicts exist across tests/bdd/steps/ (42 in uc006_sync_creatives.py, 6 in uc003_update_media_buy.py, 4 in uc006_storyboard_creative_sync.py, 2 in uc026_package_media_buy.py, 1 each in given_media_buy.py and uc003_ext_error_scenarios.py). The following are the ones a shape fix must leave alone. All paths under .

    Creatives — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc006_sync_creatives.py

    C1 — assets is the wrong TYPE, on purpose. :2659, quoted in §2. Feature obligation, tests/bdd/features/BR-UC-006-sync-creatives.feature:214:

      Scenario: Creative validation failed — schema violation
    And a creative with invalid schema structure
    ...
    And the error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST"

    C2 — format_id is explicitly None. :2630:

    @given(parsers.parse('a creative with name "{name}" but no format_id'))
def given_creative_with_name_no_format(ctx: dict, name: str) -> None:
    """Set up a creative payload with a name but no format_id — triggers CREATIVE_FORMAT_REQUIRED."""
    ...
    creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": f"creative-no-fmt-{name.lower().replace(' ', '-')}-001",
        "name": name,
        "format_id": None,
        "assets": build_assets(image_spec("image")),
    }

    Obligation at BR-UC-006-sync-creatives.feature:250 (error code should be "INVALID_REQUEST") and again as a boundary row at :1163: | missing format_id (null) | a creative with name "Banner" but no format_id | the error should include "suggestion" field |.

    C3 — format_id key ABSENT (a different wrongness from C2). :4809:

        creative_payload = {
        "creative_id": "creative-no-fmt-001",
        "name": "Creative Without Format",
        "assets": build_assets(image_spec("image")),
    }
    ctx.setdefault("creatives", []).append(creative_payload)
    ctx["creative_no_format"] = True

    C4 — assets key ABSENT, with the only "intentionally" comment in the file. :2049:

def given_creative_with_known_format_no_media_url(ctx: dict) -> None:

    To trigger that branch reliably we omit any url/asset entirely — assets are

        # Intentionally no "assets" and no "url" / "media_url" — triggers the

    C5 — assets key ABSENT, deliberate, NO comment marker. :4945 and :4965:

    @given("a creative with output_format_ids present")

    """Set up a creative with a generative format (output_format_ids populated).

    Delegates to the existing generative format setup but does NOT add

    """
    ...

        "creative_id": "creative-generative-part-001",
        "name": "Generative Partition Creative",
        "format_id": fmt,
    }

    Adding assets here would supply a prompt source and silently change which INV branch production takes. Same for given_generative_creative_exists_with_content at :5306 (dict at :5348).

    C6 — assets: {} deliberately empty, three sites. :5091 (INV-4 name-fallback), :5262 (INV-3 inputs[0].context_description fallback), :5284 (INV-4). Filling these breaks the fallback chain each grades.

    C7 — name: "" deliberately empty, four sites. :2610 (given_creative_with_empty_name, obligation at BR-UC-006-sync-creatives.feature:227), :2644 (given_creative_format_id_empty_name, boundary row at :1167), :4836 (given_creative_empty_name_known_format), and :4318 given_two_creatives_one_valid_one_empty_name where the second dict at :4330 is the deliberate half of a valid/invalid pair. Plus :7244 given_creatives_all_fail, dict at :7259:

                {
                "creative_id": f"creative-fail-{i:03d}",
                "name": "",  # empty name → per-creative validation failure
                "format_id": {"id": format_id, "agent_url": agent_url},
                "assets": assets,

    C8 — semantically-wrong format_id, shape correct. :1851 (given_creative_with_unknown_format), :1881 (given_creative_with_unreachable_agent), :6136 (given_creative_with_invalid_format_id). Shape-correct, so a shape pass will not touch them — but a "resolve every format_id against the registry" pass would.

    Pricing options — tests/bdd/steps/domain/uc026_package_media_buy.py

    P1 — the partition builder, valid and invalid branches indistinguishable. :1077:

    @given(parsers.re(r"a create_media_buy request with pricing_option_id per (?!boundary )(?P<partition>.+)"))
def given_partition_pricing_option(ctx: dict, partition: str) -> None:
    """Build create request per partition for pricing_option_id validation."""
    kwargs = _ensure_request_defaults(ctx)
    pkg = _build_default_package(ctx)
    partition = partition.strip()
    if partition == "valid_pricing_option":
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = _resolve_pricing_id(ctx, "cpm-standard")
    elif partition == "valid_with_max_bid":
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = _resolve_pricing_id(ctx, "cpm-auction")
    elif partition == "pricing_option_not_found":
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = "nonexistent-option"
    elif partition == "pricing_option_wrong_product":
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = "other-product-option"
    else:
        raise ValueError(f"Unknown pricing_option_id partition: {partition}")
    kwargs["packages"] = [pkg]

    P2 — the boundary builder, same. :1096, including an empty-string seed:

        elif "not in product" in bp:
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = "nonexistent-option"
    elif "different product" in bp:
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = "other-product-option"
    elif "empty string" in bp:
        pkg["pricing_option_id"] = ""

    P3 — deliberate DELETION of the required field. :938 and :961:

        elif partition == "missing_pricing_option_id":
        del pkg["pricing_option_id"]
...
    elif "pricing_option_id missing" in bp:
        del pkg["pricing_option_id"]

    del on a required key is a strong signal in isolation, but given_boundary_format_ids (:1053) uses pkg.pop("format_ids", None) for the *valid* "omitted" case in the same file, so "removal ⇒ deliberate refusal" does not hold.

    P4 — the only Given that both declares and verifies its wrongness. :573 and :650:

    @given(parsers.parse('the pricing_option_id "{option}" is not in product "{product_id}" pricing_options'))
def given_pricing_not_in_product(ctx: dict, option: str, product_id: str) -> None:
    ...
    resolved = _resolve_pricing_id(ctx, option)
    assert resolved not in actual_ids and option not in actual_ids, (
        f"Pricing option '{option}' (resolved: '{resolved}') should NOT be in "
        f"product '{product_id}' but found in {actual_ids}"
    )
    ctx.setdefault("expected_missing_pricing_options", []).append(option)

    The assertion is real and correct. The ctx write on the last line is read by nothing.

    P5 — the same intent with no verification. tests/bdd/steps/generic/given_media_buy.py:916:

    @given(parsers.parse('a package references pricing_option_id "{po_id}" not found on the product'))
@given(parsers.parse('a package references pricing_option_id "{po_id}" not found on the product'))
def given_nonexistent_pricing_option(ctx: dict, po_id: str) -> None:
    """Override first package pricing_option_id to a non-existent value."""
    ...
    kwargs["packages"][0]["pricing_option_id"] = po_id

    (The duplicated decorator on :916/:917 is a separate, unrelated defect.)

    4. Recommendation: make it distinguishable by construction

    The tree already contains the right mechanism twice, in the wrong places. Neither needs inventing.

    (a) Perturb a baseline; never hand-type a wrong document. Factory.payload(**overrides) (tests/factories/request.py:135) already means "wire dict, DTO validation deliberately skipped", and build() means "DTO validation runs". A seed written as one perturbation of a conformant baseline names its own wrongness in the diff:

    SyncCreativesRequestFactory.payload(creatives=[creative(assets="not-a-valid-assets-structure")])

    An automated shape fix can then rewrite the *baseline* and leave every override alone, mechanically. The exemplar already in the tree is tests/integration/test_webhook_carrier_is_a_gated_value.py:60-83, where each malformed document is a named module constant built as baseline-plus-one-perturbation, with the violated pin clause in the comment:

    #: Two schemes. The pinned schema allows at most one, so this is a document the
#: spec forbids -- both names are real AuthenticationScheme members, which is what
#: makes the pair the violation rather than either value.
TWO_SCHEME_WEBHOOK = ReportingWebhookRequestFactory.payload(
    url="https://buyer.example.com/webhook",
    authentication={"schemes": ["Bearer", "HMAC-SHA256"], "credentials": SECRET},
)

    (b) For an absent required field, use a sentinel — one sentinel. OMIT_ACCOUNT's own comment (tests/harness/media_buy_create.py:37) states the argument exactly right: *"A scenario that means to send NO account cannot signal that by omitting the kwarg -- omission is what every scenario that simply does not care about accounts also looks like."* That three-way split (absent / None / OMIT) is what C2, C3, C4, C5 and P3 all need and none of them has. The three sentinels (OMIT in tests/factories/request.py:96, OMIT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY and OMIT_ACCOUNT in tests/harness/media_buy_create.py:35,41) should be one object under one name, importable from tests.factories.

    (c) Route wrong creative assets through a named builder. make_legacy_asset_dict (tests/factories/creative_asset.py:18) already exists and already says *"Use this ONLY for legacy-input / negative tests that deliberately exercise the old shape."* C1 and the accidental {role: {"content": content}} at uc006_sync_creatives.py:5245 become trivially separable the moment one of them calls it and the other calls build_assets.

    (d) Widen the existing guard, with an explicit opt-in. tests/unit/test_architecture_bdd_no_dict_registry.py is exactly this guard for a different ctx key:

    > """Guard: BDD Given steps must not store raw dicts in ctx["registry_formats"]."""

    It scans two files (_GIVEN_FILES at :23) and has no allowlist and no escape hatch. Extended to ctx["creatives"] and to kwargs["packages"] as-is, it would fail every one of C1–C8 and P1–P3 — which is why the sentinel/factory opt-in in (a)–(c) has to land first: the guard's pass condition becomes "went through a named builder", and a deliberate malformation passes it by naming itself.

    (e) The outcome column should be visible to the Given. Cheapest version: the conftest that binds a Scenario Outline already has the Examples row; publishing the expected outcome into ctx before the Givens run would let a seeding helper assert its own intent (assert ctx["expects_refusal"] next to a perturbation), and would give a future guard something to grade. This is speculative — I did not trace whether the pytest-bdd binding makes the row available at Given time.

    5. Grounding: none of these sites currently executes

    From test-results/innet_070926_0757/bdd_inprocess.json (8195 tests):

    ModuleTotalOutcomesxfailed at setup
    test_uc026_package_media_buy728{'xfailed': 728}728
    test_uc006_sync_creatives704{'xfailed': 632, 'passed': 72}617
    test_uc002_create_media_buy1376{'xfailed': 1227, 'passed': 142, 'skipped': 3, 'failed': 4}1216
    test_uc003_update_media_buy1362{'xfailed': 1203, 'passed': 154, 'failed': 5}1151
    test_uc018_list_creatives501{'xfailed': 465, 'passed': 36}438

    Setup-phase xfail reasons, verbatim from the report:

    _pytest.outcomes.XFailed: No harness wired for UC-026 (markers: ['T-UC-026-partition-pricing-option',
  'analysis-2026-03-10', 'media_buy', 'parametrize', 'partition', 'pricing_option_id', 'schema-v3.1', 'xfail'])

    _pytest.outcomes.XFailed: UC-006 harness not yet wired for non-account scenarios

    Consequences:

  • - Every pricing-option seed in §3 (P1–P5) is dead. All 728 UC-026 tests abort in setup; given_partition_pricing_option and given_boundary_pricing_option never run. The 30 partition/boundary rows, the 3 inv2_violated and the 3 pricing_option_not_found_in_product scenarios are all setup-xfails.
  • - Every creative seed in §3 (C1–C8) is dead. The 72 passing uc006_sync_creatives tests are exclusively account-resolution, idempotency, bulk sync, blocked-webhook and INV-1/2/3 scenarios — all of which go through the *valid* given_creative_with_format (:127). test_creative_validation_failed__schema_violation, test_creative_name_empty, test_creative_format_required__missing_format_id and all 12 test_generative_build_boundary rows are xfailed in setup.

    • So today, a naive shape-normalising pass over these sites would go completely undetected — nothing turns red, in either direction. The protection against destroying a deliberate malformation is entirely the reading, which is the case for doing (a)–(d) before the shape fix rather than after.
